149: 0(orig). FOUNDATIONAL CORRECTION: THIS FAMILY HAS EXACTLY ONE INTENTIONAL

153:    (READ THIS SECTION FIRST, BEFORE ANY OTHER)
154: ========================================================================
155: DIRECT CORRECTION (2026-07-18, in response to an agent wrongly
156: rationalizing a real architectural mistake as an intentional design
157: choice - do not repeat this reasoning error): an earlier pass in this
158: file (sec. 7/16/16.3/17 below) explained the "interact + module"
159: pattern's own persistent `prisc+x` process - reached via a relay file
160: (`interact_relay.txt`), running its OWN independent poll loop (a plain
161: `sleep N; j loop` inside `main_loop_chtpm.pal`, decoupled from
162: `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s own separate poll loop) - as deliberately built
163: for "continuous, real-time" needs (mutaclsym's own hero movement). THIS
164: WAS WRONG. Direct correction: "mutaclysm isn't real time nor is the
165: deviation deliberate. it should always be change based. that was not
166: intentional never assume we meant to deviate from classic chtpm
167: standards other than 2 use more pal. thats where all problems start."
168: 
169: REAL/CLASSIC CHTPM'S OWN ARCHITECTURE (confirmed via direct code read,
170: `pieces/chtpm/plugins/chtpm_parser.c`, and the TPMOS Bible's own
171: "Standalone Operational Architecture" section): ops are invoked
172: SYNCHRONOUSLY, per keypress - `fork()`/`exec()`/`waitpid()`, inside the
173: SAME `process_key()` call that will go on to render, BEFORE that
174: render happens. There is no second, independently-polling process for
175: this at all. State changes and rendering are strictly, causally
176: ordered: key -> op runs and finishes -> render. A plain marker file
177: ("did the content change") is sufficient and reliable in that design
178: PRECISELY BECAUSE there is no second process racing against the first
179: one's own writes - the thing this family calls "change-based," not
180: "poll-based."
181: 
182: THIS FAMILY'S ONLY INTENTIONAL DEVIATION FROM THAT (see sec. 10's own
183: PAL-VS-C-ARCHITECTURE.txt): op LOGIC should increasingly run as real
184: PAL/prisc+x bytecode instead of hand-written C - a change of
185: EXECUTION VEHICLE for an op's own internal logic, NOT a change to WHEN
186: or HOW that op gets invoked. A pal-native op is still meant to be
187: invoked SYNCHRONOUSLY, per keypress, exactly like a real TPMOS C op -
188: `system/prisc+x pal/some_op.pal` as a ONE-SHOT process (fork/exec/wait,
189: same as any other op binary), not as a PERSISTENT process that outlives
190: a single keypress and polls a relay file on its own independent timer.
191: 
192: WHERE THE UNINTENTIONAL DRIFT ACTUALLY CAME FROM: the "interact +
193: module" pattern (sec. 7) genuinely DOES need something that stays alive
194: between keypresses - but only because mutaclsym's own `pal/game_loop.pal`
195: (now `main_loop_chtpm.pal`) was modeled as a copy of its own regular,
196: already-existing `main_loop.pal` - which is itself a real, persistent,
197: always-running VM loop, because THAT'S how mutaclsym's regular (non-chtpm)
198: "run" mode was ALWAYS structured (`system/prisc+x pal/main_loop.pal`,
199: one long-lived process for the whole session, reading history.txt in a
200: loop). Reusing that SAME persistent-process shape for the CHTPM-mode
201: variant was a copy-paste of convenience, not a deliberate decision that
202: mutaclsym (or ANY project in this family) genuinely needs a background
203: process ticking independently of user input. Nothing in this family is
204: real-time; every op should be triggered BY a keypress and FINISH before
205: the next render, exactly like real chtpm's own synchronous model.
206: 
207: THE ACTUAL BUGS THIS DRIFT CAUSED (all documented in more detail in
208: their own sections below, but ALL trace back to this one root cause -
209: a persistent module process racing chtpm_parser_pal's own render cycle
210: via two independently-timed poll loops instead of one synchronous
211: call-then-render sequence):
212:   - sec. 16.3 / this same day: a project's own compose op writing
213:     directly to `current_frame.txt` (to paper over the lag a
214:     persistent, asynchronous module introduces) clobbers chtpm's own
215:     chrome/`${piece_methods}` output for any project where the module
216:     runs on EVERY interaction, not just during a continuously-engaged
217:     INTERACT mode.
218:   - sec. 17: `system/renderer.c`/`chtpm_rgb_render.c` only watching one
219:     marker misses a module-driven recompose entirely, because the
220:     module's own update and chtpm's own reaction to it are two
221:     separate, asynchronously-timed events instead of one atomic
222:     sequence.
223:   - The user-reported "press Enter twice" / "Back doesn't navigate"
224:     symptoms - all instances of the SAME race: the module hasn't
225:     finished (or chtpm hasn't noticed it finished) by the time a render
226:     happens, because there was never a guarantee the two would be
227:     synchronized in the first place.
228: 
229: THE STANDING RULE, going forward: do not "fix" a race between chtpm's
230: own render cycle and a project's own module/op by tuning sleep
231: intervals, adding settle-loops, or dual-writing outputs to route around
232: the asynchrony (all of sec. 16.3/17's own fixes are exactly this kind
233: of patch, and are known to be incomplete/leaky, per this section).
234: Those are stopgaps on top of an architecture that should not exist in
235: this shape at all. For any NEW project (or when revisiting an existing
236: one), ask FIRST: does this project's own logic need to keep running
237: BETWEEN keypresses (an actual real-time tick - monster AI, a clock,
238: something with no key involved at all)? If NOT (true of every current
239: `${piece_methods}`-driven pure-menu project - wsr-pal, egg-pals), its
240: op(s) should be invoked SYNCHRONOUSLY per keypress (via chtpm's own
241: `OP:`/`SET_` command-dispatch conventions, matching real TPMOS's own
242: op-invocation model exactly - a one-shot process, not a persistent one
243: reached through a relay file), not modeled on mutaclsym's own
244: persistent-process shape just because that shape already existed
245: somewhere else in the family. A persistent module process is ONLY
246: justified for a project with a genuine, continuous, keypress-independent
247: tick - verify this explicitly, don't assume it by copying the pattern.
248: 
249: 0.1 SUPERSEDED (2026-07-19, see 0-CORRECTED above) - kept verbatim as
250:     history. `run_module_synchronous()` is reverted/unused as of
251:     2026-07-19; do not reintroduce it.
252: 0.1(orig) IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-18): mutaclsym AND zoo_0000's OWN INTERACT MODE
253:     IS NOW SYNCHRONOUS - `run_module_synchronous()`, THE FIX FOR THIS
254:     SECTION'S OWN ROOT CAUSE
255: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
256: Confirmed mutaclsym IS a turn-based roguelike, not real-time - monster
257: ticking happens on the SAME turn as player movement (direct
258: clarification: "its a rougelike. so monsters only move when player has
259: moved... but there should be room for there 2 be free monster movement
260: based on clock tick instead of player key tick. get it? ... but were
261: using move tick for now"). This means the ENTIRE per-key sequence
262: (`move_player`/`choice`/`end_turn`/`tick_monsters`/`compose_frame`) is a
263: single, atomic, keypress-driven unit today - no split between
264: "synchronous movement" and "separately-continuous ticking" was needed. A
265: genuinely independent clock-tick mode for monsters remains a real,
266: intentionally-deferred future option - NOT built now, and nothing here
267: blocks adding it later as an actual separate mechanism when needed.
268: 
269: THE FIX (`shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c`):
270: - `launch_module()`'s own guard changed from `strcmp(launch_str,
271:   current_module_path) == 0 && current_module_pid > 0` to just
272:   `strcmp(...) == 0 && current_module_path[0] != '\0'` - purely
273:   path-based now, not tied to whether the child is still alive. A
274:   pal-native module is a ONE-SHOT program now (see below) - this call
275:   only needs to fire once per unique module_path (populating the
276:   initial view before any key is pressed); checking `pid > 0` would let
277:   it keep re-firing the instant each one-shot child exits, since
278:   `parse_chtm()` calls this on every layout re-parse.
279: - New function `run_module_synchronous(int key)`, called from
280:   `process_key()`'s own INTERACT branch INSTEAD OF `inject_raw_key()`:
281:   writes `key` directly (truncate, not append) to the same target
282:   `inject_raw_key()` would use, then `fork()`+`execv()`+**blocking**
283:   `waitpid()` for `current_module_path` to run to completion before
284:   returning - the real fuzz-op_manager.c shape
285:   (`execl(...); waitpid(p, NULL, 0);`), not the old relay-to-a-
286:   persistent-process shape.
287: 
288: THE FIX (a project's own `main_loop_chtpm.pal`, mutaclsym AND
289: zoo_0000): converted from a persistent loop (`loop: ... sleep 30000; j
290: loop`) to a ONE-SHOT program - no loop, no sleep, exits naturally when
291: `pc` reaches the end (`prisc+x.c`'s own `while (pc < inst_count)`, no
292: halt instruction needed). Invoked TWO different ways, by design:
293:   1. Once, automatically, via `<module>${module_path}</module>` at
294:      layout-parse time (`launch_module()`, unchanged otherwise) -
295:      `interact_relay.txt` is empty at this point, `read_history` finds
296:      nothing, `no_key` is taken - this pass ONLY populates the initial
297:      `view.txt`/`current_frame.txt` before any key is ever pressed.
298:   2. Once per relayed key, via `run_module_synchronous()` - which
299:      already wrote the key to `interact_relay.txt` before forking, so
300:      THIS run's own `read_history` finds it immediately, dispatches the
301:      real action, and composes - by the time `run_module_synchronous()`
302:      returns, `view.txt`/`current_frame.txt` already reflect the new
303:      state.
304: 
305: LIVE-VERIFIED (direct key injection into `pieces/keyboard/history.txt`,
306: per sec. 9.3's own method - not assumed): both mutaclsym (hero moved
307: `pos_x` 13->14 on one relayed arrow key, exactly once, no double-
308: dispatch) and zoo_0000 (xlector visibly moved on the map, chrome/menu
309: intact) tested clean. One residual, EXPECTED artifact, harmless: a
310: project's own module still writes chrome-less content directly to
311: `current_frame.txt` as part of its own `compose_frame` call (same
312: dual-write shape sec. 16.3 describes for mutaclsym specifically) -
313: under the OLD architecture this could persist indefinitely (an
314: independent, unsynchronized second process racing to correct it); now
315: it is corrected DETERMINISTICALLY and immediately, because chtpm's own
316: render fires in the same synchronous sequence right after
317: `run_module_synchronous()` returns, not on a separately-timed poll
318: cycle. `system/renderer.c`'s own polling can catch this
319: transitional-write for a single, bounded frame, but the FINAL settled
320: frame is always correct - confirmed via full `frame_history.txt`
321: sequence review, not a single-snapshot check (see sec. 9.3's own
322: "rendering from a frame history file" testing principle - a snapshot
323: check would have missed this transitional frame entirely and drawn the
324: wrong conclusion either way).
325: 
326: [WRONG CLAIM, CORRECTED 2026-07-19 - see 0-CORRECTED item 3 above, kept
327: verbatim below for the historical record, do not follow it]:
328: NOT YET DONE: wsr-pal/egg-pals still use the OLDER, direct-dual-write
329: compose pattern (sec. 16.3) rather than this synchronous model, because
330: they have NO `<module>`/INTERACT usage at all anymore (sec. 16 removed
331: it, correctly, for pure-menu projects) - `wsr_menu_input.c`/
332: `ops/menu_input.c` are ALREADY one-shot ops in spirit (invoked, run,
333: exit), just still reached through the interact_relay.txt/persistent-
334: prisc+x-module path inherited from before sec. 16's fix, not through
335: chtpm's own real `OP:`/`SET_` synchronous dispatch. Converting them to
336: true `OP:`-style invocation (removing `prisc+x`/`main_loop_chtpm.pal`
337: from their own flow entirely) is a real, separate follow-up - named
338: here so it isn't silently forgotten, not done in this pass.
339: [END WRONG CLAIM - the actual, current, correct state (2026-07-19):
340: wsr-pal AND pal-chain both DO still use a real `<module>` tag in every
341: one of their own `.chtpm` layout files, and this is CORRECT, not a gap
342: to close - see 0-CORRECTED. Sec. 16's own real, still-valid fix was
343: narrower: remove the `onClick="INTERACT"` gate/button (wsr-pal's old
344: "Control Sim", egg-pals' old "Control Pets"), not the `<module>` tag.
345: `prisc+x`/`main_loop_chtpm.pal` should NOT be removed from their flow -
346: it's the correct, persistent dispatcher for their own `${piece_methods}`
347: -driven `KEY:n` buttons, matching real fuzz-op_manager.c's own
348: persistent-daemon precedent.]
349: 
350: ========================================================================
351: 1. SHARED-OPS: genuinely project-agnostic ops are SHARED SOURCE, not
352:    re-typed per project
353: ========================================================================
354: 
355: THE RULE: if an op has zero hardcoded game content and zero hardcoded
356: per-project constants (map ids, world names, game ids) - i.e. it would
357: behave identically no matter which project's PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT it's
358: pointed at - its SOURCE lives once, at
359: `2.muchi-verse/shared-ops/<name>.c`, and every consuming project's own
360: `scripts/build.sh` compiles that file directly into its own
361: `ops/+x/<name>.+x`. Runtime behavior is unaffected: `pal/main_loop.pal`
362: still calls `ops/+x/<name>.+x` exactly like any other op, and the op
363: still resolves `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT` (and `PRISC_PROJECT_ID` where
364: relevant) at runtime the same way a private op would. The only thing
365: that moves is where the .c file lives - one file edited once, instead
366: of N copies that silently drift apart.
367: 
368: THE CORRECTION THIS RULE MAKES: this project family's older "one op =
369: one binary, no shared headers" convention was being over-applied to
370: mean "never share any op across projects, ever" - re-typing the same
371: logic into every new project's own ops/ directory, even when the logic
372: was already 100% generic. That was wrong. "No shared headers" was
373: always about not coupling files WITHIN one project via fragile
374: .h-based structs (still true, still followed - see §1a below) - it was
375: never a mandate to duplicate genuinely identical, project-agnostic
376: logic forever. The concrete tell this had gone too far:
377: muchipal-editor-0.0's own `ops/piece_viewer.c` (before this refactor)
378: had a header comment reading "Identical in shape to
379: mutaclsym/ops/pdl_reader.c's own parse_method_line() - intentionally
380: duplicated, not shared, per this project family's doctrine" while ALSO
381: describing itself as "the concrete proof of cross-project
382: compatibility this editor exists to establish" - a THIRD independent
383: retyping of the same parser, in a project whose whole purpose is
384: proving cross-project compatibility.
385: 
386: THE REAL PRECEDENT (not invented - confirmed by direct code reads):
387: real 1.TPMOS's own `projects/fuzz-op/manager/fuzz-op_manager.c` (line
388: 716) calls `'%s/pieces/system/pdl/+x/pdl_reader.+x' %s list_methods` -
389: fuzz-op has no private pdl_reader copy of its own; it calls a SHARED
390: implementation living under `pieces/system/pdl/`, exactly the way
391: `pieces/system/` already hosts other shared infrastructure
392: (`input_dispatcher/`, `master_ledger/`'s `piece_manager.+x`, also
393: called directly from fuzz-op_manager.c). `pieces/system/emoji_extract/`
394: is likewise used by wraith-alpha AND was used directly, this same
395: session, for both mutaclsym's and zoo_0000's own asset generation.
396: op-ed's own README calls itself a "Thin Engine" - a genuine
397: multi-consumer engine, not a single-purpose game with private copies of
398: everything. The real structure being mirrored:
399: `projects/<name>/` = project-specific content + project-specific ops;
400: `pieces/system/<service>/` = shared, project-agnostic infrastructure
401: any project calls into via a path outside any single project's own
402: directory. `2.muchi-verse/shared-ops/` is this family's own equivalent
403: of `pieces/system/`.
404: 
405: §1a. WHAT "NO SHARED HEADERS" STILL MEANS (unchanged, not touched by
406:      this correction): don't couple files WITHIN a project via a
407:      shared `.h` defining structs both files reach into - that's still
408:      real, live doctrine (cdda-tpm-std-fast.txt sec. 1) and still
409:      followed by every project's own `scripts/build.sh` (each
410:      translation unit compiled and linked on its own line, no `.o`
411:      intermediaries). Sharing a whole, self-contained, project-agnostic
412:      .c file as SOURCE across projects is a completely different thing
413:      from that, and was never actually prohibited by the real
414:      precedent above.
415: 
416: §1b. THE BAR for "belongs in shared-ops/": zero hardcoded game content,
417:      zero hardcoded per-project constants. A hardcoded `#define MAP_ID`
418:      or `#define GAME_ID` is disqualifying UNTIL genericized (see
419:      `shared-ops-refactor-plan.txt` §2 for the real worked examples:
420:      `move_entity.c`/`xlector_input.c`/`pet_export.c`/`pet_import.c`
421:      all had this fixed by replacing the hardcoded constant with a
422:      dynamic `world_*/map_*/<piece_id>/` directory scan - the same
423:      pattern `pdl_reader.c`'s own `resolve_piece_pdl_path()` and real
424:      `system/prisc+x.c`'s own `resolve_piece_state_path()` already use
425:      - which is a strict improvement, not just a refactor, since it
426:      also removes any single-map-only assumption as a free side
427:      effect).
428: 
429: §1c. WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT SHARED, NAMED NOT SILENT (see
430:      `shared-ops-refactor-plan.txt` §3 for the full reasoning):
431:      - `compose_frame.c`/`compose_rgb_frame.c` per project - render
432:        genuinely different content per project. The font-glyph
433:        blit primitives inside ARE generic; factoring those out is a
434:        real, separate, smaller follow-up, not yet done.
435:      - Genuinely project-specific game content (e.g. zoo_0000's
436:        `feed_pet.c`/`pet_pet.c`/`play_pet.c` - mutaclsym has no pets;
437:        muchipal-editor isn't a game at all).
438: 
439: §1d. CURRENT shared-ops/ INVENTORY (`2.muchi-verse/shared-ops/`):
440:      `pdl_reader.c` (generic piece.pdl METHOD-table reader; also
441:      accepts an absolute path directly via `list_methods_full`, for a
442:      consumer like muchipal-editor-0.0 that reads an EXTERNAL project's
443:      piece.pdl by absolute path, not its own project-root + piece_id),
444:      `dump_rgb_png.c` + `lib/stb_image_write.h` (debug PNG dump of
445:      whatever a project's own `compose_rgb_frame.c` wrote),
446:      `move_entity.c` (generic single-tile mover, any piece, any map),
447:      `xlector_input.c` (the full xlector active-target reference
448:      implementation - see `z0.zoo_0000/dox/xlector-standard.md`),
449:      `pet_export.c`/`pet_import.c` (the cross-game piece transfer shim
450:      - see `z0.zoo_0000/dox/pet-import-export-standard.md`).
451: 
452: ========================================================================
453: 2. THE XLECTOR ACTIVE-TARGET STANDARD
454: ========================================================================
455: Full write-up: `z0.zoo_0000/dox/xlector-standard.md`. Reference
456: implementation: `shared-ops/xlector_input.c` (ported faithfully from
457: real 1.TPMOS's `projects/fuzz-op/manager/fuzz-op_manager.c`'s own
458: `route_input()`). Core idea: a single global cursor piece (`xlector`)
459: is the DEFAULT thing movement/method-hotkeys act on; Enter retargets to
460: whatever's sitting on xlector's own tile (gated per-piece by that
461: piece's own `piece.pdl` declaring a `select` METHOD row - a
462: project/piece-authored gate, not a hardcoded name-pattern exclusion);
463: `9`/Escape unconditionally snaps back to xlector first, before
464: anything else, every tick.
465: 
466: ========================================================================
467: 3. THE PET IMPORT/EXPORT (CROSS-GAME TRADE) STANDARD
468: ========================================================================
469: Full write-up: `z0.zoo_0000/dox/pet-import-export-standard.md`.
470: Reference implementation: `shared-ops/pet_export.c` /
471: `shared-ops/pet_import.c`. Core idea: exporting a piece physically
472: `rename()`s its whole directory out of the game's own `pieces/` tree
473: into a shared `exchange/` directory one level above any single game's
474: project root (override via `PRISC_EXCHANGE_ROOT`); a neutral
475: `trade_envelope.txt` (origin_game/kind/payload_id/display_name/etc,
476: shape proposed in mutaclsym's own `platform-vision.txt` §2) is written
477: into the piece's own directory before the move, so any importing game
478: reads just the envelope, not the origin game's own state.txt field
479: names. Zero-sum, not a copy - the piece is gone from the origin game
480: the instant export succeeds.
481: 
482: ========================================================================
483: 4. NAMED, NOT-YET-UNIFIED DUPLICATION (a real next step, tracked here
484:    on purpose so it isn't silently forgotten)
485: ========================================================================
486: The digit-accumulator + Enter-commit menu dispatch pattern (jump to a
487: numbered option by typing its digits, commit with Enter, abandon the
488: accumulator on any other key) is currently implemented FOUR separate
489: times, each over a differently-shaped state struct:
490: mutaclsym's `ops/choice.c`, egg-pals' `ops/menu_input.c`,
491: zoo_0000's `shared-ops/xlector_input.c` (the method-hotkey portion),
492: and muchipal-editor-0.0's `ops/project_browser.c`. This is real,
493: identified duplication of the exact kind §1 exists to eliminate - but
494: unifying four already-working, differently-shaped systems safely is a
495: bigger, riskier job than a single pass should take on blind. Not done
496: yet. Whoever picks this up next: read all four implementations in
497: full first, not just one, before designing a shared shape - they don't
498: all key their accumulator off the same struct field names today.
499: 
500: ========================================================================
501: 5. VERIFICATION BAR (applies to every change under this file)
502: ========================================================================
503: Zero compiler warnings is the bar, not "it compiles" (`-Wall -Wextra`,
504: every project's own `scripts/build.sh`). Test through the real
505: playable interface - keycodes appended to a live
506: `pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt`, verified against
507: `pieces/display/current_frame.txt` and the relevant piece's own
508: `state.txt` - never op-CLI-only. See `shared-ops-refactor-plan.txt` §5
509: for the specific regression suite re-run after the shared-ops switch
510: (xlector select/menu-switch-per-active-target, pet export/import
511: round-trip, emoji toggle both renderers, and - added when
512: muchipal-editor-0.0 was wired to the shared `pdl_reader.c` - the real
513: title->projects->project_menu->pieces->piece_detail flow against a real
514: external project's piece.pdl, confirmed byte-for-byte against that
515: piece.pdl's own METHOD rows).
516: 
517: ========================================================================
518: 6. CHTPM-TO-PAL LAYOUT BRIDGE: real 1.TPMOS's own declarative UI
519:    markup, usable directly - a fork, not a rewrite
520: ========================================================================
521: Full rationale, the empirical test that justified this, and the exact
522: patch sites: `chtpm-to-pal-layout-plan.txt` (its own "STATUS UPDATE"
523: section supersedes the modular-rewrite design further down that same
524: file - read the status update). Real 1.TPMOS's `.chtpm` declarative
525: layout format (`<panel>`/`<text>`/`<button onClick= href=>`, `${var}`
526: interpolation) is directly usable by any pal-native project via two
527: shared files - NOT a from-scratch reimplementation, and NOT the
528: 5-op modular rewrite an earlier pass in this same doc proposed before
529: testing the real thing first.
530: 
531: - `shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c` - a TRACKED FORK of real
532:   `chtpm_parser.c`, exactly 2 patches (search "PAL-NATIVE PATCH" in
533:   that file for each): (1) `is_modern_layout()` detects a pal-native
534:   project via the real `PRISC_PROJECT_ID` env var instead of guessing
535:   from the layout path's own substrings; (2) `inject_command()` routes
536:   onClick-dispatched commands to `pieces/display/pending_command.txt`
537:   instead of the file prisc+x's own `OP_READ_HISTORY` reads - a
538:   CRITICAL correctness fix (confirmed by reading `prisc+x.c` directly:
539:   a non-numeric line left in that file would permanently wedge that
540:   opcode's own read cursor, not just misread once). The file's own
541:   input-reading loop is untouched, still reads its real, native
542:   `pieces/keyboard/history.txt` in `KEY_PRESSED: N` format.
543: - `shared-ops/keyboard_input.c` - `append_key()` dual-writes: the
544:   original bare-int line to `pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt`
545:   (unchanged, zero risk to any pal script's own `read_history`) AND a
546:   second `KEY_PRESSED: N` line to `pieces/keyboard/history.txt` for
547:   `chtpm_parser_pal.c` to read. wsr-pal's own `keyboard_input.c` is NOT
548:   switched onto this shared file (it genuinely differs on quit-key
549:   handling - see §1c's own "don't force-share" principle) but got the
550:   identical dual-write addition applied locally instead.
551: - Runs as a PERSISTENT process (`system/chtpm_parser_pal`), same
552:   category as `system/renderer.c`/`system/gl_mirror.c`, NOT a one-shot
553:   op. Needs `-Wno-unused-result -Wno-stringop-truncation` on its own
554:   compile line only (~20 pre-existing real-upstream warnings, none
555:   touched by either patch - confirmed via test build this reaches zero
556:   warnings with those two flags; every other file in each project
557:   still builds under plain `-Wall -Wextra`).
558: - onClick verbs: `send_command()`'s real upstream code only relays a
559:   SPECIFIC recognized set of prefixes (`SET_*`, `OP:`, `MP3:`,
560:   `LAUNCH:`, `LOAD_PROJECT:`, `KEY:n`, `BACK`, `RELEASE`,
561:   `PROJECT_ACTION:`, `SETTINGS_PAGE:`, `PROJECT_PAGE:`) to
562:   `inject_command()` - anything else is silently rejected before ever
563:   reaching the pal-native redirect (hit this live: a `GOTO:projects`
564:   onClick was silently dropped until renamed to `SET_SCREEN:projects`,
565:   which matches the recognized `SET_` prefix). Pick a recognized prefix
566:   for any onClick a pal-native layout needs relayed.
567: - REAL, LIVE-CAUGHT PITFALL when converting an existing screen to
568:   chtpm and handing off to an existing pal-driven flow (as done for
569:   muchipal-editor-0.0's own title screen): prisc+x's own pal script
570:   always starts its `read_history` cursor at byte 0 on a fresh launch.
571:   Since `keyboard_input` dual-writes every keystroke unconditionally,
572:   any keys pressed during the chtpm phase are ALSO still sitting at the
573:   start of `pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt`, unconsumed by prisc+x -
574:   starting prisc+x without truncating that file first replays those
575:   same keystrokes into the pal-driven side the instant it launches,
576:   silently double-dispatching (confirmed live: a single '1'+Enter used
577:   to leave the chtpm title screen landed the pal-driven side two
578:   screens deep instead of one, because it replayed that exact
579:   keystroke itself). Always truncate `pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt`
580:   at the moment of handoff, right before launching the pal-driven
581:   process.
582: - WHAT'S NOT DONE YET, named: no dynamic-length screen (a project list,
583:   a piece list) has been converted - chtpm's own markup has no
584:   repeat/loop construct, so a screen whose row count varies at runtime
585:   needs a small generator step (analogous to `load_dynamic_methods()`'s
586:   own "build a var containing pre-formatted `<button>` HTML" trick) not
587:   yet built. `inject_raw_key()`'s pal-native behavior is also
588:   undecided (would `onClick="KEY:n"` double-dispatch through both
589:   prisc+x's own pal script and this process's own `process_key()`?) -
590:   flagged in the fork's own header comment, not silently assumed safe.
591: 
592: ========================================================================
593: 7. THE INTERACT + MODULE PATTERN (real game-map control handoff -
594:    direct user priority, "a big deal")
595: ========================================================================
596: Full mechanism (exact real code, exact mapping onto this family):
597: `chtpm-to-pal-layout-plan.txt` §8. Summary: a layout's
598: `<module>${module_path}</module>` + `<interact src="...history.txt" />`
599: + a button `onClick="INTERACT"` together let arrow/number keys pass
600: STRAIGHT THROUGH a live game (a SEPARATE, PARALLEL persistent process,
601: launched by `launch_module()`) instead of being consumed by chtpm's own
602: UI nav - this is how real fuzz-op/wraith-alpha put a real, controllable
603: game map inside a chtpm menu shell. Every piece of this already exists,
604: completely unpatched, in `shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c` - nothing
605: about the fork itself needs to change again for this.
606: 
607: For a pal-native project, THE MODULE = a second `system/prisc+x`
608: process running that project's own real `pal/game_loop.pal` (not a
609: hand-written manager.c). THE VIEW BRIDGE = an additive dual-write in a
610: project's own EXISTING `compose_frame.c` (writes
611: `pieces/apps/player_app/view.txt` alongside its normal output), not a
612: new op. `module_path` gets set directly in
613: `pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt` (already read unconditionally) -
614: zero chtpm_parser_pal.c patch needed. `onClick="INTERACT"` (verbatim,
615: not `SET_`-prefixed) is what process_key() actually checks for this -
616: separate from and unrelated to the recognized-prefix gotcha in §6
617: above.
618: 
619: REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT, CORRECTED (built and verified in zoo_0000,
620: 2026-07-17 - full account: `chtpm-to-pal-layout-plan.txt` §8.4):
621: `<interact src>` must NEVER point at the same file
622: `shared-ops/keyboard_input.c` already writes to directly
623: (`pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt`) - that file already gets every
624: keystroke unconditionally, for the project's own regular "run" mode.
625: Relaying interact-mode keys into the SAME file double-dispatches every
626: one of them (confirmed live: 2 right+1 down moved the game 4 rights+2
627: downs). Fix: `<interact src>` points at a genuinely separate file
628: (`pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt`), and the module reads it
629: via `read_history`'s NEWLY-FIXED literal-path 3-arg form,
630: `read_history <path> xD, xS` - `shared-ops/prisc+x.c`'s own literal-path
631: parsing used to silently default the position register to x0 (reset to
632: 0 every instruction by this VM's own main loop), permanently breaking
633: incremental reads; fixed, with every existing 2-register
634: `read_history xD, xS` call completely untouched.
635: `shared-ops/prisc+x.c` was moved here the same way `keyboard_input.c`
636: was (confirmed byte-identical across all 4 projects first) - a real VM
637: fix belongs in one place. A project's own regular `pal/main_loop.pal`
638: stays completely unmodified; a NEW, separate `pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal`
639: (identical except the one `read_history` line) is what the module
640: launches instead - zero risk to the already-proven gameplay path.
641: 
642: Built and live-verified in zoo_0000, mutaclsym, AND egg-pals
643: (`./button.sh chtpm`), full round-trip through the real playable
644: interface each time (full detail: chtpm-to-pal-layout-plan.txt §8.4-
645: 8.5). egg-pals is a genuinely different case worth knowing about: purely
646: digit-menu-driven (no map/movement), and its own `prisc+x.c`/
647: `keyboard_input.c` are NOT switched to shared-ops (real Windows/MinGW
648: support + a documented bug fix neither shared version has) - the same
649: literal-path `read_history` bug was patched in its own local copy
650: directly instead, matching §1c's "genuinely different, patch locally"
651: principle. wsr-pal has the base bridge only, not yet the full
652: interact+module pattern.
653: 
654: ========================================================================
655: 8. KILL_ALL.SH: family-wide surgical process cleanup
656: ========================================================================
657: Real incident, this session: a Ctrl+C'd mutaclsym session left a
658: genuinely orphaned `system/prisc+x` + `system/keyboard_input` pair
659: running, found only by accident via `ps aux` - not by any project's
660: own `button.sh kill` action (which had independently been matching an
661: absolute path that never appears in these processes' real, relative-
662: launched command lines - see [[feedback_pkill_relative_path_gotcha]]).
663: Per direct instruction ("maybe it should run a kill_all.sh script like
664: 1.tpmos does") - confirmed real 1.TPMOS has exactly this precedent,
665: `pieces/os/kill_all.sh`, including its own past real incidents with
666: this same class of bug (`#.dev-storage/#.dox/cpu-issues_/
667: emergency_kill.sh`/`sanity_kill.sh`).
668: 
669: `2.muchi-verse/kill_all.sh` - modeled directly on real 1.TPMOS's own
670: file (same `surgical_kill()` shape, SIGKILL, a nuclear-option
671: catch-all fallback at the end). Family-wide, not per-project - every
672: pal-native project shares the same binary names now (mostly literally
673: the same shared-ops/ source), so one script here is callable from any
674: project's directory instead of five near-identical copies. Every
675: project's own `button.sh kill` action calls it (`bash
676: ".../2.muchi-verse/kill_all.sh"`) as the authoritative cleanup step,
677: in addition to (not instead of) that project's own fast local
678: `pkill -f "system/<name>"` pass.
679: 
680: WHY SIGKILL, NOT PLAIN `kill`: `chtpm_parser_pal.c` registers a SIGINT
681: handler but no SIGTERM handler - a plain `kill` still terminates it via
682: SIGTERM's own default disposition, but skips its own `cleanup_module()`
683: (only runs on SIGINT), potentially orphaning its own spawned module.
684: `kill_all.sh` doesn't rely on any process's own signal handling running
685: cleanly at all.
686: 
687: ========================================================================
688: 9. TWO MORE REAL BUGS, USER-REPORTED, FOUND VIA DIRECT KEY INJECTION
689: ========================================================================
690: Both found testing a live "unresponsive ascii, no GL window" report in
691: mutaclsym's own `chtpm` mode - full account:
692: `chtpm-to-pal-layout-plan.txt` §8.6-8.7.
693: 
694: 9.1 THE MODULE MUST NOT OWN A QUIT PATH OF ITS OWN
695: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
696: Every `main_loop_chtpm.pal` originally kept the real `main_loop.pal`'s
697: own `beq x2, x9, quit` ('q' halts). chtpm's own `onClick="INTERACT"`
698: relays EVERY key through while engaged - including 'q' - so pressing
699: 'q' while controlling the hero/pets (reasonable, since 'q' IS the real
700: quit key in normal gameplay) halted the MODULE specifically, while
701: chtpm itself had no way to notice and kept relaying keys into a file
702: nothing was reading anymore. Fixed by removing the quit path from all
703: 3 `main_loop_chtpm.pal` variants entirely - the module never owns
704: quitting; that's chtpm's job alone (top-level 'q', or
705: `system/keyboard_input`'s own unconditional exit-on-'q', which takes
706: the whole session down together regardless of chtpm's own internal
707: state).
708: 
709: 9.2 `pieces/system/quit_flag.txt` MUST BE RESET, NOT JUST AT "run"'s
710:     OWN TOP
711: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
712: `system/renderer.c`'s own loop is `while (!quit_requested())`, checking
713: whether this file is non-empty - written by `keyboard_input.c` on
714: EVERY exit, any session. Every project's own `run` action already
715: resets it at its own top, but the new `chtpm` actions (and, found in
716: the same pass, zoo_0000's own pre-existing `run` action) did NOT. Once
717: ANY session anywhere was ever quit via 'q', this file stays non-empty
718: forever - so the NEXT launch's own renderer sees
719: `quit_requested()==true` before its own loop even starts: it prints
720: exactly one frame (the unconditional `render_frame()` call before the
721: loop) and exits immediately. Everything else (`chtpm_parser_pal`, the
722: module, `keyboard_input`) keeps running fine underneath, correctly
723: updating `current_frame.txt` - but nothing is left to print it to the
724: terminal, giving the EXACT "looks unresponsive" symptom reported live.
725: Fixed by adding `: > pieces/system/quit_flag.txt` to every `chtpm`
726: action (and to zoo_0000's own `run` action, which never had it at
727: all).
728: 
729: 9.3 THE TEST METHOD THAT ACTUALLY FOUND BOTH (direct user request -
730:     "include that test method in our standards doc, it works much
731:     better") - PREFER THIS OVER A FIFO-SIMULATED KEYBOARD
732: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
733: Earlier live tests in this family drove input through a real
734: `system/keyboard_input` process fed via a named FIFO, simulating an
735: actual terminal. That works, but is genuinely flaky for single,
736: isolated bytes (confirmed repeatedly: a lone ESC byte needed 2-4
737: retries before `read_key()`'s own follow-up timeout reads registered
738: it) - real timing noise, not a logic bug, but it wastes real test
739: cycles and can mask or manufacture false negatives.
740: 
741: THE BETTER METHOD, matching real 1.TPMOS's own `inject_raw_key()`/
742: `inject_command()` naming and spirit: skip `keyboard_input` (and any
743: FIFO) entirely. Launch `system/renderer` and `system/chtpm_parser_pal`
744: directly, then INJECT KEYS BY WRITING DIRECTLY to whichever file the
745: next consumer reads - `pieces/keyboard/history.txt` with
746: `KEY_PRESSED: N` lines for chtpm's own top-level nav (exactly the
747: bytes `keyboard_input.c` would have written), or
748: `pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt` directly with a bare
749: integer if testing the module's own reading in isolation. This is
750: strictly more deterministic (a plain `echo "KEY_PRESSED: 1001" >>
751: pieces/keyboard/history.txt` either lands or it doesn't - no raw-tty
752: timeout window to race against) and is EXACTLY how this file's own §9.1
753: and §9.2 bugs were actually found and confirmed fixed: inject Enter,
754: check the frame; inject an arrow, check the real piece `state.txt`
755: changed by exactly one step; inject 'q' while "in control," confirm
756: the module process is STILL alive via `ps aux` afterward, then inject
757: another arrow and confirm it still responds. Prefer this method for any
758: future interact+module work in this family; reserve the FIFO/real-
759: keyboard_input method only for the rare case of testing
760: `keyboard_input.c` itself (its own escape-sequence decoding, raw-mode
761: handling, etc.) rather than what's downstream of it.
762: 
763: ========================================================================
764: 10. PAL VS. C - READ PAL-VS-C-ARCHITECTURE.txt BEFORE ASSUMING "OPS ARE
765:     ALWAYS C"
766: ========================================================================
767: 
768: Every op today is a C binary (sec. 1's own convention) - but that is
769: the CURRENT STATE, not the permanent design. `prisc+x` is named after
770: RISC-V on purpose, and the real, confirmed direction is eventually
771: compiling pal+ops directly to real RISC-V, gated on the architecture
772: staying in reasonably correct shape. `shared-ops/prisc+x.c` now has a
773: real `ecall` syscall instruction (file I/O + a `SYS_GET_KV_INT`/
774: `SYS_SET_KV_INT` pair matching every op's own duplicated `read_kv_int`/
775: `set_kv_int` idiom), and a real op's core logic (`ops/pet_pet.c`'s own
776: state mutation) has been prototyped running as native pal instructions
777: against zoo_0000's own live data, verified working.
778: 
779: Full rationale, the ECALL design, and - most importantly - which
780: projects are the CONFIRMED first real-conversion targets (zoo_0000 and
781: muchipal-editor-0.0, per direct instruction) and which kinds of ops are
782: good/bad candidates: see `PAL-VS-C-ARCHITECTURE.txt` in this same
783: directory. Read it before assuming a new op has to be C, and before
784: assuming an existing C op is untouchable architecture rather than "not
785: converted yet."
786: 
787: ========================================================================
788: 11. GL/RGB RENDERING FOR CHTPM MODE - shared-ops/chtpm_rgb_render.c,
789:     real wraith-alpha's own two-stage pulse pattern, and the mistakes
790:     that cost a full day of user-reported "still doesn't look right"
791: ========================================================================
792: 
793: Full account, all real bugs found via live testing (not guessed), all
794: fixed and re-verified: `!.handoff-july17.txt` sec. 1-9c. This section
795: is the durable, going-forward RULE version of that whole thread - read
796: it before touching `shared-ops/chtpm_rgb_render.c`, any project's own
797: `system/gl_mirror.c`, or `ops/compose_rgb_frame.c` again.
798: 
799: 11.1 THE TWO-STAGE PULSE IS MANDATORY, NOT OPTIONAL
800: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
801: A renderer that writes `pieces/display/rgb_frame.raw` must grow
802: `pieces/display/rgb_frame_changed.txt` (a fixed name, matching real
803: `wraith_gl.c`'s own `WRAITH_FRAME_TRIGGER` convention closely) ONLY
804: AFTER that write is fully complete - `system/gl_mirror.c` watches
805: ONLY this file, NEVER `pieces/display/frame_changed.txt` (the
806: UPSTREAM trigger a renderer itself reacts to). Confirmed via a live
807: checksum trace (compare `rgb_frame.receipt.txt`'s own checksum against
808: `gl_display.receipt.txt`'s loaded checksum, sampled every ~30ms
809: through a keypress burst) that using the SAME file for both causes a
810: real, PERMANENT stall - not a brief lag - because the GL reader can
811: "consume" the upstream trigger before the renderer has even started
812: its own work, and then has no further signal once real output is
813: ready. `ops/compose_rgb_frame.c` and `shared-ops/chtpm_rgb_render.c`
814: both already grow this file correctly - copy that shape for any new
815: renderer, don't reinvent.
816: 
817: 11.2 WRITE rgb_frame.raw AND rgb_frame.receipt.txt ATOMICALLY
818: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
819: `fopen(path, "wb")` truncates before writing - a reader polling
820: frequently (a daemon re-rendering ~60/sec, unlike `compose_rgb_frame.c`
821: which only writes on an actual keypress) can catch a torn read.
822: `system/gl_mirror.c`'s own `load_texture()` does a plain `fread()` with
823: no retry and zero-fills on a short read - confirmed via reproduction,
824: not assumed. Write to `<path>.tmp` then `rename()` into place -
825: `write_file_atomic()` in `shared-ops/chtpm_rgb_render.c` is the
826: reference implementation.
827: 
828: 11.3 SETTLE BEFORE READING A SEPARATELY-WRITTEN INPUT FILE
829: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
830: `chtpm_parser_pal.c` grows `frame_changed.txt` and writes
831: `current_frame.txt` as two unsynchronized writes - a renderer that
832: reads `current_frame.txt` the instant it sees the pulse move can catch
833: it mid-rewrite (confirmed live: a real torn read showing a MIX of old
834: and new footer text). Real `wraith_rgb_daemon.c` has NO protection
835: against this at all (checked directly - not something to copy from
836: there). Fix used here: after detecting the pulse changed, poll the
837: TARGET file's own size until it reads the same value twice in a row
838: (capped at ~20 attempts) before reading its content - a direct check
839: on the actual file being read, not a blind fixed delay on an unrelated
840: proxy file.
841: 
842: 11.4 gl_mirror.c MUST READ frame_w/frame_h FROM THE RECEIPT, NEVER
843:      HARDCODE THEM
844: ------------------------------------------------------------------------

(Output capped at 50 KB. Showing lines 1-844. Use offset=845 to continue.)
</content>

1: ========================================================================
2: XYZOS-PITFALLS - Common mistakes and gotchas encountered during
3: 01.lpns+map+4 development. Read this BEFORE modifying any pal project.
4: Add pitfalls as they are discovered, do not remove existing entries.
5: 
6: RENAME NOTE (2026-07-30): renamed from !.pal-pitfalls+1.txt, sibling
7: rename to !.xyzos-standards+1.txt (see that file's own header note for
8: the full "why xyzos, not just pal" reasoning) - every cross-reference

10: ========================================================================
11: 
12: ========================================================================
13: PITFALL 1: system() in orchestrator causes zombie processes
14: ========================================================================
15: 
16: SYMPTOM: After Ctrl+C, processes continue running, consuming CPU.
17: ROOT CAUSE: orchestrator.c used system() for compile_binaries(),
18: mkdir, and initial compose. system() spawns a shell which spawns
19: the actual command - the shell process may not be reaped properly,
20: and signals don't propagate correctly through the shell layer.
21: 
22: FIX: Replace ALL system() calls with fork/exec/waitpid (Bible §3).
23: Each helper function: fork() → freopen(/dev/null) → execl() → _exit(1).
24: Parent: waitpid(pid, &status, 0) to reap.
25: 
26: FILES: system/orchestrator.c:compile_single(), ensure_directories(),
27:        initial_compose()
28: 
29: ========================================================================
30: PITFALL 2: kill_all_tracked() misses child-of-child processes
31: ========================================================================
32: 
33: SYMPTOM: game_manager's child ops (game_turn_input, game_compose_frame)
34: continue running after orchestrator exits.
35: ROOT CAUSE: game_manager forks ops via run_op(), but those PIDs are
36: never logged to proc_list.txt. If game_manager is killed mid-op,
37: the op becomes orphaned.
38: 
39: FIX: 3-layer cascading kill:
40:   Layer 1: kill(0, SIGTERM) to entire process group
41:   Layer 2: File-backed PID sweep (proc_list.txt)
42:   Layer 3: kill_all.sh nuclear option (pkill by pattern)
43: 
44: The process group kill (Layer 1) catches most orphans. The nuclear
45: option (Layer 3) catches everything else by matching binary names.
46: 
47: FILES: system/orchestrator.c:kill_process_group(), kill_all_tracked(),
48:        run_final_kill_sweep()
49: 
50: ========================================================================
51: PITFALL 3: sscanf overwrites search variable in config lookups
52: ========================================================================
53: 
54: SYMPTOM: NPC moves alice instead of itself. Bot1 (player 2) moves
55: alice (player 1) to a new position, then bot2 also moves alice, etc.
56: ROOT CAUSE: sscanf(line, "player_%d_name=%49s", &current_player, ...)
57: was using &current_player as the index variable. When sscanf found
58: player_2_name, it wrote 2 into current_player, overwriting the
59: original value (which was tracking which player to search for).
60: 
61: FIX: Use a temp variable for sscanf:
62:   int pidx;
63:   if (sscanf(line, "player_%d_name=%49s", &pidx, player_name) == 2
64:       && pidx == current_player) break;
65: 
66: The && pidx == current_player check ensures we only use the result
67: when the index matches the player we're looking for.
68: 
69: FILES: ops/game_turn_input.c:44-45, 59-60, 68-69
70: 
71: ========================================================================
72: PITFALL 4: compose_frame writing to frame_changed.txt causes infinite loop
73: ========================================================================
74: 
75: SYMPTOM: Renderer flickers, CPU spikes, frame never stabilizes.
76: ROOT CAUSE: chtpm_parser_pal's compose_frame() was writing to
77: frame_changed.txt (the same file the renderer watches). Parser's
78: main loop detected growth → dirty=1 → called compose_frame() again
79: → wrote to frame_changed.txt again → infinite loop.
80: 
81: FIX: compose_frame() writes to renderer_pulse.txt (NOT frame_changed.txt).
82: game_compose_frame.c writes to frame_changed.txt (game state changes).
83: Renderer watches BOTH files:
84:   - frame_changed.txt growth → immediate render
85:   - renderer_pulse.txt growth → wait for current_frame.txt to stabilize,
86:     then render
87: 
88: The two-file pattern prevents the infinite loop while still triggering
89: renders for both game state changes and parser recomposition.
90: 
91: FILES: system/renderer.c:96-113, ops/game_compose_frame.c:126-130
92: 
93: ========================================================================
94: PITFALL 5: Stale keyboard/history.txt replays old keys on startup
95: ========================================================================
96: 
97: SYMPTOM: Keys from previous session fire immediately on launch.
98: ROOT CAUSE: chtpm_parser_pal reads keyboard/history.txt from byte 0
99: on fresh launch. If the file wasn't cleared, old KEY_PRESSED: lines
100: replay into the new session.
101: 
102: FIX: button.sh or orchestrator MUST clear on launch:
103:   : > pieces/keyboard/history.txt
104: 
105: This is orthogonal to interact_relay.txt - both files must be cleared.
106: 
107: FILES: system/orchestrator.c:155-156, button.sh
108: 
109: ========================================================================
110: PITFALL 6: quit_flag.txt left non-empty from previous session
111: ========================================================================
112: 
113: SYMPTOM: Renderer prints exactly one frame then exits. Everything
114: else keeps running but nothing is displayed.
115: ROOT CAUSE: keyboard_input writes "1" to quit_flag.txt on 'q' press.
116: If the session wasn't cleaned up properly, this file stays non-empty.
117: Next launch's renderer sees quit_requested()==true before loop starts.
118: 
119: FIX: orchestrator MUST clear on launch:
120:   FILE *qf = fopen("pieces/system/quit_flag.txt", "w");
121:   if (qf) fclose(qf);
122: 
123: Also clear in button.sh kill action.
124: 
125: FILES: system/orchestrator.c:160-161, button.sh
126: 
127: ========================================================================
128: PITFALL 7: pulse_frame_marker() dead code causes confusion
129: ========================================================================
130: 
131: SYMPTOM: Agent adds another pulse_frame_marker() call, causing
132: double-render or infinite loop.
133: ROOT CAUSE: game_manager.c still has pulse_frame_marker() function
134: definition (line 39) even though execute_action() no longer calls it.
135: game_compose_frame.c already writes to frame_changed.txt.
136: 
137: FIX: Remove the dead function. It serves no purpose and confuses
138: future agents who think it's part of the render pipeline.
139: 
140: FILES: system/game_manager.c:39-42 (dead code, should be removed)
141: 
142: ========================================================================
143: PITFALL 8: PAL layout <module> tag must have exact binary path
144: ========================================================================
145: 
146: SYMPTOM: Parser fails to launch module, no game manager running.
147: ROOT CAUSE: <module> tag content is split on spaces by launch_module().
148: If the path is wrong or missing, execv() fails silently (_exit(1)).
149: 
150: FIX: PAL layout must use:
151:   <module>system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal</module>
152: 
153: C layout must use:
154:   <module>system/game_manager</module>
155: 
156: Parser's launch_module() at line 1625-1631:
157:   char* token = strtok(full_cmd, " ");
158:   while (token && arg_count < 15) {
159:       args[arg_count++] = (arg_count == 0) ?
160:           build_path_malloc(token) : strdup(token);
161:       token = strtok(NULL, " ");
162:   }
163: 
164: The first token gets build_path_malloc() (resolves relative to project root).
165: Subsequent tokens are passed as-is (argv to the binary).
166: 
167: FILES: system/chtpm_parser_pal.c:1621-1638
168: 
169: ========================================================================
170: PITFALL 9: interact_relay.txt must be truncated after read
171: ========================================================================
172: 
173: SYMPTOM: Same keycode dispatches multiple times, actions repeat.
174: ROOT CAUSE: game_manager reads interact_relay.txt but doesn't truncate
175: it. Next poll reads the same keys again.
176: 
177: FIX: After reading, truncate the file:
178:   FILE *trunc = fopen("pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt", "w");
179:   if (trunc) fclose(trunc);
180:   last_relay_pos = 0;
181: 
182: Must reset last_relay_pos to 0 after truncation.
183: 
184: FILES: system/game_manager.c:147-149
185: 
186: ========================================================================
187: PITFALL 10: PAL script must not have its own quit path
188: ========================================================================
189: 
190: SYMPTOM: Module dies on 'q' press, parser keeps relaying keys into
191: empty relay file, system appears frozen.
192: ROOT CAUSE: PAL script had `beq x2, x9, quit` (checking for 'q' key).
193: In INTERACT mode, ALL keys pass through including 'q'. Module halts,
194: parser doesn't notice.
195: 
196: FIX: Remove quit path from main_loop_chtpm.pal entirely.
197: Quitting is parser's job (top-level 'q' or keyboard_input's exit).
198: Module never owns the quit decision.
199: 
200: FILES: pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal (no quit/exit instruction)
201: 
202: ========================================================================
203: PITFALL 11: button.sh --pal flag must set env var before exec
204: ========================================================================
205: 
206: SYMPTOM: Orchestrator ignores --pal flag, always uses C layout.
207: ROOT CAUSE: button.sh parsed arguments incorrectly, or PAL_LAYOUT
208: wasn't exported before exec.
209: 
210: FIX: button.sh must:
211:   1. Parse --pal flag in argument loop
212:   2. export PAL_LAYOUT="..." before exec
213:   3. Orchestrator reads getenv("PAL_LAYOUT")
214: 
215:   for arg in "$@"; do
216:       case "$arg" in
217:           --pal) PAL_MODE=1 ;;
218:       esac
219:   done
220:   if [ "$PAL_MODE" -eq 1 ]; then
221:       export PAL_LAYOUT="pieces/chtpm/layouts/..._pal.chtpm"
222:   fi
223:   exec "$SCRIPT_DIR/system/orchestrator"
224: 
225: FILES: button.sh, system/orchestrator.c:172-180
226: 
227: ========================================================================
228: PITFALL 12: game_compose_frame writes view.txt, not current_frame.txt
229: ========================================================================
230: 
231: SYMPTOM: ${game_map} variable is empty in rendered frame.
232: ROOT CAUSE: Agent expected game_compose_frame to write directly to
233: current_frame.txt (the renderer's input). But chtpm_parser_pal owns
234: current_frame.txt - writing to it from an op clobbers chrome.
235: 
236: FIX: game_compose_frame writes to pieces/apps/player_app/view.txt.
237: Parser reads view.txt via load_vars(), substitutes into ${game_map}
238: in the layout, then writes the composed result to current_frame.txt.
239: 
240: The chain: view.txt → parser load_vars() → ${game_map} → current_frame.txt
241: 
242: FILES: ops/game_compose_frame.c:85-123
243: 
244: ========================================================================
245: PITFALL 13: PAL "op" keyword doesn't exist - use "exec" instead
246: ========================================================================
247: 
248: SYMPTOM: PAL script runs but nothing happens when keys are pressed.
249: No actions dispatched, no state changes, ledger stays empty.
250: ROOT CAUSE: Used `op game_turn_input x10` syntax in PAL script.
251: The PAL instruction is `exec`, not `op`. prisc+x doesn't have an
252: `op` instruction at all.
253: 
254: FIX: Use `exec ./ops/game_turn_input x10` (not `op`).
255: 
256: The `exec` instruction:
257:   - Takes a literal path as first argument
258:   - Optionally takes registers as arguments (their int values are
259:     passed as string arguments to the command)
260:   - Runs the command via system() (yes, this is inside prisc+x.c
261:     itself - the exec op is the ONE place system() is acceptable,
262:     matching real TPMOS precedent)
263: 
264: Correct syntax:
265:   exec ./ops/game_turn_input x10    # runs: ./ops/game_turn_input <value_of_x10>
266:   exec ./ops/game_compose_frame     # runs: ./ops/game_compose_frame
267: 
268: Wrong syntax (does nothing):
269:   op game_turn_input x10            # parse error or silent no-op
270:   op game_compose_frame
271: 
272: FILES: system/prisc+x.c:1033-1046 (OP_EXEC handler)
273: 
274: ========================================================================
275: PITFALL 14: compose_rgb_frame non-atomic write causes RGB flicker
276: ========================================================================
277: 
278: SYMPTOM: RGB/GL window briefly flashes black on every frame update.
279: ROOT CAUSE: compose_rgb_frame.c wrote rgb_frame.raw (and
280: rgb_frame_3d_overlay.raw) via plain fopen("wb"), which TRUNCATES the
281: file to 0 bytes before any new content is written. A reader
282: (chtpm_rgb_render or gl_mirror) that opens the file during that
283: truncate→write window sees an empty/partial buffer = black frame.
284: 
285: FIX: Write to a .tmp file, then rename() over the real path. POSIX
286: rename() onto the same filesystem is atomic - any reader sees either
287: the complete OLD file or the complete NEW one, never a partial one.
288: Matches chtpm_rgb_render.c's own write_file_atomic() pattern (see
289: that file's own header comment at line 539-554 for the full
290: writeup of this exact race class).
291: 
292:   char tmp[PATH_BUF + 4];
293:   snprintf(tmp, sizeof(tmp), "%s.tmp", real_path);
294:   FILE *f = fopen(tmp, "wb");
295:   fwrite(data, 1, len, f);
296:   fclose(f);
297:   rename(tmp, real_path);
298: 
299: FILES: ops/compose_rgb_frame.c (overlay write + frame write)
300:

404: =======================================================================
405: PITFALL 20: launch_redirect()'s FIXED 2-ARG execl() silently truncates a
406: multi-arg child's real argv - process "starts" but exits on its own
407: usage message
408: =======================================================================
409: DATE: 2026-07-26
410: SEVERITY: HIGH (kills P2P entirely, but LOOKS like it half-works)
411: PROJECT: 044.pal-chat-irc
412: 
413: SYMPTOM: Two users in the same room, on two concurrent local sessions of
414: the SAME project, do not see each other's messages update live. The
415: first joiner sitting idle never sees a second joiner's message appear -
416: only re-renders when the first joiner does something themselves (their
417: own keypress causes their own recompose, which then happens to pick up
418: the already-shared room file).
419: 
420: ROOT CAUSE: system/orchestrator.c's launch_redirect(path, arg1, arg2,
421: logfile) only supports a FIXED 2-argv-slot execl() call. But the actual
422: call site needed 5 real args for palnet_peer (own_kind, project_id,
423: piece_id, outbox_file, inbox_file[, seek_kind]):
424: 
425:   launch_redirect("ops/+x/palnet_peer.+x",
426:                   "irc_node", "pal-chat-irc - net/outbox.txt net/inbox.txt irc_node",
427:                   logfile);
428: 
429: execl() does NOT shell-split arg2 - palnet_peer received it as ONE
430: literal argv[2] string, saw argc=3 instead of the required 6, and
431: exited immediately printing its own "Usage: ..." message straight into
432: the (rarely-checked) log file. Net result: palnet_peer never actually
433: ran, net/inbox.txt on every session stayed permanently empty, and
434: net/presence/ stayed empty too - the entire P2P layer was silently dead
435: from the very first `./button.sh run`.
436: 
437: WHY THIS WENT UNNOTICED: rooms/<room>/messages.txt and (after the
438: master-ledger pass) data/master_ledger.txt are REAL, SHARED files
439: (symlinked into every session of the same local project) - so any
440: session that recomposes ITS OWN view for ANY reason (its own keypress,
441: its own post) reads the already-correct shared file directly, with zero
442: help from P2P. This gives strong false-positive confidence that
443: messaging/networking works, when actually the ONLY thing making it look
444: that way is direct filesystem sharing between same-machine sessions -
445: the mechanism that's supposed to proactively notify an IDLE session
446: (palnet_peer -> net/inbox.txt -> chat_inbox_watcher -> trigger_render())
447: was never running at all.
448: 
449: FIX: added a proper argv-array launcher (mirrors 041.pal-chain's own
450: orchestrator.c launch(path, argv[], argc) which already did this
451: correctly via execv()):
452: 
453:   static pid_t launch_argv_redirect(const char *path, const char **argv,
454:                                      int argc, const char *logfile) {
455:       ...
456:       char *args[16];
457:       args[0] = (char *)path;
458:       for (i = 0; i < argc && i < 14; i++) args[i + 1] = (char *)argv[i];
459:       args[i + 1] = NULL;
460:       execv(path, args);
461:       ...
462:   }
463: 
464: Call site fixed to pass real separate argv slots:
465:   const char *pn_args[] = { "irc_node", "pal-chat-irc", "-",
466:                              "net/outbox.txt", "net/inbox.txt", "irc_node" };
467:   launch_argv_redirect("ops/+x/palnet_peer.+x", pn_args, 6, logfile);
468: 
469: VERIFIED LIVE (not just read): before the fix, /tmp/pal_chat_irc_palnet_peer.log
470: contained palnet_peer's own usage-error text and net/presence/ was empty
471: for both sessions. After the fix, presence files appeared with two
472: DISTINCT ports (9950/9951) and a real message posted from session A
473: appeared in session B's own net/inbox.txt within seconds.
474: 
475: CHECK YOUR OWN PROJECT: any orchestrator.c using a fixed-arg launch()/
476: launch_redirect() helper is only ever correct for children that need
477: <= that many args. If a call site's arg2 (or similar) contains embedded
478: spaces representing MULTIPLE logical tokens, that's the smell - it means
479: someone is passing a whole argv as one string to a function that can't
480: split it. grep every orchestrator.c in this family for this pattern.
481: 
482: FILES: 044.pal-chat-irc/system/orchestrator.c (launch_argv_redirect added,
483:        palnet_peer call site fixed)
484: 
485: =======================================================================
486: PITFALL 21: TESTING VIA DIRECT OP INVOCATION OR ISOLATED-NODE NETWORK
487: SIMULATION DOES NOT PROVE THE REAL LOCAL MULTI-SESSION UX WORKS
488: =======================================================================
489: DATE: 2026-07-26
490: SEVERITY: HIGH (false "PASS" verdict on a completely broken feature)
491: PROJECT: 044.pal-chat-irc (applies to any project with an orchestrator +
492: P2P daemon + shared local session state)
493: 
494: SYMPTOM: An agent tests P2P/multi-session chat by directly invoking
495: ops/+x/palnet_peer.+x and ops/+x/chat_inbox_watcher.+x by hand with
496: correctly-formed argv, across two INDEPENDENT project copies simulating
497: two separate machines. Everything passes - distinct ports, message
498: propagation, dedup, replay. A written test report says "SAFE TO
499: PROCEED." The user then runs the ACTUAL entry point real users use
500: (`./button.sh run` x2, same real project, real symlinked sessions) and
501: the feature is completely broken (see PITFALL 20).
502: 
503: ROOT CAUSE: Testing ops directly bypasses the orchestrator's own launch
504: code entirely - the exact code path PITFALL 20's bug lived in. A
505: synthetic two-independent-node test also can't be fooled by the
506: shared-symlinked-file false-positive described in PITFALL 20, so it
507: proved the network protocol logic works while proving NOTHING about
508: whether the real project ever actually launches that logic correctly
509: for a real local user. Two structurally different kinds of "the P2P
510: layer works" claims got conflated: "the ops, given correct args, do the
511: right thing" (true, and was genuinely tested) vs. "the real button.sh
512: run entry point actually launches those ops with correct args for a
513: real local multi-session user" (false, and was NOT tested until the
514: user pushed back and asked directly).
515: 
516: FIX / STANDING RULE: for any feature involving an orchestrator-launched
517: daemon (P2P, watchers, background tick processes), at least ONE test
518: pass MUST go through the actual user entry point (`./button.sh run`,
519: twice concurrently for multi-session features) before writing "SAFE TO
520: PROCEED" or "PASS" in a test report - not a headless op-level
521: substitute, no matter how thorough the op-level coverage is. Op-level
522: and isolated-node tests are still valuable (they prove the underlying
523: logic is sound and isolate the SPECIFIC bug faster once you know
524: something's wrong) but they are a SUPPLEMENT to, never a REPLACEMENT
525: for, one real end-to-end run through the actual launcher path.
526: 
527: RELATED, ALSO DISCOVERED THIS SESSION: don't hand-edit a state file
528: (current_layout.txt, chat_menu_state.txt, etc.) that a LIVE persistent
529: pal module also owns and actively re-syncs - the module's own next idle
530: loop tick will silently overwrite your edit back to whatever IT thinks
531: the state is (it never learned about the manual edit; it has no idea a
532: human/agent intervened). To move a live session into a given
533: screen/room/state for testing, drive it through REAL key injection
534: (pieces/keyboard/history.txt, [TIMESTAMP] KEY_PRESSED: <code> format per
535: _.0.aigent-testing-k3.txt) so the state change happens through the
536: module's own real code path, not around it.
537: 
538: ALSO OBSERVED THIS SESSION (worth a quick mention for future agents):
539: system/keyboard_input.c busy-spins near 100% CPU per session in a
540: headless/no-/dev/tty environment (API sandbox) rather than blocking
541: cleanly - two concurrent test sessions pegged ~93% CPU EACH. Kill test
542: sessions immediately after use in such environments, don't leave them
543: running "for later." Also: `./button.sh kill` / kill_all.sh did not
544: always complete in a single invocation under that CPU load in this
545: environment (observed exit code 144 twice) - always verify with a
546: `ps aux | grep` afterward rather than trusting the exit code alone; a
547: manual `kill -9 <pid>` follow-up closed the gap both times it happened.
548: 
549: FILES: 044.pal-chat-irc/system/keyboard_input.c (CPU-spin - ROOT-CAUSED
550:        AND FIXED, see PITFALL 22 below),
551:        044.pal-chat-irc/pieces/os/kill_all.sh (unreliable single-shot
552:        completion under CPU load, NOT fully root-caused - mitigated via
553:        an outer verify+retry pattern, see standing rule above)

555: =======================================================================
556: PITFALL 22: keyboard_input.c's read_key() busy-spins at ~100% CPU with
557: no /dev/tty (headless/API-sandbox testing) - ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED,
558: directory-wide (10 project copies)
559: =======================================================================
560: DATE: 2026-07-26
561: SEVERITY: HIGH (pegs a full CPU core per session indefinitely, no
562: natural ceiling - compounds fast with 2+ concurrent test sessions)
563: 
564: SYMPTOM: `./system/keyboard_input` shows ~93-100% CPU in `ps aux`,
565: indefinitely, for every headless/backgrounded test session launched via
566: `./button.sh run`. Confirmed live: two concurrent sessions each pegged
567: a separate core at ~99-100% for the full 3+ minutes they were left
568: running.
569: 
570: ROOT CAUSE (system/keyboard_input.c's read_key()):
571: 
572:   static int read_key(void) {
573:       char c;
574:       int nread;
575:       while ((nread = read(STDIN_FILENO, &c, 1)) != 1) {
576:           if (nread == -1 && errno != EAGAIN) return -1;
577:       }
578:       ...
579:   }
580: 
581:   enable_raw_mode() sets VMIN=0/VTIME=1 on stdin so a REAL terminal's
582:   read() blocks up to ~100ms before returning 0 when nothing's typed -
583:   the loop above is meant to be naturally throttled by that. But
584:   tcsetattr() SILENTLY FAILS (return value never checked) when stdin
585:   isn't a real tty - which is exactly what a headless/API-sandbox test
586:   run looks like (backgrounded via `&`, stdin redirected from
587:   /dev/null or a closed pipe). On a non-tty fd, read() returns 0
588:   INSTANTLY every single call, forever - the while loop's only
589:   condition for continuing IS "did I get exactly 1 byte", so it spins
590:   as fast as the CPU allows with zero blocking, zero sleep, zero upper
591:   bound.
592: 
593: FIX: add a short usleep() on the nread==0/retry path:
594: 
595:   while ((nread = read(STDIN_FILENO, &c, 1)) != 1) {
596:       if (nread == -1 && errno != EAGAIN) return -1;
597:       usleep(20000);  /* caps the spin; negligible cost on a real
598:                           terminal where this path is already rare and
599:                           already throttled by VTIME */
600:   }
601: 
602:   Verified live: CPU usage for system/keyboard_input dropped from
603:   ~99-100% to ~0.1% after rebuilding with this fix, same test session
604:   shape (button.sh run --pal, backgrounded, no tty).
605: 
606: DIRECTORY-WIDE SCOPE: this file is described in its OWN header comment
607: as a "SHARED OP" independently copied (not symlinked) into every
608: project in this family. Found the IDENTICAL unfixed pattern in 9 other
609: copies (014.wsr-pal, 041.pal-chain, 041.pal-forum, 045.muchi-pal-agent,
610: 01.muchi-pals, 101.lpns+map+4, 101.mutaclsym, 002.zoo, 0.user-pal) and
611: applied the SAME fix to all of them - do not assume fixing your one
612: project's copy is enough; grep the whole tree
613: (`find <root> -iname keyboard_input.c`) and check every hit.
614: INTERESTING PRECEDENT MISSED: 101.ledger-player-npc-simple+3's own copy
615: ALREADY had a bare `usleep(10000)` here with no comment - meaning this
616: exact bug was found and partially fixed once before, in one project,
617: and never propagated or written down anywhere until now. Write pitfalls
618: down BEFORE fixes get lost in a single project's diff.
619: 
620: STANDING RULE: when testing ANY of these projects headlessly (no real
621: terminal), assume this fix needs to be in place, or that keyboard_input
622: itself should simply be skipped/killed immediately after launch if the
623: project's own binary predates this fix and can't be rebuilt - check
624: `ps aux` CPU% for it within a few seconds of any headless launch and
625: kill/rebuild rather than letting it run.
626: 
627: FILES: system/keyboard_input.c in all 11 copies across the muchiverse
628:        tree (see DIRECTORY-WIDE SCOPE above for the full list)
629: 
630: =======================================================================
631: PITFALL 23: parse_attributes() 1-byte heap-buffer-overflow -> silent
632: malloc corruption -> chtpm_parser_pal dies a few frames after startup
633: (directory-wide, all 11 copies)
634: =======================================================================
635: DATE: 2026-07-26
636: SEVERITY: CRITICAL (silently kills the whole UI stack; was previously
637: misdiagnosed as an orchestrator/palnet_peer "hang")
638: PROJECT FOUND: 041.pal-chain (root-caused via AddressSanitizer)
639: 
640: SYMPTOM: `./button.sh run` appears to "hang" - keyboard_input blocks in
641: the foreground as expected (see PITFALL 21's headless-testing note),
642: but chtpm_parser_pal ALSO silently disappears from `ps aux` within a
643: couple seconds of startup, so nothing ever renders. A prior session's
644: own smoke test reported this as an unexplained hang and gave up. It is
645: NOT a hang - the parser crashes.
646: 
647: ROOT CAUSE (system/chtpm_parser_pal.c, parse_attributes(), scanning an
648: attribute name):
649: 
650:   char* name_start = pos; while (*pos && *pos != '=' && !isspace(*pos)) pos++;
651:   char saved = *pos; *pos = '\0'; while (*(++pos) && isspace(*pos));
652: 
653: When the attribute name runs all the way to the end of the strdup'd
654: attribute string (saved == '\0' - i.e. pos was ALREADY at the buffer's
655: own null terminator), `while (*(++pos) && ...)` unconditionally
656: increments pos PAST that terminator BEFORE ever checking it - a 1-byte
657: READ past the end of the heap allocation. Confirmed via AddressSanitizer:
658: 
659:   ERROR: AddressSanitizer: heap-buffer-overflow ... READ of size 1
660:   #0 parse_attributes system/chtpm_parser_pal.c:2202
661:   #1 parse_chtm system/chtpm_parser_pal.c:2474
662:   0x... is located 0 bytes to the right of 508-byte region
663:   allocated by strdup() at system/chtpm_parser_pal.c:2197
664: 
665: That single out-of-bounds read corrupts glibc's own malloc bookkeeping
666: for the adjacent heap chunk (undefined behavior, not merely a harmless
667: stray read) - the NEXT malloc/free call downstream then aborts with:
668: 
669:   malloc(): invalid next size (unsorted)
670: 
671: ...which is why the crash appears to happen "a few frames later" with
672: no obvious connection to the actual bug, and why it looked like a hang
673: from the outside (no core dump inspected, no ASan run) rather than a
674: crash.
675: 
676: FIX: only advance past the terminator if there's a real character to
677: skip:
678: 
679:   char saved = *pos; *pos = '\0';
680:   if (saved) { pos++; while (*pos && isspace((unsigned char)*pos)) pos++; }
681: 
682: VERIFIED LIVE: before the fix, ASan caught the overflow every run and
683: the plain (non-ASan) binary aborted with the malloc corruption message
684: within ~1-2 seconds of `./button.sh run`. After the fix: full daemon
685: stack (orchestrator, renderer, chtpm_parser_pal, keyboard_input,
686: prisc+x, palnet_peer) launched and stayed alive/stable for 10+ seconds
687: straight with real frame content rendering, verified via direct process
688: inspection, not just a log message.
689: 
690: DIRECTORY-WIDE SCOPE: chtpm_parser_pal.c is a "SHARED OP" independently
691: copied (not symlinked) into 11 projects across this tree - found and
692: fixed the IDENTICAL bug in ALL of them (including 044.pal-chat-irc's
693: own copy, which had simply never triggered it - a dormant time bomb
694: depending on the exact shape of whatever CHTPM layout XML a given
695: project happens to load first). grep every copy for
696: `while (\*(\+\+pos) && isspace` before trusting any of them are safe.
697: 
698: DEBUGGING METHOD WORTH REMEMBERING: when a persistent daemon
699: mysteriously "disappears" from `ps aux` shortly after launch with no
700: obvious cause, don't assume a hang or an orchestrator/launch-arg bug
701: (PITFALL 20's class) without checking first - recompile the exact
702: binary with `-fsanitize=address -g` and run it directly (bypass the
703: orchestrator's fork/exec, run it standalone against a hand-built
704: session directory with correct symlinks) to get an exact file:line
705: crash location in seconds, instead of guessing from process-list
706: absence or grepping for suspicious code. A plain (non-ASan) run's own
707: "malloc(): invalid next size" message just means "somewhere upstream
708: overflowed a heap buffer" - it points at neither the actual overflow
709: site nor even reliably at the correct function.
710: 
711: ALSO WORTH REMEMBERING: when checking whether a project's own daemons
712: are alive via `ps aux | grep`, match on the process/binary name alone
713: (`system/renderer`, `ops/\+x/palnet_peer`, etc.), NEVER require the
714: project's own absolute path as part of the same match - children
715: launched via a relative execv() path (`execv("./system/renderer", ...)`
716: from a cwd-changed parent) show up in `ps aux` as `./system/renderer`
717: with NO absolute path prefix at all. A grep requiring both produces a
718: false "nothing is running" reading even when the full stack is alive
719: and healthy - this exact mistake was made twice this session (once in
720: testing/test_multiuser_p2p.sh's cleanup check, once while manually
721: investigating this very pitfall) before being caught both times by an
722: independent broader recheck.
723: 
724: FILES: system/chtpm_parser_pal.c in all 11 copies across the muchiverse
725:        tree (014.wsr-pal, 041.pal-chain, 041.pal-forum, 045.muchi-pal-
726:        agent, 01.muchi-pals, 044.pal-chat-irc, 101.lpns+map+4,
727:        101.mutaclsym, 101.ledger-player-npc-simple, 002.zoo,
728:        0.user-pal/00.login-signup)
729: 
730:

737: WRONG: a single <scroller> strip like `SKIN: 👨 [👨🏻] 👨🏼 …` with a/d (or
738: INTERACT lock) to cycle. User tries to "select" a skin and either
739: navigates away (w/s/digits swallowed or strip steals focus) or has no
740: per-option index to jump to. Deviated from std chtpm menu pattern.
741: 
742: RIGHT (std): under a [+]/[-] fold, inject one <button> per option so
743: the parser assigns real navigable indexes:
744: 
745:   [ ] 7. [+] Skin  👨🏻
746:   (Enter open)
747:   [ ] 7. [-] Skin  👨🏻
748:   [>] 8. [👨🏻]          ← KEY:301 applies skin index 1
749:   [ ] 9. 👨🏼
750:   …
751: 
752: Implementation notes for avatar-creation:
753:   - compose writes dna_opts_skin=… markup into gui_state (lazy XML sub)
754:   - each button onClick="KEY:300+idx" (gender 310+, hair 320+, …)
755:   - avatar_menu_input maps those key codes → cycle_dna.+x <uuid> <field> <idx>
756:   - fold [+]/[-] only hides/shows children; no ACTIVATE trap, no a/d strip
757: 
758: ALSO: do not put onClick="ACTIVATE" on DNA folds if children need global
759: numbered nav — ACTIVATE scopes navigability and INTERACT-on-child was
760: broken via send_command("INTERACT") (not a real command verb).
761: 
762: FILES: ops/avatar_compose_frame.c, ops/avatar_menu_input.c,
763:        pieces/chtpm/layouts/avatar_manage.chtpm, customize.chtpm
