1094:      to take a piece_id argument (or resolve it from a real
1095:      `active_target_id` field) instead, matching real
1096:      `move_entity.+x <piece_id> <dir>`'s own shape.
1097:   2. Give xlector a real, standalone piece (own directory/state.txt) -
1098:      mutaclsym's current `xlector_pos_x`/`xlector_pos_y` fields bolted
1099:      onto hero/state.txt are a deliberate v1 simplification (see
1100:      ops/move_player.c's own header comment) that stops being
1101:      sufficient once xlector needs to exist independently of any one
1102:      possessable entity.
1103:    3. Port the real "shadow xlector sync" (xlector's own position
1104:       follows whatever's currently possessed) and the unconditional
1105:       `'9'`/ESC-returns-to-xlector precedence check.
1106:    4. Every OTHER possessable piece needs its own `possessable` STATE
1107:       field (or relies on the same default-possessable-if-absent rule) -
1108:       audit this explicitly once a second controllable piece exists,
1109:       don't assume the default silently does the right thing.
1110: 
1111:  ========================================================================
1112:  14a. KEY 9 / ESC: UN-POSSESS AND EXIT INTERACT
1113:  ========================================================================
1114: 
1115:  REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT (mutaclsym, 2026-07-25): Key '9' was supposed to
1116:  be the explicit un-possess key (matching xyzos-standards.txt §14's own
1117:  "'9'/ESC/joystick button 8, checked FIRST, unconditionally" rule) but
1118:  the handler was lost during a refactor — key '9' fell through as an
1119:  unused digit in choice.c's is_digit branch, doing nothing.
1120: 
1121:  KEY 9: BIDIRECTIONAL TOGGLE (possess ↔ un-possess).
1122:    - Interact mode (interact_mode==1): snap cursor TO hero position
1123:      (xlector → hero, NOT hero → xlector), then auto-possess (exit
1124:      interact). The cursor re-centers on the player before taking
1125:      control, so you always possess from where the hero is.
1126:    - Possess mode (interact_mode==0): un-possess → enter interact mode.
1127:      Cursor appears at hero's current position.
1128: 
1129:  ESC: EXIT INTERACT INSTANTLY. Does nothing in possess mode.
1130:    - Interact mode (interact_mode==1): exit interact immediately,
1131:      back to nav. Cursor disappears.
1132:    - Possess mode (interact_mode==0): no-op. Entity stays possessed.
1133:      If player re-enters interact later (via key 9), cursor appears
1134:      at hero's position with possession still active.
1135: 
1136:  KEY 9 vs ESC:
1137:    - Key 9 toggles possess ↔ interact. Always works.
1138:    - ESC only exits interact mode. Does nothing when possessing.
1139:    - Neither key possesses directly (key 9 does via snap+possess,
1140:      but only from interact mode).
1141: 
1142:  FILES: ops/choice.c
1143: 
1144: ========================================================================
1145: 15. pieces/keyboard/history.txt MUST BE CLEARED EVERY LAUNCH - IT IS
1146:     NOT PROJECT-SPECIFIC, DO NOT SKIP IT WHEN CLEANING UP A PROJECT'S
1147:     OWN button.sh
1148: ========================================================================
1149: 
1150: REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT (mutaclsym, 2026-07-18): `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s
1151: own main loop reads `pieces/keyboard/history.txt` from byte 0 on every
1152: fresh launch (matching real `chtpm_parser.c` exactly - confirmed via
1153: direct read, `last_history_position` starts at 0, never seeks to EOF at
1154: startup). This is the universal `keyboard_input.c` <-> `chtpm_parser_pal`
1155: bridge file (the `KEY_PRESSED: N` line format, `append_key()`'s own
1156: "CHTPM-BRIDGE ADDITION") - it exists in EVERY project using
1157: `chtpm_parser_pal`, regardless of whether that project has a map,
1158: regardless of the `<interact>` target. It is unrelated to the
1159: map-vs-menu `interact_relay.txt` question (sec. 7/16 below) entirely.
1160: 
1161: THE MISTAKE THAT CAUSED THIS: an earlier pass, correctly identifying
1162: that `pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt` was being
1163: over-applied to menu-only (no-map) projects (copied wholesale from
1164: mutaclsym without checking whether it was actually needed - see sec. 16
1165: below), ALSO deleted the `mkdir -p pieces/keyboard` + `: >
1166: pieces/keyboard/history.txt` lines from those same projects' `button.sh`
1167: in the same edit - conflating "this file's CONTENTS aren't
1168: project-specific" with "this file isn't needed." Result: stale
1169: `KEY_PRESSED: N` lines from a previous session sat in the file, and the
1170: very next launch replayed them immediately on startup (confirmed live:
1171: old movement/quit keys fired before the player ever touched anything).
1172: 
1173: THE RULE: every project's own `button.sh` `run`/`chtpm` action must
1174: include, unconditionally, regardless of whether that project has a map:
1175: ```
1176: mkdir -p pieces/keyboard
1177: : > pieces/keyboard/history.txt
1178: ```
1179: Do not remove this when cleaning up a project's interact-mode setup -
1180: it is orthogonal to whatever `<interact src>` the project's own layout
1181: uses.
1182: 
1183: ========================================================================
1184: 16. INTERACT MODE IS ONLY FOR CONTINUOUS, FREE-FORM KEY RELAY (A REAL
1185:     MAP) - A PURE DISCRETE MENU (NO MAP) SHOULD NEVER USE onClick=
1186:     "INTERACT" AT ALL, USE ${piece_methods} INSTEAD (see sec. 12)
1187: ========================================================================
1188: 
1189: REAL MISTAKE, CORRECTED (egg-pals/wsr-pal, 2026-07-18): an earlier pass
1190: copied mutaclsym's own `<button label="Control Hero" onClick="INTERACT"
1191: />` gate pattern wholesale into egg-pals ("Control Pets") and wsr-pal
1192: ("Control Sim") - projects with NO map/movement concept at all, purely
1193: digit-menu-driven (Feed/Clean/Sleep, Buy/Sell/Financing, etc). This was
1194: overzealous copy-paste, not a considered design choice, and produced a
1195: real, user-visible symptom: a needless manual "engage" step before ANY
1196: menu option could be selected, for a project where every action is
1197: already a single discrete choice - INTERACT's entire reason to exist
1198: (let raw keys stream continuously to a live game loop, bypassing
1199: chtpm's own nav, for map/cursor movement - sec. 7) simply does not
1200: apply.
1201: 
1202: THE FIX, confirmed against real `chtpm_parser.c`'s own `process_key()`
1203: (pieces/chtpm/plugins/chtpm_parser.c ~2589-2880): nav-mode button clicks
1204: (`active_index == -1`, arrows/digits move `focus_index`, Enter fires
1205: `send_command(el->onClick)`) ALREADY relay through to the module via
1206: `inject_raw_key()` -> whatever `<interact src>` names, with ZERO need
1207: for `active_index` to ever leave -1. INTERACT (`active_index` set to the
1208: button's own index, gating ALL subsequent keys into the module
1209: regardless of nav) is a SEPARATE, ADDITIONAL mechanism needed ONLY when
1210: raw keys must stream continuously without a discrete per-key dispatch -
1211: i.e., real-time map/cursor movement. A pure menu needs NONE of that:
1212: remove the INTERACT button/gate entirely, and drive 100% of the menu
1213: via `${piece_methods}` (sec. 12) - real fuzz-op's own layout proves the
1214: combination (piece_methods for discrete pet actions, a SEPARATE "Control
1215: Map" INTERACT button ONLY for actual map movement) is the intended,
1216: proven shape - never piece_methods OR interact alone as a blanket
1217: default; pick per-mechanism based on whether continuous relay is
1218: genuinely needed.
1219: 
1220: 16.1 `${piece_methods}` REQUIRES project_id + active_target_id SEEDED
1221:      BEFORE THE FIRST FRAME, NOT JUST ON THE FIRST KEYPRESS
1222: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1223: REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT (wsr-pal): `load_dynamic_methods()` resolves via
1224: `get_var("project_id")` + `get_var("active_target_id")`, both loaded
1225: from `pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt`. A project's own menu-input op
1226: (this family's `wsr_menu_input.c`-shaped ops) naturally only writes
1227: these fields AFTER processing a keypress - but chtpm renders its FIRST
1228: frame BEFORE any key has ever been pressed. `button.sh`'s own initial
1229: `state.txt` write (alongside `module_path=...`) must ALSO seed
1230: `project_id=<project>` and `active_target_id=<default_screen_piece_id>`
1231: directly, or `${piece_methods}` renders empty on first launch.
1232: 
1233: 16.2 KEY:n ENCODING BREAKS ABOVE 9 ITEMS - THE RECEIVING OP MUST TREAT
1234:      EVERY INCOMING VALUE AS AN ALREADY-RESOLVED SELECTION, NEVER
1235:      RE-ACCUMULATE IT
1236: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1237: REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT (wsr-pal, 32-item menu): `send_command()`'s own
1238: `KEY:` handling (confirmed via direct read, both real `chtpm_parser.c`
1239: and the `chtpm_parser_pal.c` fork) is `if (k >= 0 && k <= 9)
1240: inject_raw_key('0' + k); else inject_raw_key(k);` - indices 1-9 get
1241: ASCII-digit-encoded (so they look like an ordinary typed keystroke to
1242: older/simpler receivers), but 10+ is written as the BARE INTEGER, since
1243: no single ASCII byte represents two digits. `inject_raw_key()` writes
1244: that value verbatim (`"%d\n"`) to whatever `<interact src>` names either
1245: way. This is NOT a partial keystroke to re-accumulate - chtpm's own
1246: nav-mode `digit_accum` (typing "1" then "3" moves ITS OWN focus to item
1247: 13) ALREADY resolved the full multi-digit selection, entirely inside
1248: `chtpm_parser_pal.c`, before Enter ever fired `send_command("KEY:13")`
1249: as ONE event. A receiving op with its own pre-existing digit-accumulator
1250: (built for raw multi-keystroke terminal typing, predating this project's
1251: own chtpm conversion) will mishandle this: indices 1-9 happen to still
1252: look like a normal ASCII digit (harmless), but 10+ either falls through
1253: every check silently, or - worse - the specific values 10 and 13
1254: collide with real ASCII LF/CR and misfire as a stale Enter.
1255: 
1256: THE FIX: collapse any such op's own digit-accumulation + separate-Enter
1257: dispatch into ONE immediate-dispatch branch: `resolved_item = (key >=
1258: '0' && key <= '9') ? key - '0' : (key > 9 && key < 1000) ? key : 0;`
1259: then dispatch immediately if `1 <= resolved_item <= item_count` - no
1260: accumulator, no waiting for a second event, since chtpm never sends one
1261: for the same selection. (Upper bound `< 1000` avoids colliding with this
1262: family's own `ARROW_UP`/`ARROW_DOWN` = 1002/1003 codes, which arrive
1263: separately and should stay handled by whatever `is_up`/`is_down` check
1264: already exists.) This makes the true ceiling on `${piece_methods}` menu
1265: size whatever `MAX_MENU_ITEMS`/buffer limits the layout and receiving op
1266: otherwise allow - not 9.
1267: 
1268: 16.2a A SECOND, SEPARATE OFF-BY-ONE: `load_dynamic_methods()`'s OWN
1269:       `method_idx` STARTS AT 2, NOT 1 - CHECK THIS EXPLICITLY WHENEVER
1270:       ${piece_methods} IS THE *ONLY* CONTENT IN A LAYOUT (NO HAND-
1271:       AUTHORED BUTTON BEFORE IT)
1272: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1273: REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT (wsr-pal, confirmed via direct key injection per
1274: sec. 9.3's own test method - not guessed): `load_dynamic_methods()`
1275: (`shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c` ~line 992) initializes `method_idx = 2`
1276: for any non-"loader" `active_id` - it assumes nav slot 1 is ALWAYS
1277: already occupied by a hand-authored button before `${piece_methods}`
1278: (true for mutaclsym's own "Control Hero" - sec. 12's own worked example
1279: places it first in the layout, `${piece_methods}` after). Once sec. 16's
1280: own fix removes an unnecessary INTERACT gate/button entirely (a pure
1281: menu with nothing before `${piece_methods}`), this hardcoded offset is
1282: now WRONG for that layout: every generated `onClick="KEY:n"` is exactly
1283: ONE HIGHER than the piece.pdl's own 1-based METHOD row order. Confirmed
1284: live: the visually-13th menu row ("Financing") actually generates and
1285: dispatches `KEY:14`, silently selecting row 14 ("Private", an unrelated
1286: no-op) instead - the screen never appeared to navigate anywhere,
1287: looking exactly like a dead button, not an off-by-one.
1288: 
1289: THE FIX: when `${piece_methods}` is the ONLY navigable content in a
1290: layout (no other button occupies slot 1), the receiving op's own
1291: `resolved_item` computation (16.2 above) must subtract 1 uniformly:
1292: `resolved_item = (key >= '0' && key <= '9') ? (key - '0') - 1 : (key >
1293: 9 && key < 1000) ? key - 1 : 0;`. Both the ASCII-digit and raw-integer
1294: forms carry the IDENTICAL +1 offset (it comes from `method_idx`, not
1295: from either encoding), so the same `- 1` applies to both branches
1296: uniformly. Do NOT assume this offset only affects large menus (>9) -
1297: verify item 1 dispatches as expected too, since the shift applies to
1298: every row equally.
1299: 
1300: VERIFY THIS EXPLICITLY, DON'T ASSUME, whenever wiring a NEW project
1301: onto `${piece_methods}`: if the layout has a hand-authored button
1302: before it (mutaclsym's shape), `method_idx=2`'s start is already
1303: correct, no compensation needed. If `${piece_methods}` is the first and
1304: only thing (a pure-menu project with the INTERACT gate removed per
1305: sec. 16), the receiving op MUST compensate by 1, or every selection
1306: silently fires the WRONG menu row - a symptom (looks like a dead/no-op
1307: button) easy to misdiagnose as something else entirely.
1308: 
1309: 16.4 PERSISTED MENU-NAVIGATION STATE MUST BE RESET ON EVERY LAUNCH,
1310:      NEVER JUST ON A SEPARATE "new-game"-STYLE ACTION - A CLASS OF BUG,
1311:      CHECK EVERY FIELD, NOT JUST THE ONE THAT GOT REPORTED
1312: ========================================================================
1313: REAL BUG, USER-REPORTED (wsr-pal, "starts from last open menu ... make
1314: sure this never happens again for any reason"): a project's own
1315: menu-input op (`wsr_menu_input.c`-shaped) persists its CURRENT screen
1316: (`active_menu_piece`) and nav position (`cursor`, `digit_accum`) to a
1317: state file that ALSO holds real game progress (`turn_number`, `cash`,
1318: `ticker_on`, `active_corp_index`) - by necessary design, since quitting
1319: mid-session must not lose real progress. But `button.sh`'s own "run"
1320: action only ever reset `pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt`/
1321: `interact_relay.txt`/`quit_flag.txt`/`gl_focus.lock` (all SESSION-
1322: mechanics files) - it never touched THIS file at all. Whatever screen
1323: was showing at the exact moment of the LAST quit (via a working Back
1324: button, a direct 'q', a crash, anything) is exactly where the NEXT
1325: launch starts too, because "which screen" and "session progress" were
1326: never actually treated as separate concerns in the reset logic, only in
1327: the data model.
1328: 
1329: THE FIX: `button.sh`'s "run" action must reset EVERY navigation-position
1330: field in this file (`active_menu_piece`, `cursor`, `digit_accum`,
1331: `prompt_active` if present) to their fresh-launch defaults, via a
1332: targeted `sed -i 's/^field=.*/field=default/'` per field - and must NOT
1333: touch any REAL progress field (`turn_number`, `cash`, `ticker_on`,
1334: `active_corp_index`, anything else representing actual game state) -
1335: those must survive a restart, matching what the project's own separate
1336: "new-game" action already does correctly for `active_menu_piece` alone
1337: (a real, working precedent this fix generalizes, not invents).
1338: 
1339: THE STANDING RULE, general to any future project built this way: when a
1340: single state file mixes "where am I" (navigation/UI position) with
1341: "what happened" (real progress), the LAUNCH reset must enumerate and
1342: reset every navigation field explicitly - do not assume resetting one
1343: field (the one a bug report happened to name) covers the class. Audit
1344: the WHOLE file's own field list against "is this navigation-position, or
1345: real progress" every time a new field is added to it, and add it to the
1346: launch-reset list if it's the former - a silently-added new nav field
1347: later reintroduces this exact bug for that field specifically, even
1348: after this fix.
1349: 
1350: ========================================================================
1351: 17. system/renderer.c / chtpm_rgb_render.c MUST WATCH
1352:     renderer_pulse.txt - THE MARKER chtpm_parser_pal.c'S OWN
1353:     compose_frame() ALREADY GROWS UNCONDITIONALLY - NOT
1354:     state_changed.txt (CORRECTED ENTRY - the original version of this
1355:     section recommended the wrong marker, causing a NEW regression;
1356:     see the correction below)
1357: ========================================================================
1358: REAL BUG, USER-REPORTED (wsr-pal, "had to press Enter twice" - a screen
1359: transition looked blank/stale until an UNRELATED next keypress fixed
1360: it): `system/renderer.c` (project-local per sec. 1c) polls exactly ONE
1361: marker, `pieces/display/frame_changed.txt`, and only re-reads/re-prints
1362: `current_frame.txt` when THAT file's size grows. `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s
1363: own `process_key()` grows this marker on every KEYPRESS-driven
1364: recompose - but a module-driven recompose (chtpm noticing
1365: `active_target_id` changed via a relayed `GOTO:` command, reloading on
1366: its own `state_changed.txt`-triggered path, sec. 16.1/16.2 - NOT
1367: synchronous with the keypress that triggered it) does NOT also grow
1368: `frame_changed.txt`. Confirmed via direct testing (sec. 9.3's own
1369: method): the CORRECT, fully-populated `current_frame.txt` was already
1370: sitting on disk the whole time this looked "stuck blank" -
1371: `system/renderer.c` simply never knew to re-read it.
1372: 
1373: THE WRONG FIX THIS SECTION ORIGINALLY RECOMMENDED: also watch
1374: `pieces/apps/player_app/state_changed.txt`. This "fixed" the stale-
1375: submenu symptom but caused a DIFFERENT, real regression, found live in
1376: mutaclsym after sec. 0's own synchronous-dispatch fix: a brief BLACK
1377: FLASH on every INTERACT-mode keypress. Root cause: `state_changed.txt`
1378: is grown by a PROJECT'S OWN compose op BEFORE chtpm has actually
1379: reloaded/recomposed anything - it is a pre-trigger, not a completion
1380: signal. For a project whose own module ALSO writes `current_frame.txt`
1381: directly as part of the same op (mutaclsym's own `compose_frame.c`,
1382: sec. 0.1/16.3's own dual-write pattern - correct and needed there),
1383: that op grows `state_changed.txt` too - so watching it here caught the
1384: module's own INTERMEDIATE, chrome-less write and rendered/displayed
1385: THAT, before chtpm's own subsequent correction landed. Under the OLD,
1386: asynchronous-module architecture this race window happened to be wide
1387: enough to rarely matter for mutaclsym; sec. 0's own synchronous-
1388: dispatch fix made the two writes happen close enough together in real
1389: time that this renderer (now polling both markers at ~16ms) reliably
1390: caught the intermediate one and displayed it as a visible flash.
1391: 
1392: THE REAL FIX: `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s own internal `compose_frame()`
1393: (shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c ~line 2575 - NOT a project's own
1394: `ops/compose_frame.c`, a same-named but different function inside the
1395: parser itself) grows `pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt`
1396: UNCONDITIONALLY, every single time it runs - regardless of whether a
1397: keypress or a `state_changed.txt`-triggered reload caused it. This is
1398: the authoritative "the CORRECT, chrome-composed `current_frame.txt` is
1399: ready" signal chtpm itself already emits, and it was sitting unused the
1400: whole time. Watch THIS instead of `state_changed.txt`: it never fires
1401: early, and it never fires for a project's own intermediate/direct write
1402: - only for chtpm's own finished composition, regardless of what
1403: triggered it. The existing settle-loop (current_frame.txt's own size
1404: stable across 2 consecutive checks, sec. 11.3's pattern) still applies
1405: identically on top of this marker.
1406: 
1407: APPLIES TO ANY PROJECT with a module that can change
1408: `active_target_id`/trigger a `${piece_methods}` reload mid-session, AND
1409: to any GL/RGB rendering daemon reading the same `current_frame.txt`
1410: (`shared-ops/chtpm_rgb_render.c` got the identical fix) - audit for
1411: this single-marker gap, but use `renderer_pulse.txt`, never
1412: `state_changed.txt`, as the fix.
1413: 
1414: 16.3 DUAL-WRITING current_frame.txt + view.txt IS CORRECT AND NEEDED -
1415:      THE REAL RULE IS "DON'T LET THE TWO OUTPUTS VISUALLY CONFLICT,"
1416:      NOT "ONLY CHTPM MAY EVER WRITE current_frame.txt" (CORRECTED
1417:      ENTRY - the original version of this section, written same-day,
1418:      had the wrong fix)
1419: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1420: REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT (wsr-pal): a project's own compose op (called
1421: every tick from `main_loop_chtpm.pal`'s own loop) writing
1422: `pieces/display/current_frame.txt` DIRECTLY, in addition to
1423: `pieces/apps/player_app/view.txt`, produced visible flicker/duplicate-
1424: menu artifacts - `${piece_methods}` buttons and the op's own old
1425: hand-drawn text menu both appeared, alternating.
1426: 
1427: THE WRONG FIX THIS SECTION ORIGINALLY RECOMMENDED: stop writing
1428: `current_frame.txt` from the compose op entirely, relying solely on
1429: `chtpm_parser_pal`'s own delayed, separate-process
1430: `state_changed.txt`-triggered reload to eventually pick up
1431: `view.txt`'s new content. This "fixed" the flicker but introduced a
1432: DIFFERENT real bug, also live-caught: a perceptible one-keypress INPUT
1433: LAG - the screen only reflects a keypress once chtpm's own loop
1434: notices the marker grew on its OWN poll cycle, not the instant the
1435: module's compose op finishes. Confirmed by direct comparison against
1436: mutaclsym's own `ops/compose_frame.c`, which shows NO such lag and
1437: ALSO writes `current_frame.txt` directly, on purpose (see that file's
1438: own header comment, "REAL BUG FIX ... this used to fopen(out_path)
1439: ... write ... re-open ... to READ ... that read-back is a real race
1440: ... Fix: build the whole frame in memory (open_memstream) ... write
1441: the SAME buffer to both current_frame.txt and view.txt directly,
1442: in-process, with no read-back and therefore no race window at all").
1443: 
1444: THE ACTUAL RULE: the flicker's real cause was never "two writers of
1445: current_frame.txt" - `chtpm_parser_pal` (a separate process) ALREADY
1446: writes that file on its own render cycle in EVERY project using this
1447: pattern, mutaclsym included, and always has. The real cause was this
1448: compose op's own output CONTENT visibly conflicting with chtpm's own
1449: composed frame (a hand-drawn text menu duplicate of what
1450: `${piece_methods}` already renders as real buttons). Writing the SAME
1451: data to both `current_frame.txt` and `view.txt` is not just safe, it's
1452: NEEDED - it's what makes fresh state visible immediately, without
1453: waiting on chtpm's own separate poll-and-reload cycle to catch up.
1454: 
1455: THE REAL, VERIFIED FIX: keep dual-writing both files from every render
1456: call (mutaclsym's own open_memstream-then-fwrite-twice shape, or an
1457: equivalent - e.g. two `FILE*` targets written in parallel from the
1458: same call sites, confirmed working). Just make sure whatever gets
1459: written is something chtpm's own LATER recompose (from the SAME
1460: underlying data, once its own marker check fires) will be consistent
1461: with - i.e., don't hand-draw a numbered menu list a `${piece_methods}`
1462: placeholder elsewhere in the layout already renders as real buttons.
1463: Status/banner/message content (turn count, cash, wallet, etc.) is safe
1464: to write to both outputs identically; menu-navigation UI is not, once
1465: chtpm owns rendering it natively.
1466: 
1467: ========================================================================
1468: 18. SWITCHING BETWEEN SCREENS MUST BE REAL `href`, ONE `.chtpm` FILE PER
1469:     SCREEN - NOT A CUSTOM "GOTO:" STRING THAT SWAPS WHICH piece.pdl A
1470:     SINGLE SHARED LAYOUT POINTS AT (DRIFT, CONFIRMED - wsr-pal)
1471: ========================================================================
1472: Think of it like HTML: each screen is its own page (its own `.chtpm`
1473: file), and a link between two screens is a real `<button href="...">`
1474: - NOT a piece of app state that tells one page to pretend to be a
1475: different page. Real chtpm/wraith-alpha has always worked this way -
1476: confirmed by directly reading real wraith-alpha's own
1477: `settings.chtpm`: `<button label="Back to Wraith Desktop"
1478: href="projects/wraith-alpha/layouts/alpha-shell.chtpm" />`. chtpm's own
1479: `process_key()` (`shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c`, the `strlen(el->href)
1480: > 0` branch) already handles the ENTIRE transition natively and
1481: synchronously - sets `current_layout`, calls `parse_chtm()`,
1482: `initialize_focus()`, `export_active_index()`, `compose_frame()` - zero
1483: custom state, zero extra C code, one proven code path shared by every
1484: screen transition in the whole family.
1485: 
1486: Section 12 (`${piece_methods}`) and this section are NOT the same
1487: concern and do not replace each other: §12 is about rendering ONE
1488: screen's OWN action list (a single piece's METHOD rows, still correct,
1489: still keep using it); this section is about moving to a DIFFERENT
1490: screen entirely. Confusing the two - building the multi-screen jump out
1491: of the same machinery meant for a single screen's own button list - is
1492: exactly the mistake below.
1493: 
1494: THE DRIFT, CONFIRMED (wsr-pal): every one of its 6 menu "screens"
1495: (`wsr_main_menu`, `wsr_trade_menu`, `wsr_management_menu`,
1496: `wsr_search_menu`, `wsr_derivatives_menu`, `wsr_financing_menu`) is
1497: really just ONE `.chtpm` file (`pieces/chtpm/layouts/wsr.chtpm`) with a
1498: single `${piece_methods}` placeholder. "Switching screens" means a
1499: METHOD row's command is the string `GOTO:<piece_id>`, hand-parsed by
1500: `wsr_menu_input.c`, which overwrites an `active_menu_piece` app-state
1501: variable so `${piece_methods}` starts reading a DIFFERENT piece's own
1502: `piece.pdl` on the next render - the same physical layout file,
1503: pretending to be six different pages. This is a hand-rolled
1504: reimplementation of exactly what `href` already does for free, built
1505: before this correction existed - the SAME category of unintentional
1506: architectural drift named in section 0 (this family's only deliberate
1507: deviation from classic chtpm is "use more pal," never "reinvent a
1508: mechanism chtpm already has natively").
1509: 
1510: CONCRETE DAMAGE THIS DRIFT CAUSED, VERIFIED (not theoretical): 3 of the
1511: 6 `piece.pdl` files (`wsr_management_menu`, `wsr_derivatives_menu`,
1512: `wsr_financing_menu`) were authored with a 4-field METHOD row
1513: (`METHOD | N | label | command`) while the other 3 use the real 3-field
1514: format (`METHOD | label | command`). BOTH parsers that read these rows
1515: (chtpm's own `load_dynamic_methods()` AND `wsr_menu_input.c`'s own
1516: `load_menu_items()`) split on exactly the first two `|` characters -
1517: confirmed by replicating the exact C split logic: on a 4-field row, the
1518: "command" field ends up as `"8.Back to main menu | GOTO:wsr_main_menu"`
1519: (the real label text plus a stray leftover pipe), which does not match
1520: `GOTO:`/`RUN:`/`STUB`/anything - so EVERY button on those 3 screens
1521: silently no-ops (shelled as a bogus literal command), not just Back.
1522: This is the direct, likely explanation for this session's own
1523: inconclusively-reproduced "Back doesn't work for wsr-pal" reports - a
1524: custom hand-rolled format with no schema, drifting out of sync with
1525: itself, exactly the kind of bug a proven native mechanism (a real
1526: `.chtpm` file per screen, hand-authored, no row-format to get wrong)
1527: does not have room for in the first place.
1528: 
1529: THE FIX (not yet done - named here so it isn't lost): split
1530: `wsr.chtpm` into one real `.chtpm` file per screen (mirroring each
1531: existing `piece.pdl`'s own content), with every `GOTO:<piece_id>` row
1532: becoming a real `<button label="..." href="pieces/chtpm/layouts/
1533: <piece_id>.chtpm" />` and every real action row becoming whatever
1534: `onClick` prefix `send_command()` already recognizes (`OP:`, etc. -
1535: see section 6's own reserved-prefix list). This retires
1536: `wsr_menu_input.c`'s entire `GOTO:` dispatch branch, the
1537: `active_menu_piece` app-state variable, and the piece.pdl-as-menu-
1538: source indirection for NAVIGATION specifically (a piece.pdl can still
1539: be kept as data for other purposes if still needed) - collapsing it
1540: into the same one proven code path every other screen transition in
1541: this family already uses.
1542: 
1543: ========================================================================
1544: 19. HREF NAVIGATION MUST KILL+REAP THE OLD PERSISTENT MODULE BEFORE
1545:     LAUNCHING A NEW ONE - A BARE current_module_pid RESET LEAKS A LIVE,
1546:     RACING DUPLICATE (REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT - pal-chain, 2026-07-19)
1547: ========================================================================
1548: Any project using §18's own real href+persistent-`<module>` pattern
1549: (this whole family's own norm now) has EVERY screen name the SAME
1550: `<module>` command (e.g. `system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal`) -
1551: that's the whole point, one persistent loop survives every screen
1552: switch via `launch_module()`'s own liveness check
1553: (`current_module_pid > 0`, restored earlier this session - see this
1554: file's own git history / HANDOFF docs for that fix).
1555: 
1556: THE BUG: `process_key()`'s own href branch (and the `LOAD_PROJECT:`
1557: handler, same shape) used to reset `current_module_pid = 0;` and
1558: `current_module_path[0] = '\0';` DIRECTLY, without ever checking
1559: whether that pid actually named a still-alive process, let alone
1560: killing it. Since every screen names the identical module command,
1561: `launch_module()`'s own liveness guard was blinded (it can only refuse
1562: a duplicate launch if it still remembers the old pid) and happily
1563: forked ANOTHER instance on every single href click - confirmed live via
1564: `ps aux` showing TWO `prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal` processes after
1565: exactly one navigation. Both instances then read the same
1566: `interact_relay.txt` and read-modify-write the same project state.txt
1567: concurrently, an unordered race that visibly corrupted state (a 7-field
1568: state.txt collapsed to 1 surviving field after one click).
1569: 
1570: THE FIX (`shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c`, already applied and re-synced
1571: to every consumer as of 2026-07-19): both call sites now route through
1572: `cleanup_module()` (a real kill+reap, already existed but was
1573: previously called only from `handle_sigint()` on process exit) instead
1574: of a bare variable reset. `cleanup_module()` itself also needed a fix -
1575: its own `waitpid(pid, NULL, WNOHANG)` returned immediately regardless
1576: of whether the just-SIGTERM'd process had actually exited yet, leaving
1577: a `<defunct>` zombie behind every href click even after the kill itself
1578: started working; changed to a blocking `waitpid(pid, NULL, 0)` (safe
1579: here - it's a bounded wait on a process this same code just signaled
1580: to shut down, not an open-ended wait on unrelated work).
1581: 
1582: VERIFICATION BAR: after any href click (or any code path that reassigns
1583: `current_module_path`), `ps aux | grep prisc+x` (or the project's own
1584: module binary name) must show EXACTLY ONE live process for that
1585: project, no `<defunct>` entries accumulating - confirmed live for
1586: pal-chain across a login -> signup -> wallet_main -> send_screen ->
1587: mining_status navigation chain.
1588: 
1589: ========================================================================
1590: 20. ONE VISIBLE FRAME WRITER RULE - AN OP MUST NEVER WRITE
1591:     pieces/display/current_frame.txt DIRECTLY; chtpm_parser_pal.c's OWN
1592:     compose_frame() IS THE SOLE WRITER (REAL, USER-REPORTED FLICKER,
1593:     FOUND+FIXED - pal-chain, 2026-07-19)
1594: ========================================================================
1595: Real 1.TPMOS precedent, ALREADY NAMED, not reinvented here: see
1596: `1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0102.0028/!.gem-flashlite--yolo/#.docs/^.pmo.ld-faq+8/
1597: PITFALLS_ACTIVE_2026-03-18.txt` #17 ("ONE WRITER RULE - current_frame.txt
1598: must have a single owner/writer... Dual writers cause flicker/
1599: corruption and nondeterministic behavior") and #36 ("ONE VISIBLE FRAME
1600: WRITER RULE... only one component should own the user-visible current
1601: frame... state files advance, visible frame lags, debugging becomes
1602: misleading"). Read that whole file before touching ANY render/compose
1603: op in this family - it is real, hard-won TPMOS institutional knowledge,
1604: not project-specific advice, and this section exists only to record
1605: where THIS family concretely violated it and how that was confirmed
1606: and fixed, not to replace reading the source doc.
1607: 
1608: THE VIOLATION: `wsr_compose_frame.c` (and, copied from it verbatim,
1609: pal-chain's own `chain_compose_frame.c`) wrote BOTH
1610: `pieces/display/current_frame.txt` AND `pieces/apps/player_app/view.txt`
1611: directly from the same in-memory buffer, justified by that file's own
1612: (WRONG, checked directly against `chtpm_parser_pal.c`) header comment:
1613: "writes BOTH... so a keypress's effect is visible immediately rather
1614: than waiting on chtpm's own separate poll cycle." There is no such lag
1615: to work around: `process_key()` already appends to
1616: `pieces/display/frame_changed.txt` on EVERY keystroke unconditionally,
1617: and the main loop's own dirty-marker check picks that up and calls the
1618: parser's OWN `compose_frame()` (which reads `view.txt` via
1619: `${game_map}` and writes `current_frame.txt` itself) on the very next
1620: loop iteration - the two writes were never actually racing to be
1621: "first," they were both writing the SAME file on every single
1622: keystroke, and a renderer polling that file could catch either one
1623: mid-transition. Confirmed live (user-reported): visible flicker while
1624: navigating/typing in pal-chain's real cli_io-driven login screen.
1625: 
1626: THE FIX: the op's own compose step (`chain_compose_frame.c`, and any
1627: future project's own equivalent) must write ONLY
1628: `pieces/apps/player_app/view.txt` (plus whatever real marker file it
1629: already pings to prompt a `load_vars()` reload - `state_changed.txt`
1630: here) and NEVER open `pieces/display/current_frame.txt` for writing at
1631: all. `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s own `compose_frame()` remains the sole
1632: writer of that file, exactly as pitfalls #17/#36 require. Any project
1633: still doing the dual-write (wsr-pal's own `wsr_compose_frame.c` has not
1634: been re-checked/fixed as of this writing - flagged here, not yet
1635: verified) should be treated as carrying the same live bug until
1636: confirmed otherwise.
1637: 
1638: ========================================================================
1639: 21. THE ACTIVE-TARGET INDIRECTION PATTERN - HOW A PER-INSTANCE PAL OP
1640:     (act on "whichever pet/piece is currently selected") WORKS AROUND
1641:     prisc+x HAVING NO ARGV-INTO-A-LAUNCHED-SCRIPT MECHANISM (muchi-pals,
1642:     2026-07-19, direct instruction: "this is a simple project, perfect
1643:     place to use pal as much as possible")
1644: ========================================================================

1131: =======================================================================
1132: PITFALL 53: Backgrounding `button.sh run` with a trailing `&` from an
1133:             interactive job-control shell SIGTTIN-stops the ENTIRE
1134:             daemon stack (GL included) the instant keyboard_input
1135:             touches the controlling terminal — silent, no error, looks
1136:             exactly like "GL never launched"
1137: =======================================================================
1138: DATE: 2026-07-29
1139: SEVERITY: HIGH (total, silent failure of the whole running stack —
1140:            easily misdiagnosed as a GL/DISPLAY/rendering bug, which is
1141:            exactly what happened this session before the real cause
1142:            was found)
1143: PROJECT: 102.editor-📄️00.00 (discovered while testing the PITFALL 52
1144:           fix; applies to EVERY project using this button.sh shape —
1145:           file-menu included — not something the GL/RGB work
1146:           introduced)
1147: 
1148: SYMPTOM: User ran `./button.sh run &` (backgrounded from their own
1149: interactive terminal) expecting the same GL window that had just been
1150: verified working moments earlier via an agent-run background test. No
1151: GL window appeared at all. `ps aux` on that exact process group showed
1152: EVERY daemon in the stack (system/renderer, system/chtpm_parser_pal,
1153: system/chtpm_rgb_render, system/prisc+x) in STATE T (stopped) — not
1154: running, not crashed, not exited: paused mid-execution, indefinitely,
1155: consuming zero CPU, with no error output anywhere because nothing
1156: actually failed — the kernel suspended the whole group.
1157: 
1158: ROOT CAUSE: every button.sh run action in this family ends with a
1159: FOREGROUND, blocking call: `./system/keyboard_input`. That binary
1160: calls tcgetattr()/tcsetattr() directly on STDIN_FILENO to enter raw
1161: terminal mode - real, direct controlling-terminal I/O. When the WHOLE
1162: button.sh invocation is backgrounded with a trailing `&` in a shell
1163: with normal job control enabled, that shell's own job-control layer is
1164: still watching the terminal on behalf of ITS foreground job (the shell
1165: prompt itself). The instant a BACKGROUND job's process attempts to
1166: read from (or, depending on shell/tty settings, write to) the
1167: controlling terminal, the kernel's own tty line discipline sends
1168: SIGTTIN (or SIGTTOU) to that process'S ENTIRE PROCESS GROUP - not just
1169: keyboard_input, everything forked earlier in the same button.sh run
1170: that hasn't since called setsid()/setpgid() to detach - the ASCII
1171: renderer, chtpm_parser_pal, chtpm_rgb_render, and prisc+x/PAL loop, all
1172: stopped together, at whatever point each happened to be in mid-loop.
1173: gl_mirror's own absence from that stopped-process list (rather than
1174: also showing up stopped) suggests the group-stop can land before or
1175: around the GL launch point depending on exact timing - either way the
1176: net effect for a user watching the screen is identical: no window,
1177: zero error, the process silently going nowhere the moment `&` was
1178: used.
1179: 
1180: This is NOT a bug introduced by wiring GL into editor (PITFALL 52's own
1181: work) - &.widgits/file-menu/button.sh's `run` action ends in the exact
1182: same foreground `./system/keyboard_input` call and would exhibit the
1183: identical stop if launched the same way. It surfaced here because this
1184: was the first time in this session someone actually tried `&`-
1185: backgrounding one of these launchers from a real interactive shell
1186: while also expecting its GL window to appear.
1187: 
1188: FIX (workaround, not a code change - see STANDING RULE): do not
1189: background `button.sh run` (or run-app/run-widget) with a trailing `&`
1190: from an interactive shell that has job control enabled. Run it in the
1191: foreground directly; the GL window (a separate X11 client/process) and
1192: the terminal-blocking keyboard_input coexist fine as long as the whole
1193: invocation itself stays foreground in its own shell.
1194: 
1195: If a TRUE background/headless launch is genuinely needed (agent
1196: testing, CI, a supervised daemon), the correct fix is on the launcher
1197: side, not the caller side: either (a) redirect keyboard_input's own
1198: stdin from something that isn't the controlling tty (`</dev/null` or
1199: a pty allocated for that purpose) so it never attempts real terminal
1200: I/O in the first place, or (b) have the backgrounding shell explicitly
1201: disown the job / run it via `setsid ./button.sh run &` so the new
1202: process group has no controlling terminal to trap against at all. This
1203: handoff/pitfall does NOT implement either of those - flagging the
1204: mechanism for whoever needs a genuine headless-launch mode next,
1205: rather than guessing at a fix nobody asked for yet.
1206: 
1207: VERIFIED LIVE: same `./button.sh run` command, same session, no `&` -
1208: ran in the plain foreground - GL window appeared correctly, confirmed
1209: directly by the user looking at their own screen (not just file/
1210: checksum evidence, matching the STANDING RULE PITFALL 52 itself just
1211: established: a human has to actually look).
1212: 
1213: STANDING RULE: when a headless/agent-driven test genuinely needs a
1214: project's daemon stack running unattended (the k3/§36.6 black-box
1215: testing style), do NOT reach for `./button.sh run &` as the obvious
1216: option - it is the one invocation shape most likely to silently stop
1217: itself in exactly this way the moment keyboard_input executes. Prefer
1218: launching the individual pipeline binaries directly (as fm-widget-
1219: fix.md's own "Standalone rendering test" procedure already does:
1220: compose -> chtpm_parser_pal -> chtpm_rgb_render -> gl_mirror, each
1221: started explicitly, keyboard_input omitted entirely) when the goal is
1222: unattended verification rather than real interactive use.
1223: 
1224: FILES: none changed - this is a shell/job-control interaction, not a
1225:        code bug; documented here so the NEXT agent debugging "GL/
1226:        daemon silently doesn't start" checks `ps aux` STATE column
1227:        (T = stopped) before assuming a rendering, DISPLAY, or config
1228:        bug

1249: SYMPTOM (per the discovered doc, not independently reproduced this
1250: session): GL window shows correctly when file-menu is launched

1255: data (0/491520 non-black), because chtpm_rgb_render's very FIRST

1257: current_frame.txt at all, and then a second race (pulse size already

1261: ROOT CAUSE OF THE RACE (already root-caused in the discovered doc, not
1262: re-derived this session): chtpm_rgb_render's startup order was
1263: gl_mirror -> chtpm_rgb_render -> chtpm_parser_pal, i.e. the renderer's
1264: own one-shot initial render + its baseline pulse-size read both happen
1265: BEFORE the parser has ever run compose_frame() once — a pure ordering
1266: bug, made far more likely to actually trigger under an app-launched
1267: combined session (5+ concurrent daemons = wider scheduler preemption
1268: window per that doc's own "Layer 3") than standalone (1-2 daemons).
1269: 
1270: DOCUMENTED FIX (per that doc's own "THE FIX" + "FILES CHANGED"
1271: sections): reorder to chtpm_parser_pal FIRST, poll (up to ~2s) for
1272: current_frame.txt to actually exist, THEN gl_mirror, THEN
1273: chtpm_rgb_render — so the renderer's own initial render and baseline
1274: pulse-size read both happen strictly after the first real compose.
1275: Explicitly listed as applied to "&.widgits/file-menu/button.sh —
1276: startup order (parser first + wait)", with "REMAINING ISSUES: None
1277: found" as of that doc's own last line.

1282: immediately after, then conditionally `gl_mirror`/`chtpm_rgb_render`
1283: inside the widget-profile block — no poll-for-current_frame.txt step
1284: anywhere, matching the doc's own documented "OLD ORDER (broken)"

1295: 52/53): every live test this session ran EITHER file-menu alone or
1296: editor alone directly via each project's own button.sh — never through
1297: @.apps/text-editor-xyz/button.sh, the actual combined "app" launcher
1298: that runs both concurrently. Low-contention single-project testing is
1299: exactly the condition under which this doc's own Layer 3 analysis says
1300: the race is LEAST likely to trigger — a clean single-project test
1301: proves relatively little about the higher-contention combined-launch
1302: path, similar in spirit to PITFALL 21's own "op-level test proves
1303: nothing about the real multi-process path" lesson.
1304: 
1305: STANDING RULE: before considering any editor<->file-menu GL/RGB work
1306: "done," it must be verified through `@.apps/text-editor-xyz/button.sh
1307: run` specifically (the actual combined launcher), not just each
1308: project's own standalone button.sh — this is the one invocation shape
1309: known to have hit a real, previously-fixed-then-apparently-regressed
1310: race condition. Do not assume "works standalone" implies "works
1311: combined."
1312: 
1313: UPDATE (2026-07-30) — RE-CONFIRMED LIVE AND RE-FIXED: the race does
1314: still reproduce today, and NOT only under the combined-launcher's
1315: extra CPU contention as first suspected — it reproduced running
1316: 102.editor-📄️00.00/button.sh run completely standalone, foreground,
1317: no combined launcher involved at all (single-project, low-daemon-count
1318: — exactly the condition earlier assumed to make it "unlikely"). This
1319: narrows the earlier "why wasn't this caught" theory: contention makes
1320: it MORE likely, but it is not required to trigger it — a handful of
1321: processes competing for scheduling on one core was already enough.
1322: Re-applied the documented reorder (chtpm_parser_pal already started
1323: first in both projects; added the missing "poll up to 2s for
1324: pieces/display/current_frame.txt to exist" step before launching
1325: gl_mirror/chtpm_rgb_render) to BOTH:
1326:   - 102.editor-📄️00.00/button.sh (never had this fix — new GL/RGB
1327:     wiring this session, PITFALL 52, inherited the same bug pattern by
1328:     copying the already-broken current file-menu/button.sh as the
1329:     reference instead of the documented fixed version)
1330:   - &.widgits/file-menu/button.sh (the actual regression this pitfall
1331:     entry first flagged)
1332: User-confirmed live: GL window renders real content (not black) on
1333: repeated real runs after the fix, using the same foreground
1334: `./button.sh run` invocation that reproduced the bug moments earlier.

1346: =======================================================================
1347: PITFALL 55: EMERGENCY_KILL.sh's own `pkill -x`/`pgrep -x` (exact-name
1348:             match) can NEVER kill two of the nine binaries it targets
1349:             by name — /proc/[pid]/comm truncates to 15 characters, and
1350:             both chtpm_parser_pal (17) and chtpm_rgb_render (16) exceed
1351:             that, silently and permanently
1352: =======================================================================
1353: DATE: 2026-07-30
1354: SEVERITY: CRITICAL (the house's own designated "nuclear/emergency"
1355:            runaway-process killer cannot kill exactly the two daemons
1356:            most prone to runaway CPU in this session's own GL/RGB work
1357:            — and its own end-of-run check uses the identical broken
1358:            match, so it prints "All project processes terminated."
1359:            while both keep running, uncaught)
1360: 
1361: SYMPTOM: none visible without deliberately testing — this is a silent,
1362: total failure mode. `pkill -x "chtpm_parser_pal"` against a real,
1363: currently-running process by that exact name returns nothing killed,
1364: `pgrep -x "chtpm_parser_pal"` reports no match, and the script's own
1365: final verification loop (built on the same `-x` check) reports success
1366: regardless.
1367: 
1368: ROOT CAUSE: `-x` (both pgrep and pkill) matches the process name as
1369: stored in the kernel's own bookkeeping (historically /proc/[pid]/comm,
1370: TASK_COMM_LEN = 16 bytes including the null terminator -> 15 visible
1371: characters, unconditional, regardless of the real argv[0] or the
1372: binary's own on-disk filename). Any binary name longer than 15
1373: characters is truncated by the KERNEL itself before `-x` ever sees it.
1374: Verified live: started a dummy process literally named
1375: `chtpm_parser_pal` (via a 17-character executable filename executed
1376: directly, so argv[0] is exactly that string) — `cat /proc/<pid>/comm`
1377: reported `chtpm_parser_pa` (15 chars, silently truncated), and
1378: `pgrep -x "chtpm_parser_pal"` reported no match against that same live
1379: process. Repeated for `chtpm_rgb_render` (16 chars) with the identical
1380: result (`chtpm_rgb_rende`, 15 chars, no match).
1381: 
1382: Every other name on the script's own target list (orchestrator,
1383: keyboard_input, gl_mirror, egg_window, avatar_window, renderer,
1384: prisc+x) happens to be <=15 characters, which is exactly why this went
1385: unnoticed — the bug only affects 2 of 9 names, and those 2 are the
1386: newest additions (GL/RGB pipeline daemons), not the original set the
1387: script was likely first written and tested against.
1388: 
1389: FIX: switched EMERGENCY_KILL.sh from `pkill -x "$name"` to `pkill -f`
1390: with an ANCHORED regex, `(^|/)NAME( |$)`, matching against the
1391: UNTRUNCATED /proc/[pid]/cmdline instead of the truncated comm field —
1392: while still requiring NAME to appear as its own whole token or
1393: path-final-component (never a bare substring), preserving the original
1394: script's own explicitly-stated "safe vs Chrome" property. Verified
1395: live, both directions:
1396:   - `pgrep -af '(^|/)renderer( |$)'` produces ZERO matches against a
1397:     real running Chrome (whose own cmdline only ever contains these
1398:     strings glued to "=", e.g. "--type=renderer" — the anchor
1399:     deliberately does not match that shape).
1400:   - The anchored pattern DOES match a live 17-character-named test
1401:     process that plain `-x` could never see.
1402:   - Ran the actual fixed EMERGENCY_KILL.sh against two dummy processes
1403:     named exactly `chtpm_parser_pal` and `chtpm_rgb_render` — both
1404:     killed, confirmed via a fresh pgrep immediately after (the script's
1405:     own visible exit code was a flaky nonzero value under this
1406:     environment's own load — same already-documented `kill_all.sh`-
1407:     class flakiness noted elsewhere in this file; the real state is in
1408:     the process table, not the exit code, and that was clean).
1409: 
1410: ALSO ADDED (direct user request, "could it be more comprehensive"):
1411: a blanket sweep of every `pieces/sessions/` directory in the house
1412: (bounded `find -maxdepth 4 -type d -path '*/pieces/sessions'`, a
1413: directory-NAME match, not a content walk — stays fast, and covers
1414: every project using this newer session-per-launch pattern rather than
1415: the old hardcoded 3-project list, which never touched
1416: 102.editor-📄️00.00, &.widgits/file-menu, or @.apps/text-editor-xyz's
1417: own /tmp/.text-editor-xyz-editor-* sessions at all). Explicit,
1418: confirmed design choice: this does NOT check whether a session is
1419: still legitimately in progress before removing it — matches the
1420: script's own stated "nuclear kill" purpose, at the cost of also being
1421: able to destroy someone's genuinely-unsaved in-progress session state.
1422: Acceptable for an EMERGENCY script; would not be acceptable for a
1423: routine/scheduled cleanup tool.
1424: 
1425: STANDING RULE: any binary name added to a future `-x`-based kill/check
1426: script MUST be checked against the 15-character TASK_COMM_LEN limit
1427: before assuming exact-name matching will work — `echo -n "$name" | wc -c`
1428: and reject anything over 15, or just use the anchored `-f` pattern from
1429: this fix as the default going forward instead of `-x` at all.
1430: 
1431: FILES: EMERGENCY_KILL.sh (pkill/pgrep matching mechanism rewritten;
1432:        blanket pieces/sessions/ sweep added)
1433: 
1434: =======================================================================
1435: PITFALL 56: fm_menu_input.c/editor_menu_input.c's FILE-field and
1436:             INTERACT typing paths can NEVER accept multi-byte UTF-8
1437:             (emoji) — and this house's own directory tree has emoji
1438:             path segments, meaning its own absolute path could never
1439:             be typed through either field. Fixed via a new, real,
1440:             separate PASTE mode in both ops.
1441: =======================================================================
1442: DATE: 2026-07-30
1443: SEVERITY: MEDIUM (blocks any real-key-path test/use that needs to
1444:            reference this house's own absolute path — not a crash,
1445:            just silent, wrong, truncated-looking input)
1446: 
1447: SYMPTOM: building a level-2 (§36.6) harness that types a real absolute
1448: fixture path character-by-character into file-menu's FILE field
1449: produced a garbled, wrong path — every emoji path segment
1450: (🤖️🪤️🏠️, 🥡️🪜️, ❤️‍🔥️, ...) silently vanished, leaving gaps ("//" where
1451: a segment used to be) or truncating early depending on exactly which
1452: byte landed where.
1453: 
1454: ROOT CAUSE: both handle_file_browser() (fm_menu_input.c) and the
1455: INTERACT canvas path (editor_menu_input.c's insert_char() call site)
1456: gate typed input at `key >= 32 && key <= 126` — single-byte ASCII
1457: only. This is not an oversight: the whole system's own "keypress"
1458: model is one int per keystroke, matching a real physical keyboard's
1459: one-scancode-per-press behavior — a multi-byte UTF-8 emoji was never
1460: one keystroke on a real keyboard either (IME composition or paste, not
1461: a bare keycode). Confirmed live: this house's own project directory
1462: literally contains emoji as real path segments, meaning its own
1463: absolute path could never be typed through the FILE field one keycode
1464: at a time, by design, regardless of any test-harness bug.
1465: 
1466: FIX: added a genuinely separate PASTE mode to both ops — `<op> PASTE
1467: "<string>"` as a second CLI invocation shape (distinct from `<op>
1468: <int-keycode>`), appending the given string WHOLE (real UTF-8 bytes,
1469: no per-character validation) to whichever field is active:
1470: fm_menu_input.c's handle_paste() (SEARCH/FILE field, file_browser mode
1471: only) and editor_menu_input.c's insert_string() (INTERACT typing,
1472: requires INTERACT already engaged — same real gate insert_char()'s own
1473: call site already has). This is the SAME op binary a REAL paste
1474: trigger (Ctrl+V/clipboard, not yet wired to any actual capture
1475: mechanism) would eventually call — adding this mode is a real
1476: capability, not a test-only shortcut, even though only a test harness
1477: exercises it as of this session.
1478: 
1479: VERIFIED LIVE: `fm_menu_input.+x PASTE "<full emoji-laden house
1480: path>"` produced an exact, byte-for-byte correct path_buffer — full
1481: emoji preserved, zero truncation, zero garbling — compared directly
1482: against the identical string.
1483: 
1484: ALSO DISCOVERED WHILE FIXING THIS (separate, real timing bug in the
1485: PRE-EXISTING per-keystroke path, not the new PASTE mode): injecting
1486: characters into interact_relay.txt/keyboard/history.txt faster than
1487: ~16.7ms apart (main_loop_chtpm.pal's own `sleep 16667` poll interval)
1488: silently drops characters — confirmed live, a tight no-delay bash loop
1489: truncated a path after ~12 characters, adding a 50ms sleep between
1490: each injected keystroke fixed it completely. Any future key-injection
1491: harness for this house's PAL loop family needs this same throttle;
1492: it's a property of the PAL loop's own polling rate, not specific to
1493: file-menu or this harness.
1494: 
1495: FILES: &.widgits/file-menu/ops/fm_menu_input.c (handle_paste() + PASTE
1496:        mode in main()), 102.editor-📄️00.00/ops/editor_menu_input.c
1497:        (insert_string() + PASTE mode in main()),
1498:        @.apps/text-editor-xyz/test-harn-ed-app/scenarios/common.sh
1499:        (fm_paste()/ed_paste() call these; inject_string()/fm_relay_
1500:        string() gained the 50ms per-character throttle for the
1501:        ordinary keystroke path)

1504: PITFALL 57: GL windows render emoji as a meaningless, arbitrary
1505:             colored block instead of anything resembling the real
1506:             emoji — chtpm_rgb_render.c's emoji-color table is entirely
1507:             hand-curated per game entity and has no entry for plain
1508:             decorative text emoji
1509: =======================================================================
1510: DATE: 2026-07-30
1511: SEVERITY: LOW-MEDIUM (cosmetic — GL window still renders and functions
1512:            correctly otherwise; the block is just visually wrong/
1513:            meaningless, reported directly by the user testing the real
1514:            GL output)
1515: 
1516: SYMPTOM: 102.editor-📄️00.00's own seed buffer content ("hi 😅 😆 🐧")
1517: rendered in its GL window as flat colored blocks with no visible
1518: relation to the actual emoji (a face, a face, a penguin).
1519: 
1520: ROOT CAUSE: system/chtpm_rgb_render.c's emoji_colors[] lookup table is
1521: populated ENTIRELY from game-entity-specific sources: 4 hand-authored
1522: registry files (terrain_types.txt, furniture_types.txt, items.txt,
1523: monster_types.txt, each mapping a specific game object to a specific
1524: emoji+color+asset) plus a short hardcoded list (add_hardcoded_emoji_
1525: color(), previously only hero/xlector). Any emoji NOT in one of those
1526: five sources falls through to utf8_hash_color() — an FNV-hash of the
1527: emoji's own UTF-8 bytes turned into an RGB color with ZERO relation to
1528: the emoji's actual appearance. Editor's own decorative text emoji were
1529: never going to be in a terrain/furniture/item/monster registry (they
1530: aren't game entities at all) and had no hardcoded entry either.
1531: 
1532: FIX (stopgap, see !.pal-2do.txt 2DO 1 for the real, generic, not-yet-
1533: wired fix): added 3 new add_hardcoded_emoji_color() calls for the 3
1534: specific emoji currently in use (😅/😆/🐧), using their real,
1535: observed dominant colors, with asset_id values pointing at
1536: emoji_assets/ folders that do NOT exist yet — safe, because
1537: load_emoji_voxels() already fails closed on a missing folder (fopen
1538: fails, returns 0, caller skips the voxel blit, flat color still
1539: applies) — confirmed by reading that function directly before relying
1540: on it.
1541: 
1542: UPDATE (2026-07-30, SAME DAY) — REAL FIX NOW DONE, not just the
1543: stopgap above: !.pal-2do.txt 2DO 1's own "real, generic, already-
1544: EXISTING solution" has been wired in. Copied #.emoji-studio-
1545: 501.02.05t's own emoji-gen-atlas.c + emoji-xtract.c (+ their stb_image
1546: libs) verbatim into 014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/ops/, compiled as ops/+x/
1547: emoji_gen_atlas.+x + ops/+x/emoji_xtract.+x, propagated to
1548: 102.editor-📄️00.00 and &.widgits/file-menu via their own build.sh
1549: (same copy-from-wsr-pal pattern already used for chtpm_rgb_render/
1550: gl_mirror). New functions in chtpm_rgb_render.c
1551: (decode_utf8_codepoint(), ensure_emoji_asset_generated(),
1552: add_dynamic_emoji_color()) sit in blit_text()'s own fallback path,
1553: tried BEFORE utf8_hash_color(): any codepoint not already in
1554: emoji_colors[] gets a REAL voxel asset generated on first use (real
1555: FreeType rasterization via the actual NotoColorEmoji.ttf font already
1556: installed on this system, confirmed present), cached both on disk
1557: (pieces/registry/emoji_assets/<HEX>/voxels_16.csv — hex-codepoint
1558: naming adopted from wraith-alpha's own convention, so assets are
1559: shareable between the two) and in-process (so later frames in the same
1560: run hit the table directly, no repeated shell-outs per frame). The 3
1561: hand-curated stopgap entries above still work fine as an override
1562: layer (a project can still want a non-natural color for a specific
1563: emoji) — they are simply no longer load-bearing for the general case.
1564: 
1565: VERIFIED LIVE, end-to-end, with a BRAND NEW emoji never referenced
1566: anywhere in this house before (🦄, U+1F984 — not one of the 3 stopgap
1567: entries, not in wraith-alpha's own pre-existing asset set either):
1568: launched 102.editor-📄️00.00's real button.sh run, engaged INTERACT for
1569: real, inserted 🦄 into the live buffer, and confirmed
1570: pieces/registry/emoji_assets/1F984/voxels_16.csv was created
1571: AUTOMATICALLY during that live run (138/256 real non-transparent
1572: voxel pixels — a genuine recognizable shape, not empty), with
1573: rgb_frame.raw showing substantial real pixel variation immediately
1574: after (chtpm_rgb_render still alive, still rendering, no crash, no
1575: stall) — proving the on-demand path fires correctly inside the real
1576: running app, not just in an isolated CLI test of the two tools.
1577:

1596: =======================================================================
1597: PITFALL 58: file-menu's REAL menu-driven NEW/SAVE/SAVE_AS/LOAD/PING
1598:             has been silently doing NOTHING since this code was
1599:             written — enqueue_cmd()/enqueue_cmd_with_path() pass the
1600:             WRONG directory to fm_enqueue_cmd.+x, which then finds no
1601:             focus.txt and exits 1, unseen (stderr -> /dev/null the
1602:             whole way up). Found ONLY because a real level-2 (§36.6)
1603:             harness exists now — the level-1 harness that predates it
1604:             could not have caught this even in principle.
1605: =======================================================================
1606: DATE: 2026-07-30
1607: SEVERITY: CRITICAL (the actual, real, human-facing file-menu <->
1608:            editor feature — the whole point of building both projects
1609:            together — has never worked via the real UI, only via
1610:            direct op invocation)
1611: 
1612: SYMPTOM: building the first real level-2 (§36.6) harness for LOAD
1613: (demo_load.sh, driving file-menu's own menu via real key injection,
1614: exactly as a human would) produced this sequence, confirmed live:
1615:   - file-menu's OWN state (fm_state.txt) showed EVERY step succeeding
1616:     perfectly: mode correctly switched to file_browser, browse_dir
1617:     correctly resolved from a valid focus, path_buffer correctly
1618:     received the pasted path, cursor nav correctly reached confirm_idx,
1619:     Enter correctly fired and even set status_line="Command sent" —
1620:     every visible signal said success.
1621:   - Yet editor's own inbox.txt (pieces/system/widget_cmds/inbox.txt)
1622:     stayed completely empty. editor_buffer.txt never changed.
1623:   - No error appeared ANYWHERE a human or a level-1 test would ever
1624:     look — because there wasn't one to see: everything downstream of
1625:     the actual bug reported nothing (see ROOT CAUSE), and the real
1626:     failure point's own stderr is routed to /dev/null through the
1627:     whole button.sh chain.
1628: 
1629: ROOT CAUSE (system/fm_menu_input.c): `has_focus()` correctly reads
1630: focus from `"%s/pieces/system/focus.txt"` (project_root, WITH the
1631: pieces/system suffix — confirmed correct because browse_dir kept
1632: resolving correctly using it). But `enqueue_cmd()` and
1633: `enqueue_cmd_with_path()` — three lines away in the SAME source file —
1634: pass bare `project_root` (WITHOUT that suffix) as the
1635: `<widget_state_dir>` argument to `ops/+x/fm_enqueue_cmd.+x`.
1636: fm_enqueue_cmd.c's own focus lookup is `"%s/focus.txt"` against
1637: whatever directory it's given — so it looked for focus.txt at
1638: `project_root/focus.txt` (no such file — the real one lives one level
1639: deeper, at `project_root/pieces/system/focus.txt`, matching how
1640: button.sh actually invokes fm_set_focus.+x: `fm_set_focus.+x
1641: "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/system" "$FOCUS_SESSION"` — the suffix is
1642: supplied by the CALLER, not baked into fm_set_focus.c itself).
1643: fm_enqueue_cmd.c's own `read_kv(focus, "inbox_path", ...)` found
1644: nothing, its own guard fired (`fprintf(stderr, "fm_enqueue_cmd: no
1645: focus (run fm_set_focus first)\n"); return 1;`), and the whole
1646: operation silently did nothing — no line ever reached inbox.txt, no
1647: signal reached anywhere fm_menu_input.c's own caller (prisc+x's
1648: OP_EXEC via popen()) surfaces to a user.
1649: 
1650: WHY THIS WENT UNNOTICED UNTIL NOW: %.harnesses/file-menu+editor/
1651: scenarios/demo_load_save.sh (the pre-existing, level-1-only harness —
1652: see PITFALL 50/§36.6's own critique of it) calls
1653: `ops/+x/fm_enqueue_cmd.+x` DIRECTLY as a bash subprocess, with the
1654: CORRECT wdir argument (`"$SESSION_DIR/pieces/system"`) supplied
1655: straight from the harness script itself — completely bypassing
1656: enqueue_cmd_with_path() and this exact bug. That harness's own 6/6
1657: "ALL PASS" was real and correct FOR WHAT IT TESTED (the cmd-bus
1658: contract, given a correct wdir) — it was never capable of proving the
1659: one thing that actually matters to a human user: that choosing LOAD
1660: from file-menu's own real menu, with a real keypress, actually does
1661: anything. This is the single clearest, most direct confirmation this
1662: session found for why §36.6 exists at all — an op-level PASS and a
1663: real, working feature are two different claims, and this house had
1664: been trusting the wrong one for this exact feature the whole time.
1665: 
1666: FIX: enqueue_cmd() and enqueue_cmd_with_path() now build
1667: `wdir = project_root + "/pieces/system"` (a new
1668: FM_WIDGET_STATE_SUBDIR constant) and pass THAT to fm_enqueue_cmd.+x,
1669: matching has_focus()'s own already-correct path exactly.
1670: 
1671: VERIFIED LIVE: rebuilt file-menu, re-ran the level-2 harness — LOAD:
1672: editor_buffer.txt now matches the loaded fixture byte-for-byte AND
1673: current_frame.txt (the actual rendered frame) shows it. SAVE_AS: typed
1674: new text into editor's own INTERACT canvas for real, saved via
1675: file-menu's own real SAVE_AS menu flow, and the resulting file ON DISK
1676: contains exactly what was typed. Both via real key injection through
1677: real running processes, zero op-level shortcuts, per §36.6.
1678: 
1679: FILES: &.widgits/file-menu/ops/fm_menu_input.c (enqueue_cmd(),
1680:        enqueue_cmd_with_path() — wdir argument fixed)
1681: SEE ALSO: @.apps/text-editor-xyz/test-harn-ed-app/ (the level-2 harness
1682:        that found this — see its own README.txt); §36.6 in
1683:        !.xyzos-standards+1.txt (the standard this bug is the concrete
1684:        justification for)

1689: — looked like a real remaining bug in the generic path. Added

1732: ROOT CAUSE: this house's own directory tree names every path-segment
1733: emoji with an explicit trailing VARIATION SELECTOR-16 (U+FE0F), not a
1734: bare codepoint - confirmed via direct byte inspection: "🤖️" is
1735: actually U+1F916 (ROBOT FACE) + U+FE0F, "❤️‍🔥️" is a full 5-codepoint
1736: ZWJ sequence (U+2764 + U+FE0F + U+200D(ZWJ) + U+1F525 + U+FE0F).
1737: blit_text() decodes ONE UTF-8 CODEPOINT at a time, not one grapheme
1738: cluster - so before this fix, VS16/ZWJ (both invisible formatting
1739: codepoints with no real glyph) fell through to the exact same generic
1740: on-demand emoji path as any real character: ensure_emoji_asset_
1741: generated() actually shelled out to FreeType for hex_id=FE0F, which
1742: rendered a near-empty glyph (confirmed: 4/256 non-transparent pixels)
1743: - but blit_text() ALWAYS draws a solid VOXEL_PX-wide (60,60,60) gray
1744: backdrop block for a generic-path tile regardless of how empty the
1745: voxel art itself is, so every VS16 (and every ZWJ) still consumed a
1746: full extra 16px gray block. Editor's own seed-buffer test content
1747: (😅/😆/😇/🦄) was typed bare with no VS16 suffix, so it never hit this
1748: - same binary, different content, hence the two projects LOOKED
1749: different despite running identical code.
1750: 
1751: FIX: blit_text() (system/chtpm_rgb_render.c) now peeks the decoded
1752: codepoint before doing anything else in its >=0x80 branch; if it's
1753: U+FE0E, U+FE0F, or U+200D, the byte sequence is skipped entirely (p
1754: advances, x_px does NOT, no tile drawn, loop continues) rather than
1755: being treated as a real, unmatched emoji needing on-demand generation.
1756: ZWJ sequences are not composed into one combined glyph (this file has
1757: no glyph-composition capability) - skipping the ZWJ/VS16 still
1758: correctly renders e.g. heart + fire as two adjacent real emoji tiles
1759: with no junk block between them, which is the actual reported bug,

794: apply to EVERY project in this family, not just what happened this session.
795: 
796: WHERE EVERYTHING LIVES: yz.muchiverse/2.muchi-verse-0.0/ is home - every real
797: project is a sibling directory inside it (1.muchi-pal-agent🤖️, 1.pal-chat-irc,
798: 1.pal-chain⛓️, 1.pal-forum, 1.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️️, 2.zoo_0000, 0.user-pal👤️, 1.wsr-pal,
799: pal-scripts, 0.gemma-only♦️, etc). shared-ops/ holds the CANONICAL copy of code shared across projects
800: (prisc+x.c, chtpm_parser_pal.c, keyboard_input.c, palnet_peer.c) - each
801: project keeps its OWN local copy under system/ or ops/, synced in
802: deliberately (sync_shared_op.sh / a plain diff-then-cp), not symlinked -
803: see shared-ops-manifest.txt for the current consumer list per file. Design
804: docs live either in #.dox/ (family-wide, e.g. USER-PAL-STANDARD.txt,
805: PAL-CHAT-IRC-STANDARD.txt) or as a per-project *-STANDARD.txt/
806: *-ARCHITECTURE.txt/ROADMAP-*.txt. !.pal-standards.txt is the single
807: running rulebook - read it, it accumulates every real gotcha found across
808: every project (session isolation, cli_io behavior, kill patterns, etc).
809: 
810: THE CORE TECH STACK, in one paragraph: prisc+x is a tiny custom VM (RISC-V-
811: style registers, li/beq/j/custom-op bytecode) that runs .pal scripts.
812: chtpm_parser_pal is the shared UI engine - real declarative <cli_io>/
813: <button>/${piece_methods} layouts, drives BOTH terminal rendering and (via
814: chtpm_rgb_render) real RGB pixel rendering from the SAME layout. Every
815: project is: a chtpm layout (pieces/chtpm/layouts/*.chtpm) + a .pal script
816: (the main loop, calls custom ops each tick) + a pile of small, self-
817: contained C "ops" (each does ONE thing, owns its own root-resolution/
818: constants, never includes shared headers) + plain pipe-delimited or
819: key=value text files as ALL state (state.txt, context_log.txt, registry
820: files) - no databases, no JSON-as-state, no sockets unless a project
821: genuinely needs real networking (palnet_peer.c).
822: 
823: STANDING CONVENTIONS THAT APPLY EVERYWHERE (violate these and you will
824: reintroduce bugs that were already found and fixed once):
825:   - SESSION ISOLATION: every project's button.sh `run` action creates a
826:     throwaway pieces/sessions/<id>/ dir, symlinking in shared/static code
827:     and shared/persistent data, with real private ephemeral UI state -
828:     deleted on exit. Never launch a second instance directly against a
829:     project's own root - always go through button.sh's own session
830:     creation, or replicate it exactly if scripting around it (see pal-
831:     scripts/launch_irc_agent.sh for a worked example).
832:   - KILL BY CWD, NEVER BY ARGV: every session's own processes have
833:     IDENTICAL argv text (`system/prisc+x pal/main_loop.pal` etc.) - a bare
834:     `pkill -f` will kill the WRONG session's process. Always match
835:     /proc/$pid/cwd against the specific session dir you mean to kill.
836:   - PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT IS CACHED, NOT LIVE: every op/process reads this
837:     env var ONCE at startup into a static buffer. Moving/renaming a
838:     directory updates a running process's kernel cwd (Linux follows
839:     renames), but NOT this cached string - a leftover process can keep
840:     writing to the OLD path indefinitely. Always re-sweep for orphaned
841:     processes (by real /proc/$pid/cwd, not by name) after any directory
842:     move, and do it TWICE - once isn't always enough.
843:   - ONE VISIBLE FRAME WRITER: no op writes pieces/display/current_frame.
844:     txt (or a project's own view.txt) directly except chtpm_parser_pal's
845:     own internal compose step - an op writes its OWN view.txt, then pings
846:     frame_changed.txt.
847:   - REGISTER-SENTINEL BUG CLASS in prisc+x.c's own opcode parsers: several
848:     opcodes (OP_READ_HISTORY, OP_EXEC) had a parsing bug where an unset
849:     register field defaulted to 0 (a REAL register, hardwired-reset every
850:     instruction) instead of an explicit "not given" sentinel (-1). If a
851:     new opcode or new calling form is ever added, check this exact class
852:     of bug before trusting it - "does an unset field default to something
853:     indistinguishable from a valid value" is the actual question to ask.
854:   - DESIGN-DOC-FIRST: every real feature in this family's history has a
855:     doc written and read BEFORE code - don't skip this even under time
856:     pressure, it's what keeps multi-session work coherent.
857:   - ALWAYS DIFF BEFORE OVERWRITING a synced shared file - check
858:     shared-ops-manifest.txt for "NOT synced from here" notes before
859:     blindly copying a canonical file over a project's own fork of it.
860:   - REGISTRY + SWITCH + SELF-CONTAINED OPS is the one pattern that shows
861:     up in every single feature in this family (see section 5 above) -
862:     when in doubt about how to add a new swappable behavior anywhere,
863:     this is almost always the answer.
864:   - ALWAYS FIX KNOWN-WRONG INFO IN DOCS the moment it's noticed, don't
865:     wait to be asked - a standing instruction from this user specifically.
866: 
867: THE USER'S OWN COLLABORATION STYLE, worth internalizing:
868:   - Terse, direct, technical - expects real investigation (read the actual
869:     file, run the actual process) over "should work" reasoning. Live-test
870:     everything with real running processes wherever possible.
871:   - Corrects wrong assumptions directly and expects the correction to

874:   - Wants honest, calibrated answers when asked for an opinion/estimate

879:   - Asks for architecture discussion/clarifying questions BEFORE big,
