1: # !.HOUSE_STDS.md — General House Standards, From Zero
2: 
3: Written 2026-08-02. This document is a **general reference for a fresh agent with zero context**, covering everything directly learned/verified while building `&.widgits/board-viewer` (a GL widget with a real camera/raymarch view) — not just the widget-specific parts. It's organized so you can jump to the section you need; §A-§C are general house mechanics that apply to almost any project in this dev-env tree, §D onward get progressively more widget/3D-specific.
4: 
5: **Scope honesty**: this reflects what was directly touched and verified this session. It is not a claim of exhaustive coverage of every file/project in this house — where something is known only as a pointer (a doc that exists but wasn't read in depth), it's listed as an index entry, not elaborated. Follow the pointer, don't assume this doc already told you everything in it.
6: 
7: **If you only read one section first, read §F (pitfalls) before writing any code** — every entry there was a real, live-caught bug that cost real debugging time.
8: 
9: ---
10: 
11: ## A. Core CHTPM/PAL mechanics (applies to nearly every project here)
12: 
13: ### A.1 The basic shape
14: 
15: Every project in this style has:
16: - `system/` — shared engine binaries (`prisc+x` the PAL VM, `chtpm_parser_pal` the menu/layout engine, `keyboard_input`, `renderer`, optionally `chtpm_rgb_render`/`gl_mirror` for a GL window). These are almost always **copied** (not symlinked) from a canonical source project into each consuming project — see §A.4.
17: - `ops/` — project-specific C binaries (compiled to `ops/+x/<name>.+x`) that do real work: read/write state, render content, dispatch commands.
18: - `pal/*.pal` — tiny PAL scripts that glue ops together in a loop (read a key, dispatch it, compose a frame, sleep, repeat).
19: - `pieces/chtpm/layouts/*.chtpm` — XML-ish layout files defining screens: buttons, text, substitution variables.
20: - `pieces/system/`, `pieces/display/`, `pieces/apps/player_app/` — where state, frame buffers, and trigger/marker files actually live.
21: - `default_op.txt` — registers every op name your `.pal` scripts can call, format: `name type handler {description}`.
22: - `button.sh` — the launcher; conventionally supports `run`/`build`/`kill`/`check` verbs at minimum.
23: 
24: ### A.2 Session isolation
25: 
26: A "session" is `pieces/sessions/<timestamp>-<pid>/` — a fresh, isolated working directory for one running instance. `system`/`ops`/`pal`/`pieces/chtpm`/`pieces/registry` are typically **symlinked** into it (so binary rebuilds apply live); anything that needs to actually **persist** across the session's lifetime or be genuinely writable (state files, board data) must be explicitly touched + symlinked in `button.sh`, or it silently writes into the ephemeral session dir and vanishes on cleanup. **This exact class of bug (a real state file never symlinked, writes silently going nowhere) has been found and fixed multiple times across different projects this house** — always check `button.sh`'s own symlink list against every file your ops actually write to.
27: 
28: ### A.3 The digit-dispatch / numbered-button convention — READ CAREFULLY, a real mistake was made and corrected here
29: 
30: Almost every screen shows numbered items (`[>] 1. Label`, `[ ] 2. Label`...), driven by a `piece.pdl` file with `METHOD | Label | COMMAND` rows. **`resolved_item = (key - '0') - 1` is CORRECT for this dispatch path, not a bug** — this was gotten wrong once this session (a "fix" was briefly applied to civ-txt/tactics-txt's own copies, live-caught as breaking working code, and reverted before being tested/shipped). The reason it's correct: `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s own `${piece_methods}` button-generator starts its internal `method_idx` at **2**, not 0 or 1 (`method_idx = (loader) ? 1 : 2`, `chtpm_parser_pal.c:1115`) — so the FIRST numbered row's `onClick` becomes `"KEY:2"`, which `send_command()`'s own `"KEY:"` handler turns into the raw ASCII digit `'2'` (not `'1'`) via `inject_raw_key('0'+k)`. `(key-'0')-1` is the correct compensation for that +2 offset: key=`'2'`(50) → `resolved_item=(50-48)-1=1` → `items[0]`, the real first item. **Do not "fix" this in any file whose numbered items are auto-generated via `${piece_methods}`** — that's the standard, dominant case across this house.
31: 
32: **Where the `-1` genuinely IS wrong**: only in code paths that receive a truly raw, literal keyboard digit that never passed through the engine's own `KEY:N` regeneration — e.g. a hand-rolled digit accumulator reading `interact_relay.txt` directly outside the normal numbered-button system (this was real, and fixed, in `board-viewer`'s own now-deleted nav-mode digit dispatch, before that whole mechanism was replaced by the real `onClick="INTERACT"` static button, §D.2 — that code path never went through `${piece_methods}` at all, so the +2 offset never applied there). **Before touching this formula in any file, first determine whether the numbers on screen come from `${piece_methods}` (almost certainly yes, and the `-1` is correct) or from something reading raw keys directly (rare, and only there might the `-1` be wrong).** When genuinely unsure, verify against `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s own `method_idx` initial value directly, don't assume either direction.
33: 
34: ### A.4 Real code over stale docs, always
35: 
36: Doc comments in this house are sometimes wrong/stale — several were found and corrected this session (mutaclysm's own `ops/choice.c` header claimed `'i'`/`'e'` were still live interact/emoji toggles; they'd been removed). **When a doc comment and the actual code disagree, trust the code, verify by direct read, and fix the doc** (standing instruction: "if u need to update stale docs, pls do, no need to have confusion in future agents"). Never assume a comment describes current behavior without spot-checking against the real function it's attached to, especially for anything load-bearing you're about to build on top of.
37: 
38: ### A.5 The `[>]`/`[^]`/`[ ]` focus-glyph convention
39: 
40: Numbered/interactive elements render with a 3-char prefix: `[>]` = focused (navigable, not yet active), `[^]` = active/engaged (typing, or a real `onClick="INTERACT"` element currently owns input), `[ ]` = neither. This is drawn **entirely inside the shared `chtpm_parser_pal.c` engine** (`render_element()`), not by any project's own compose op — don't try to reimplement this glyph logic yourself.
41: 
42: ---
43: 
44: ## B. The rendering/display pipeline, end to end
45: 
46: Understanding this chain is required before touching any GL-related work — most of the real bugs this session were races or misunderstandings within this pipeline.
47: 
48: ```
49: your compose op (writes text)
50:   → pieces/apps/player_app/view.txt  (your own raw content)
51:   → pieces/display/current_frame.txt  (chtpm_parser_pal's own chrome-wrapped composition, substituting ${game_map} etc from view.txt)
52:       [trigger: pieces/display/frame_changed.txt grown by chtpm_parser_pal.c's process_key() on EVERY key, unconditionally]
53:       [trigger: pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt grown by chtpm_parser_pal.c's own internal compose_frame(), also unconditional]
54:   → system/renderer (ASCII terminal path) — polls frame_changed.txt-equivalent markers, prints current_frame.txt, logs to frame_history.txt
55:   → system/chtpm_rgb_render (GL path, persistent background daemon) — polls BOTH trigger files above, font-rasterizes current_frame.txt into:
56:       pieces/display/rgb_frame.raw (raw RGBA32 buffer) + rgb_frame.receipt.txt (frame_w/frame_h/checksum)
57:       [trigger: pieces/display/rgb_frame_changed.txt grown once a new rgb_frame.raw is ready]
58:   → system/gl_mirror (real GLUT window) — polls rgb_frame_changed.txt, uploads rgb_frame.raw as a GL texture, blits one quad
59:       [receipt: pieces/display/gl_display.receipt.txt, for cross-checking source vs. what actually got uploaded]
60: ```
61: 
62: **Key facts that cause real bugs if missed**:
63: - `chtpm_rgb_render` is a genuinely project-agnostic daemon — it has zero game-state awareness, it just rasterizes whatever text is in `current_frame.txt`. It re-renders on EITHER trigger file growing, and **both grow unconditionally on every key**, regardless of what your own project's ops do or don't call. You cannot suppress this from your side.
64: - If your own project ALSO writes `rgb_frame.raw` directly (e.g. a custom 3D renderer), you get a real write-write race against this daemon — see §E.1 for the actual fix (write a separate overlay file instead).
65: - The **receipt + checksum pattern** (`rgb_frame.receipt.txt`'s `frame_w`/`frame_h`/`checksum_fnv1a64`, `gl_display.receipt.txt` for the GL-upload side) is real, working, and load-bearing — `gl_mirror.c` reads its OWN window/texture dimensions from the receipt dynamically rather than hardcoding them, specifically because two different renderers can write different-sized frames to the same path. If you add a new renderer, follow this pattern rather than hardcoding dimensions anywhere downstream.
66: - **Headless GL verification**: `ops/dump_rgb_png.c` (real, working, in `101.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️️+18.01`) converts `rgb_frame.raw` into an actual viewable PNG — built specifically because an agent can't look at a live GLUT window. Port this (or just do direct pixel-buffer sampling in Python, which is what was actually used this session — see §G) rather than relying on eyeballing a screenshot.
67: 
68: ---
69: 
70: ## C. Testing & CPU-safety discipline (standing, non-negotiable)
71: 
72: - **CPU safety is paramount** — this house has crashed from unthrottled idle-poll loops before. Every idle-sync PAL loop pattern uses `sleep <ms>` between poll iterations; never remove that. Every automated test must be `timeout`-wrapped.
73: - **`timeout` does not reliably kill backgrounded children** of the script it wraps — verify no leftover process afterward (`ps aux | grep yourproject`) and clean up by PID directly (`kill <pid>`) if anything survived. This was a real, repeated issue this session.
74: - **`pkill` may be sandboxed/blocked** in some environments — every invocation returned a non-zero exit regardless of pattern, including `--version`, in this session's own environment. Use `ps aux | grep` + targeted `kill <pid>` instead of assuming `pkill` works.
75: - **Never touch the user's own live testing sessions.** If you see `run-widget`/other long-running processes you didn't start, leave them alone — they're very likely the user's own open windows.
76: - **Cross-session state contamination**: shared (non-session-scoped) state files can get stomped by a stale concurrent session. Kill all stale sessions before every test, or you'll chase phantom bugs that are really just two test runs fighting over the same file.
77: - **Headless testing technique**: `NO_GL=1 bash button.sh run-app <focus_root>` (or your project's equivalent) gets a real running session with zero display dependency. Inject raw decimal keycodes directly into `interact_relay.txt` (one per line) to simulate exactly what the real engine's key-relay would write — this is how every fix in this document was verified without ever opening a real window.
78: - **Verifying pixel-level output without a screen**: read the raw RGBA buffer directly in Python (`open(path,'rb').read()`, index by `(y*W+x)*4`), sample known coordinates or scan for known colors. This is real, fast, and was how both a horizontal-mirror bug and an upside-down-camera bug were confirmed and fixed — pure reasoning about the math got both wrong on the first attempt.
79: 
80: ---
81: 
82: ## D. Widgets — a separate GL-window program paired to a host project
83: 
84: ### D.1 What a "widget" actually is here
85: 
86: A **separate program** (own `system/` binaries, own GL window, own PAL/CHTPM session) that:
87: - gets spawned by a host project via a real OS process (`setsid ... button.sh run-widget <host_project_root> &`), passing the host's real project root as a focus argument — **not** a same-session screen/view change inside the host's own CHTPM layout (that was a real, corrected architecture mistake early in this house's own history building this — `@.apps/BOARD_WIDGET_ARCHITECTURE.md` §0).
88: - reads the host's own real files directly (e.g. `board.txt`) for real-time sync — no new IPC needed, just knowing which root to read.
89: - renders as ASCII (headless/no-DISPLAY fallback) or a real GL window (default when `$DISPLAY` is set).
90: - `setsid` is required when spawning — plain `system(cmd) + &` does NOT create a new process group, so a `timeout` wrapping the HOST project's own test run can cascade-kill a supposedly-independent widget process.
91: 
92: ### D.2 Nav mode — the real mechanism, do NOT reinvent it
93: 
94: `onClick="INTERACT"` is a **reserved string** the shared `chtpm_parser_pal.c` engine special-cases entirely inside itself — NOT something reachable via a dynamically-generated `piece.pdl` METHOD row (those always become `onClick="KEY:N"`, confirmed via direct code read of the engine's own button-generator; only a small whitelist of literal parser commands like `LOAD_PROJECT:`/`BACK`/`RELEASE` are exceptions, and `INTERACT` isn't one of them). It must be a real, hand-written `<button onClick="INTERACT">` in the `.chtpm` XML itself:
95: 
96: ```xml
97: <panel time_reactive="true">
98:     <module>system/prisc+x pal/main_module.pal</module>
99:     <interact src="pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt" />
100:     <button label="Nav Mode (camera + selector)" onClick="INTERACT" /><br/>
101:     <text label="${game_map}" /><br/>
102:     ${piece_methods}
103: </panel>
104: ```
105: 
106: Once engaged, the engine itself: sets its own internal `active_index` (nothing for your op to track), relays raw keys (arrows remapped to sentinel 1000-1003) into whatever your `<interact src>` names, consumes ESC itself before your op ever sees it, and flips the `[>]`/`[^]` glyph automatically. It also exports `pieces/display/active_gui_is_typing.txt` (`"1"`=active,`"0"`=not) for your own status display — read this, don't invent your own flag. **Consequence**: your own menu_input op needs almost no gating logic — it's only ever invoked with camera/arrow codes while genuinely inside real nav mode, by construction. Full investigation: `&.widgits/interact-fix-widget.txt`.
107: 
108: ### D.3 Scaffolding — copy `board-viewer`'s shape
109: 
110: ```
111: your-widget/
112:   button.sh                # two profiles: run-app (ASCII/headless), run-widget (GL)
113:   default_op.txt            # registers your project's own ops
114:   scripts/build.sh           # compiles ops, copies system/ binaries
115:   ops/
116:     yourwidget_compose_frame.c
117:     yourwidget_menu_input.c
118:     yourwidget_render_3d.c     # only if doing a 3D view
119:   pal/main_module.pal
120:   pieces/
121:     chtpm/layouts/yourwidget.chtpm
122:     registry/fonts/ascii/       # copied from wsr-pal
123:     registry/emoji_assets/      # real voxel textures, see §E.3
124:   projects/your-widget/pieces/<piece_id>/   # holds the dynamically-regenerated piece.pdl
125: ```
126: 
127: `scripts/build.sh`: copy (never symlink) `system/prisc+x`, `chtpm_parser_pal`, `chtpm_rgb_render`, `keyboard_input`, `renderer` from `014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/system/` — **wsr-pal's own `chtpm_rgb_render` already has BOTH the generic on-demand emoji-generation feature AND the `MAP3D_MARKER`/overlay-compositing feature needed for a 3D view (§E.1)**. Do NOT swap this for mutaclysm's own fork — mutaclysm's copy has zero on-demand emoji generation (it only recognizes emoji hand-curated from its own registry files, which won't exist in your project), and swapping to it silently breaks 2D emoji rendering entirely. This was a real, live-caught regression this session — an earlier research pass incorrectly concluded wsr-pal's copy lacked overlay support; it does not, verify directly with `grep -n "blit_overlay\|MAP3D" system/chtpm_rgb_render.c` before believing otherwise. `gl_mirror` also comes from wsr-pal (real `interact_relay.txt` key-forwarding; mutaclysm's own copy is mirror-only, no forwarding). Also copy `ops/+x/emoji_gen_atlas.+x` + `emoji_xtract.+x` from wsr-pal (§E.3).
128: 
129: ---
130: 
131: ## E. The 3D view — three real, separate pieces
132: 
133: ### E.1 Avoiding the rgb_frame.raw race
134: 
135: If your own 3D-render op writes `pieces/display/rgb_frame.raw` directly, it races `chtpm_rgb_render`'s own unconditional re-render (§B) — a real, live-caught bug (3D frame briefly shows, then reverts to stale 2D content within ~30ms). **The fix**: your 3D op writes to a SEPARATE file — `pieces/display/rgb_frame_3d_overlay.raw` + `.receipt.txt` (format: `overlay_w=%d\noverlay_h=%d\n`, no checksum) — and never touches `rgb_frame.raw` at all. `chtpm_rgb_render` watches for a single `0x01` (SOH) byte as a line's first character in `current_frame.txt`; on finding it, composites your overlay in at that row, then skips `ceil(overlay_h/GLYPH_H)` further source lines. Your own 2D compose op must therefore emit, only while `render_mode==1`:
136: ```c
137: fputc(0x01, out); fputc('\n', out);
138: for (int i = 0; i < N; i++) fputc('\n', out);  // N = ceil(overlay_h_px / 16), MUST match your 3D op's own overlay height
139: ```
140: Keep this constant in sync manually between the two files — no shared-header convention in this house's self-contained-ops style, so this is a real, easy-to-silently-break invariant; comment it loudly in both files, pointing at each other.
141: 
142: ### E.2 Camera controls — full key scheme (ported from mutaclysm's `ops/camera_control.c`)
143: 
144: | camera_mode | q/e (yaw) | r/t (pitch) | w/a/s/d (pan) | c/v (z-level) | f (reset) |
145: |---|---|---|---|---|---|
146: | 1 — first person | ±10° | ±10°, clamp[-89,89] | ❌ locked to anchor | ❌ | yaw=180,pitch=6 |
147: | 2 — third person | ±10° | ±10° | ❌ locked to anchor | ❌ | yaw=180,pitch=6 |
148: | 3 — free roam | ±10° | ±10° | ✅ pans z/x | ✅ | pan=0,yaw=180,pitch=-90 |
149: | 4 — bird's-eye | ❌ no-op | ❌ no-op | ✅ pans y/x, ABSOLUTE coords | ✅ | pan **centers on your anchor/selector**, NOT (0,0) |
150: 
151: `'0'` toggles `render_mode` (bidirectional). `'1'`-`'4'` switch `camera_mode`, only while `render_mode==1`. **Real bug already fixed**: mode 4's default/reset pan must center on your widget's own selector position (matching where the 2D view starts), not `(0,0)` (the board's corner) — both the `'1'-'4'` switch handler and your render op's own read-side default need this.
152: 
153: ### E.3 Camera basis math — two empirically-verified branches, do not collapse to one formula
154: 
155: ```c
156: Vec3 dir = { cos(pitch)*sin(yaw), sin(pitch), cos(pitch)*cos(yaw) };
157: forward = normalize(dir);
158: Vec3 right, up;
159: if (fabs(forward.y) > 0.999) {                 // near-vertical (bird's-eye-like)
160:     Vec3 fallback_up = { 0, 0, 1 };
161:     right = normalize(cross(fallback_up, forward));
162:     up    = normalize(cross(right, forward));
163: } else {                                        // general/near-horizontal (FPS-style)
164:     Vec3 world_up = { 0, 1, 0 };
165:     right = normalize(cross(world_up, forward));
166:     up    = normalize(cross(forward, right));
167: }
168: ```
169: Two earlier single-formula attempts each fixed one branch while silently breaking the other (a horizontal mirror in one case, upside-down in the other) — this is a well-known top-down-vs-forward-facing camera convention ambiguity, not something you can derive correctly by pure algebra. **Verify empirically** (§C, controlled test board with asymmetric corner markers + direct pixel sampling) before trusting any camera math, including this, if ported to a different coordinate convention.
170: 
171: ### E.4 Real raymarching, not a rasterizer
172: 
173: A **rasterizer** (project 3D geometry to screen space, sort back-to-front, fill polygons with solid colors) is fundamentally different from, and visually inferior to, real raymarching — and was explicitly rejected in this house once asked directly ("did we do the real raymarching or cheat?"). **If asked for "real 3D" or "like mutaclysm," build the raymarcher.** Real raymarching = a per-pixel loop casting a ray through 3D space, testing it against actual geometry via an Amanatides & Woo grid-DDA walk, sampling real texture data at the hit point. Port these four pieces verbatim from mutaclysm's `ops/compose_rgb_frame.c`:
174: 1. **`ray_aabb_hit_3d()`** — slab-method ray/box intersection, self-contained.
175: 2. **`box_face_uv()`** — hit point + face index → 2D texture UV.
176: 3. **`get_voxel8_cached()`/`sample_voxel8_pixel()`** — real voxel-texture CSV loading/sampling (resolution-agnostic despite the "8" in the name; size your pixel buffer for your assets' actual resolution).
177: 4. **The DDA loop** — per pixel, `ray_dir = normalize(forward + right*a + up*b)` where `a=(sx-W/2)/focal, b=(H/2-sy)/focal`, then Amanatides & Woo grid-step testing `ray_aabb_hit_3d` per cell until first hit.
178: 
179: This house's own board-viewer simplified mutaclysm's floor-pass/wall-raymarch split into ONE unified per-cell AABB test (thin slab for flat/water terrain, real tall box for raised terrain) — reasonable if your terrain model is "one scalar height per cell," not mutaclysm's richer walkable/furniture registry. **Performance**: a 640×480 per-pixel raymarch against a ~14×14 board rendered in ~70ms — fast enough for every idle tick; verify your own timing before assuming this holds at a different scale.
180: 
181: ### E.5 Real textures via the on-demand emoji-voxel pipeline — don't hand-pick colors
182: 
183: `system/chtpm_rgb_render.c` (wsr-pal's copy) has a genuine, working "GENERIC ON-DEMAND EMOJI GENERATION" path: any unrecognized UTF-8 codepoint gets shelled out to `ops/+x/emoji_gen_atlas.+x <emoji> <tmp.png>` (real FreeType + `NotoColorEmoji.ttf`) then `emoji_xtract.+x <tmp.png> 0 16 <csv>`, cached forever at `pieces/registry/emoji_assets/<hex-codepoint>/voxels_16.csv`. If your 2D compose op emits real emoji per terrain type, those exact voxel assets are already on disk by the time you build a 3D view — point your raymarcher's texture sampler at the same hex-codepoint path your 2D glyph-mapping uses, and 2D/3D show consistent textures for free. Also check `101.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️️+18.01/pieces/registry/emoji_assets/` for existing hand-curated assets (`t_grass`, `t_tree`, `t_water_dp`, `hero`, `zombie`, `xlector`, etc.) worth reusing directly.
184: 
185: ---
186: 
187: ## F. Pitfalls — every one a real, live-caught bug
188: 
189: 1. **A dynamically-generated piece.pdl METHOD row can never trigger `onClick="INTERACT"`.** §D.2.
190: 2. **`(key-'0')-1` is CORRECT for standard `${piece_methods}` numbered dispatch — do not "fix" it there.** §A.3 has the full, corrected writeup after a real mistake was made and caught this session. Only reading raw keys OUTSIDE that system needs the plain `key-'0'` form.
191: 3. **`'q'` must never quit anything** — a standing house rule, already enforced in `chtpm_parser_pal.c`/`keyboard_input.c`, but `gl_mirror.c` (`014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/system/gl_mirror.c`) had a leftover exception (remapped Ctrl+C to literal `'q'`, forwarded it as content, THEN quit on it) — fixed at the source this session; verify the fix is still present before assuming `'q'` is safe to bind to anything real.
192: 4. **Camera orientation bugs are not obvious from code review** — verify empirically, every time. §E.3, §C.
193: 5. **3D view's default camera position must match where the 2D view starts** (board center/selector default), not `(0,0)`.
194: 6. **Don't unconditionally pad rendered cells with literal spaces** — the font rasterizer already advances by real pixel width per glyph; unconditional padding produces a visible, unwanted gap. Only add extra characters (like a selector bracket) around genuinely-different cells.
195: 7. **A state file never explicitly symlinked in `button.sh` writes silently into the ephemeral session dir and vanishes.** §A.2. This exact bug class has recurred across multiple unrelated projects.
196: 8. **Cross-session state contamination** via shared, non-session-scoped state files. §C.
197: 9. **`pkill` may be sandboxed/blocked**; use `ps aux | grep` + `kill <pid>`. §C.
198: 10. **A running session's `.pal` script loads once at startup, not per-tick** — op *binary* rebuilds apply live (symlinked `ops/`), but editing the `.pal` script's own call sequence needs a real session restart to take effect.
199: 11. **`system() + &` does not create a new process group** — `setsid` is required when spawning a widget you want genuinely independent of the spawning process's own signal/timeout handling.
200: 12. **Never swap `chtpm_rgb_render` for mutaclysm's own fork casually** — it lacks the generic on-demand emoji-generation feature wsr-pal's copy has, and swapping to it silently breaks 2D emoji rendering while "fixing" 3D overlay support that wsr-pal's copy already had all along. Verify feature presence directly (`grep`) rather than trusting an earlier research pass's conclusion — one was wrong about this exact thing this session.
201: 13. **Doc comments can be stale** — verify against real code before trusting a comment's claim about current behavior, especially anything load-bearing. §A.4.
202: 
203: ---
204: 
205: ## G. When unsure whether to deviate from precedent (mutaclysm, or any other reference project)
206: 
207: **Ask, don't guess.** Standing instruction, repeated directly multiple times: *"trust mutaclysm and ask me if you're unsure that you may have false assumptions when you want to deviate for parity."* Concretely:
208: - If a reference project has a real, working mechanism for something you need, go read the actual code before designing your own — don't assume a simpler-looking approach is equivalent (§D.2's `onClick="INTERACT"` mistake is the canonical example of this going wrong).
209: - If you genuinely need to simplify relative to the reference (e.g. a single-scalar-height terrain model vs. a full walkable/furniture registry), that's fine — say so explicitly as a deliberate, scoped-down choice, not an oversight.
210: - If the reference project's own code has a real, discovered inconsistency (its own comment claims parity between two code paths that don't actually match), don't silently propagate the bug — flag it, and target the STATED intent, not the buggy implementation.
211: - Never invent a screen/menu/interaction pattern from scratch when real precedent exists somewhere in this house — read it first.
212: 
213: ---
214: 
215: ## H. Index — companion docs and where things live
216: 
217: 1. `@.apps/BOARD_WIDGET_ARCHITECTURE.md` — widget process architecture in depth (spawn/focus, real-time sync, command-bus pattern, drag-and-drop re-pairing plan).
218: 2. `&.widgits/5-pov-widgit.md` — full camera/POV/extrusion/emoji rendering model, with citations.
219: 3. `&.widgits/interact-fix-widget.txt` — the `onClick="INTERACT"` investigation in full.
220: 4. `&.widgits/view-vs-muta.md` — the rgb_frame.raw race + camera orientation bug investigations, in full.
221: 5. `&.widgits/BOARD_WIDGET_PROGRESS.txt` — full chronological build log, every bug in the order found — read this for the *story*, not just conclusions.
222: 6. `&.widgits/WIDGIT_BIBLE.md` — pre-existing widget conventions doc (two launch profiles, file-mediated IPC, xyzfs discovery — largely superseded/expanded by the docs above for anything GL/3D-specific, but still the reference for the base widget contract).
223: 7. `2.muchi-verse/GRAND-ARCHITECTURE.md` — the house's own governing constraints (e.g. "only `gl_mirror.c` may call GL/GLUT primitives, everything else stays portable C" — referenced but not independently re-verified this session, treat as authoritative).
224: 8. `101.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️️+18.01/` — the reference project for real camera/raymarch/voxel-texture code; also has its own `extrude-emoji.md` bug report (documents a real, still-unfixed gap: entities don't get raymarch-extruded there, only walls/terrain do).
225: 9. `014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/` — canonical source for the shared engine binaries (`prisc+x`, `chtpm_parser_pal`, `chtpm_rgb_render`, `gl_mirror`, `keyboard_input`, `renderer`) and the on-demand emoji-generation ops.
226: 
227: **Not covered here** (known to exist, not independently investigated this session — treat as open index entries, go read them if the work touches these areas): the `cli_io` field-key-injection mechanic (digit-jump→Enter activates, type, ESC deactivates — referenced in memory as a distinct, real mechanic from the numbered-button dispatch this doc covers), the xyzfs ledger/login system (widgets in this doc's scope deliberately skip it — no user login means ledger calls hard-exit), drag-and-drop project re-pairing (explicitly deferred/unbuilt, see `BOARD_WIDGET_ARCHITECTURE.md` §8), and the broader multi-project family structure beyond what was directly touched building this widget.

(End of file - total 227 lines)
</content>

278:       registered.

1: #!/bin/bash
2: # button.sh - launcher for user-pal, modeled directly on pal-forum's
3: # own button.sh (real interact+module chtpm pattern). Session-isolated
4: # from day one (USER-PAL-STANDARD.txt sec. 3) - built AFTER session
5: # isolation was already proven in pal-forum, so this is the correct
6: # starting shape, not a retrofit.
7: ACTION="${1:-help}"
8: SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
9: 
10: case "$ACTION" in
11:     compile|c|build)
12:         bash "$SCRIPT_DIR/scripts/build.sh"
13:         ;;
14:     run|r|start)
15:         cd "$SCRIPT_DIR"
16:         # SESSION ISOLATION (xyzos-standards.txt sec. 23, USER-PAL-STANDARD.txt
17:         # sec. 3) - every "run" gets a private, throwaway directory for
18:         # its own ephemeral UI state (keyboard history, interact_relay,
19:         # gui_state), deleted on exit.
20:         #
21:         # SHARED, PERSISTENT, NEVER SESSION-SCOPED (symlinked in, never
22:         # copied, never deleted on exit): users/ (the real identity
23:         # registry) and current_login.txt (USER-PAL-STANDARD.txt sec. 2
24:         # - deliberately NOT like pal-forum's own net/session.txt; this
25:         # file must outlive any one session, or "save logged in user
26:         # data in one place" would mean nothing the moment a terminal
27:         # closes).
28:         SESSION_ID="$(date +%s)-$$"
29:         SESSION_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/sessions/$SESSION_ID"
30:         mkdir -p "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/system" "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/display" \
31:                  "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/apps/player_app" "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/keyboard" \
32:                  "$SESSION_DIR/projects/user-pal/manager"
33:         mkdir -p "$SCRIPT_DIR/users"  # shared, real - not session-scoped
34:         # xyzfs/ is the multi-user filesystem: xyzfs/users/<uuid>/...
35:         # Shared + durable (never session-scoped). Created empty at first run;
36:         # per-user trees appear under users/<uuid>/ at signup.
37:         mkdir -p "$SCRIPT_DIR/xyzfs/bin" "$SCRIPT_DIR/xyzfs/users"
38:         touch "$SCRIPT_DIR/current_login.txt"  # ensure the symlink target exists
39:         # Ensure guest session.pdl exists until first real login
40:         if [ ! -f "$SCRIPT_DIR/xyzfs/session.pdl" ]; then
41:             cat > "$SCRIPT_DIR/xyzfs/session.pdl" << 'EOF'
42: SECTION      | KEY                | VALUE
43: ----------------------------------------
44: META         | piece_id           | xyzfs_session
45: META         | version            | 1.0
46: STATE        | mode                 | guest
47: STATE        | user_id              | 
48: STATE        | user_uuid            | 
49: STATE        | display_name         | Guest
50: STATE        | xyzfs_path           | 
51: STATE        | logged_in_at         | 0
52: STATE        | active_avatar_uuid   | 
53: STATE        | active_avatar_path   | 
54: EOF
55:         fi
56:         ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/xyzfs" "$SESSION_DIR/xyzfs" 2>/dev/null || true
57:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/system" "$SESSION_DIR/system"
58:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/ops" "$SESSION_DIR/ops"
59:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/pal" "$SESSION_DIR/pal"
60:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/default_op.txt" "$SESSION_DIR/default_op.txt"
61:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/chtpm" "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/chtpm"
62:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/projects/user-pal/pieces" "$SESSION_DIR/projects/user-pal/pieces"
63:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/users" "$SESSION_DIR/users"
64:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/xyzfs" "$SESSION_DIR/xyzfs"
65:         ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/current_login.txt" "$SESSION_DIR/current_login.txt"
66: 
67:         cd "$SESSION_DIR"
68:         : > pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt
69:         : > pieces/keyboard/history.txt
70:         : > pieces/display/userpal_screen_changed.txt
71:         : > projects/user-pal/manager/gui_state.txt
72: 
73:         cat > pieces/system/userpal_menu_state.txt << 'EOF'
74: last_message=Welcome to user-pal.
75: EOF
76:         cat > pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt << 'EOSTATE'
77: module_path=system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal
78: project_id=user-pal
79: active_target_id=login
80: EOSTATE
81: 
82:         export PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT="$SESSION_DIR"
83:         export PRISC_PROJECT_ID="user-pal"
84: 
85:         # PRE-SEED view.txt BEFORE chtpm's first load_vars/compose_frame.
86:         # Real bug (live-caught 2026-07-27 via login harness): chtpm_parser
87:         # runs compose_frame() once at startup; if pieces/apps/player_app/
88:         # view.txt does not exist yet it caches game_map="[Map Loading...]".
89:         # The persistent pal loop then writes a real view.txt and pings
90:         # frame_changed.txt, but that ping often lands before the main
91:         # loop samples last_display_pulse_size - so the marker never
92:         # "grows" again and the first paint stays Map Loading until the
93:         # next keypress. Humans and harnesses both saw this. Seeding
94:         # compose once here makes the first load_vars see real content.
95:         if [ -x "./ops/+x/userpal_compose_frame.+x" ]; then
96:             ./ops/+x/userpal_compose_frame.+x >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
97:         elif [ -x "$SCRIPT_DIR/ops/+x/userpal_compose_frame.+x" ]; then
98:             "$SCRIPT_DIR/ops/+x/userpal_compose_frame.+x" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
99:         fi
100: 
101:         ./system/renderer &
102:         RENDERER_PID=$!
103:         ./system/chtpm_parser_pal pieces/chtpm/layouts/login.chtpm >/dev/null 2>&1 &
104:         CHTPM_PID=$!
105: 
106:         # See pal-forum/button.sh's own identical comment: chtpm_parser_pal
107:         # has no SIGTERM handler of its own, so its own spawned module
108:         # (system/prisc+x) must be reaped by cwd match, not a bare
109:         # `pkill -f` (argv text is identical across every session).
110:         kill_own_module() {
111:             local pid cwd
112:             for pid in $(pgrep -f "system/prisc\+x" 2>/dev/null); do
113:                 cwd="$(readlink -f "/proc/$pid/cwd" 2>/dev/null)"
114:                 if [ "$cwd" = "$SESSION_DIR" ]; then
115:                     kill -9 "$pid" 2>/dev/null
116:                 fi
117:             done
118:         }
119: 
120:         # Deletes ONLY this session's own private directory - never the
121:         # real, shared system/ops/pal/users/current_login.txt (symlinks;
122:         # `rm -rf` on the session dir removes the symlink itself, not
123:         # its target).
124:         trap 'kill "$RENDERER_PID" "$CHTPM_PID" 2>/dev/null; kill_own_module; rm -rf "$SESSION_DIR"' EXIT INT TERM
125: 
126:         : > pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt
127: 
128:         ./system/keyboard_input
129: 
130:         kill "$RENDERER_PID" "$CHTPM_PID" 2>/dev/null
131:         kill_own_module
132:         ;;
133:     kill|k|stop)
134:         # NOTE: same blunt shape as every other project's own "kill"
135:         # debug action (pal-forum/pal-chain button.sh) - matches by
136:         # binary name only, so it is NOT session-scoped and NOT
137:         # project-scoped (a plain `pkill -f "system/prisc\+x"` matches
138:         # every project's own module, not just user-pal's). This is
139:         # fine for a manual debug helper a human runs deliberately; the
140:         # real "run" path's own exit trap uses kill_own_module() (cwd-
141:         # scoped) instead, never this. Do not use "kill" while another
142:         # project's own session might be running.
143:         pkill -f "system/keyboard_input" 2>/dev/null
144:         pkill -f "system/renderer" 2>/dev/null
145:         pkill -f "system/prisc\+x" 2>/dev/null
146:         pkill -f "system/chtpm_parser_pal" 2>/dev/null
147:         echo "done"
148:         ;;
149:     check|verify)
150:         for b in system/prisc+x system/keyboard_input system/renderer \
151:                  system/chtpm_parser_pal ops/+x/userpal_create_account.+x \
152:                  ops/+x/userpal_login.+x ops/+x/userpal_logout.+x \
153:                  ops/+x/userpal_whoami.+x ops/+x/userpal_menu_input.+x \
154:                  ops/+x/userpal_compose_frame.+x; do
155:             if [ -x "$SCRIPT_DIR/$b" ]; then echo "OK   $b"; else echo "MISSING $b"; fi
156:         done
157:         ;;
158:     help|h|-h|--help)
159:         echo "user-pal button.sh"
160:         echo ""
161:         echo "Usage: ./button.sh <action>"
162:         echo "  compile, c, build   - Build prisc+x + ops"
163:         echo "  run, r              - THE REAL PLAYABLE UI (interactive, needs a real terminal)"
164:         echo "  kill, k, stop       - Kill any lingering user-pal processes"
165:         echo "  check, verify       - Verify all binaries exist"
166:         echo "  help, h             - Show this help"
167:         ;;
168:     *)
169:         echo "Unknown action: $ACTION"
170:         exit 1
171:         ;;
172: esac

(End of file - total 172 lines)
</content>
```

1: #!/bin/bash
2: # button.sh - file-menu widget (ops + GL widget chrome)
3: # House pattern: session-isolated UI state + shared system/ops/pal.
4: # run-widget: GL-primary window for file operations against a focused editor.
5: # Profile: RUN_PROFILE=app (default) or RUN_PROFILE=widget
6: #   app mode:    ascii_renderer=1, keyboard_input foreground
7: #   widget mode: ascii_renderer=0, no keyboard_input, gl_mirror for GL + input
8: ACTION="${1:-help}"
9: SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
10: 
11: run_widget_session() {
12:     local FOCUS_SESSION="${1:-}"
13:     local PROFILE="${RUN_PROFILE:-}"
14:     # Auto-detect profile: headless (no DISPLAY) → app mode, else → widget mode
15:     if [ -z "$PROFILE" ]; then
16:         if [ -z "$DISPLAY" ]; then
17:             PROFILE="app"
18:         else
19:             PROFILE="widget"
20:         fi
21:     fi
22:     SESSION_ID="$(date +%s)-$$"
23:     SESSION_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/sessions/$SESSION_ID"
24:     mkdir -p "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/system" "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/display" \
25:              "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/apps/player_app" "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/keyboard" \
26:              "$SESSION_DIR/projects/file-menu/manager"
27: 
28:     ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/system" "$SESSION_DIR/system" 2>/dev/null || true
29:     ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/ops" "$SESSION_DIR/ops" 2>/dev/null || true
30:     ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/pal" "$SESSION_DIR/pal" 2>/dev/null || true
31:     ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/chtpm" "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/chtpm" 2>/dev/null || true
32:     ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/registry" "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/registry" 2>/dev/null || true
33:     ln -s "$SCRIPT_DIR/default_op.txt" "$SESSION_DIR/default_op.txt" 2>/dev/null || true
34:     mkdir -p "$SESSION_DIR/projects/file-menu/pieces"
35:     ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/projects/file-menu/pieces/file-menu" \
36:             "$SESSION_DIR/projects/file-menu/pieces/file-menu" 2>/dev/null || true
37: 
38:     cd "$SESSION_DIR"
39:     : > pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt
40:     : > pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt
41:     : > pieces/keyboard/history.txt
42:     : > pieces/display/fm_screen_changed.txt
43:     : > pieces/display/frame_changed.txt
44:     : > pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt
45:     : > pieces/system/fm_state.txt
46:     echo "$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd)" > pieces/system/house_root.txt
47:     cat > pieces/system/fm_state.txt << 'EOF'
48: path_buffer=
49: EOF
50:     cat > pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt << 'EOSTATE'
51: module_path=system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal
52: project_id=file-menu
53: active_target_id=file-menu
54: EOSTATE
55: 
56:     export PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT="$SESSION_DIR"
57:     export PRISC_PROJECT_ID="file-menu"
58: 
59:     # If no focus given, try ledger to find an active editor
60:     if [ -z "$FOCUS_SESSION" ] && [ -x ./ops/+x/ledger_peers.+x ]; then
61:         FOCUS_SESSION="$(PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT="$SESSION_DIR" ./ops/+x/ledger_peers.+x editor 2>/dev/null | head -1 | cut -d'|' -f1)"
62:     fi
63:     if [ -n "$FOCUS_SESSION" ] && [ -x ./ops/+x/fm_set_focus.+x ]; then
64:         ./ops/+x/fm_set_focus.+x "$SESSION_DIR/pieces/system" "$FOCUS_SESSION" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
65:     fi
66: 
67:     if [ -x ./ops/+x/fm_compose_frame.+x ]; then
68:         ./ops/+x/fm_compose_frame.+x >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
69:     fi
70: 
71:     # Start both ASCII renderer and layout parser (chtpm_parser_pal runs PAL, renderer prints ASCII)
72:     ./system/renderer &
73:     RENDERER_PID=$!
74:     ./system/chtpm_parser_pal pieces/chtpm/layouts/file_menu_main.chtpm >/dev/null 2>&1 &
75:     CHTPM_PID=$!
76: 
77:     GL_PID=""
78:     RGB_PID=""
79: 
80:     # Optionally start GL widget (widget mode or auto-detection)
81:     if [ "$PROFILE" = "widget" ] || ([ "$PROFILE" = "app" ] && [ -n "$DISPLAY" ]); then
82:         if [ -x ./ops/+x/ledger_append.+x ]; then
83:             PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT="$SESSION_DIR" \
84:                 ./ops/+x/ledger_append.+x ONLINE widget file-menu "$SESSION_DIR" $$ \
85:                 "FILE MENU" pieces/system/widget_cmds/inbox.txt >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
86:         fi
87:         # THREE-LAYER RACE FIX (re-applied 2026-07-30 — see
88:         # !.xyzos-pitfalls+1.txt PITFALL 54 and this project's own
89:         # widget-bug-fix-j29.txt under @.apps/text-editor-xyz/: this
90:         # exact wait step was documented as already fixed here once,
91:         # found MISSING again this session — regression, cause not yet
92:         # determined). chtpm_rgb_render's own initial render + baseline
93:         # pulse-size read must happen AFTER chtpm_parser_pal's first
94:         # compose_frame(), or rgb_frame.raw gets stuck permanently
95:         # all-black (the poll loop only re-renders on a pulse-size
96:         # CHANGE from its own baseline).
97:         waited=0
98:         while [ ! -s pieces/display/current_frame.txt ] && [ "$waited" -lt 20 ]; do
99:             sleep 0.1
100:             waited=$((waited + 1))
101:         done
102:         if [ -z "$NO_GL" ] && [ -x ./system/gl_mirror ]; then
103:             ./system/gl_mirror >/dev/null 2>&1 &
104:             GL_PID=$!
105:         fi
106:         if [ -z "$NO_GL" ] && [ -x ./system/chtpm_rgb_render ]; then
107:             ./system/chtpm_rgb_render >/dev/null 2>&1 &
108:             RGB_PID=$!
109:         fi
110:     fi
111: 
112:     kill_own_module() {
113:         local pid cwd
114:         for pid in $(pgrep -f "system/prisc\+x" 2>/dev/null); do
115:             cwd="$(readlink -f "/proc/$pid/cwd" 2>/dev/null)"
116:             if [ "$cwd" = "$SESSION_DIR" ]; then
117:                 kill -9 "$pid" 2>/dev/null
118:             fi
119:         done
120:     }
121: 
122:     trap 'if [ -x ./ops/+x/ledger_append.+x ]; then PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT="$SESSION_DIR" ./ops/+x/ledger_append.+x OFFLINE widget file-menu "$SESSION_DIR" $$ "FILE MENU" pieces/system/widget_cmds/inbox.txt >/dev/null 2>&1 || true; fi; kill "$RENDERER_PID" "$CHTPM_PID" "$GL_PID" "$RGB_PID" 2>/dev/null; kill_own_module; rm -rf "$SESSION_DIR"' EXIT INT TERM
123: 
124:     # Clear history and run keyboard input in foreground
125:     : > pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt
126:     ./system/keyboard_input
127: 
128:     kill "$RENDERER_PID" "$CHTPM_PID" "$GL_PID" "$RGB_PID" 2>/dev/null
129:     kill_own_module
130: }
131: 
132: case "$ACTION" in
133:     compile|c|build)
134:         bash "$SCRIPT_DIR/scripts/build.sh"
135:         ;;
136:     run-app|app|a)
137:         RUN_PROFILE=app run_widget_session "${2:-}"
138:         ;;
139:     run-widget|widget|w)
140:         RUN_PROFILE=widget run_widget_session "${2:-}"
141:         ;;
142:     run|r|start)
143:         run_widget_session "${2:-}"
144:         ;;
145:     focus|f)
146:         FOCUS="${2:-}"
147:         if [ -z "$FOCUS" ]; then
148:             echo "Usage: ./button.sh focus <editor_session_root>"
149:             exit 1
150:         fi
151:         mkdir -p "$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/sessions/live-focus"
152:         if [ -x "$SCRIPT_DIR/ops/+x/fm_set_focus.+x" ]; then
153:             "$SCRIPT_DIR/ops/+x/fm_set_focus.+x" "$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/sessions/live-focus" "$FOCUS"
154:         fi
155:         ;;
156:     kill|k|stop)
157:         pkill -f "system/keyboard_input" 2>/dev/null
158:         pkill -f "system/renderer" 2>/dev/null
159:         pkill -f "system/prisc\+x" 2>/dev/null
160:         pkill -f "system/chtpm_parser_pal" 2>/dev/null
161:         echo "done"
162:         ;;
163:     check|verify)
164:         for b in system/prisc+x system/keyboard_input system/renderer \
165:                  system/chtpm_parser_pal ops/+x/fm_compose_frame.+x \
166:                  ops/+x/fm_menu_input.+x ops/+x/fm_set_focus.+x \
167:                  ops/+x/fm_enqueue_cmd.+x; do
168:             if [ -x "$SCRIPT_DIR/$b" ]; then echo "OK   $b"; else echo "MISSING $b"; fi
169:         done
170:         ;;
171:     help|h|-h|--help)
172:         echo "file-menu widget — File browser for project operations"
173:         echo ""
174:         echo "Usage: ./button.sh <action> [editor_session]"
175:         echo "  run | r | start          - Auto-detect mode: ASCII if headless, GL if DISPLAY"
176:         echo "  run-widget | widget | w  - Force GL window mode"
177:         echo "  run-app | app | a        - Force ASCII terminal mode"
178:         echo "  run <session>            - Auto-detect mode, focused on specific project"
179:         echo "  focus <session>          - Pre-set project focus"
180:         echo "  compile                  - Build ops + system"
181:         echo "  kill                     - Kill processes"
182:         echo "  check                    - Verify binaries"
183:         echo ""
184:         echo "Modes:"
185:         echo "  ASCII (app mode): keyboard input foreground, renders to terminal"
186:         echo "  GL (widget mode): window mode, gl_mirror handles rendering + input"
187:         echo ""
188:         echo "Environment:"
189:         echo "  RUN_PROFILE=app          - Force ASCII mode regardless of DISPLAY"
190:         echo "  RUN_PROFILE=widget       - Force GL mode (default if DISPLAY set)"
191:         echo ""
192:         echo "Commands sent to focused project: NEW / SAVE / SAVE_AS:<path> / LOAD:<path>"
193:         ;;
194:     *)
195:         echo "Unknown: $ACTION"; exit 1 ;;
196: esac
