<path>/home/no/Desktop/🤖️🪤️🏠️/🥡️🪜️/🪜️-00.00/NNEST_CLEAN_PARENT/NNEST-11.15/x0.parent-level-dev-env-04.04/yz.muchiverse/44.xyz❤️‍🔥️00.10/#.haiku+/!.xyzos-standards+1.txt</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1645: Written per direct instruction, to solidify a reusable family-wide
1646: pattern rather than a one-off hack local to one project - any future
1647: project needing a pal op that acts on "whichever entity is currently
1648: selected" (not a fixed, single target like an owner's own wallet/user
1649: state) should use this, not reinvent it.
1650: 
1651: 21.1 THE REAL BLOCKER, CONFIRMED BY DIRECT CODE READ, NOT ASSUMED
1652: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1653: `ecall`'s own path/key arguments (`SYS_OPEN`, `SYS_GET_KV_INT`,
1654: `SYS_SET_KV_INT`) are COMPILE-TIME LITERALS baked into the instruction
1655: stream at parse time (`i->literal_arg`/`i->literal_arg2`) - a `.pal`
1656: script cannot compute or receive a path at runtime, only reference one
1657: already written into its own source text. Separately, `main(int argc,
1658: char **argv)` (confirmed by direct read, both `system/prisc+x.c` here
1659: and `shared-ops/prisc+x.c`) only ever recognizes 4 argv positions -
1660: `<prog> [mem_in] [mem_out] [ops_file]` - there is no argv[5]-into-a-
1661: register convention, exactly as PAL-VS-C-ARCHITECTURE.txt sec.6 already
1662: named as a real, still-open blocker. Together this means a general
1663: `toggle_sleep.pal <pet_id>` - the direct pal-native equivalent of
1664: `ops/toggle_sleep.c`'s own `argv[1]` - literally cannot be written today;
1665: the only precedent (`z0.zoo_0000/pal/ops_native/pet_pet_prototype.pal`)
1666: sidesteps this by hardcoding a single pet (`pet_mochi`) and says so
1667: plainly in its own header comment.
1668: 
1669: 21.2 THE PATTERN: A FIXED SCRATCH FILE STANDS IN FOR "THE ARGUMENT"
1670: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1671: One well-known, fixed path - `pieces/system/active_target.txt` - is
1672: what every per-instance pal op's own `ecall` literal always names.
1673: Whatever C op already knows which real piece is "current" (this
1674: family's own dispatcher shape - `chain_menu_input.c`'s own
1675: `get_current_piece_id()`, or a project's own equivalent tracking
1676: `selected_pet`/`active_target_id`) does three things around invoking
1677: such a pal op, all plain file I/O ("communicate via files, as usual" -
1678: direct instruction, matching this whole family's own "if it's not in a
1679: file, it's a lie" doctrine already applied everywhere else):
1680:   1. COPY IN: read the real piece's own `state.txt`, write JUST the
1681:      key(s) the pal op actually touches into
1682:      `pieces/system/active_target.txt` (not the whole file - keep the
1683:      scratch file narrow and disposable, not a shadow copy of the whole
1684:      piece).
1685:   2. RUN: `system/prisc+x pal/ops_native/<op>.pal` (a plain child
1686:      process, synchronous - this family's whole architecture is
1687:      already "dispatch, run to completion, then compose," sec.
1688:      0-CORRECTED - no new concurrency concern here since dispatch is
1689:      strictly serial per keypress).
1690:   3. COPY OUT: read the (now-mutated) key(s) back out of
1691:      `active_target.txt`, write them into the REAL piece's own
1692:      `state.txt` (preserving every other line, same `read-all/rewrite-
1693:      one-key` idiom every op in this family already uses), then
1694:      truncate `active_target.txt` back to empty - it is a disposable
1695:      scratch, never a piece of its own, never left holding stale data
1696:      between actions.
1697: 
1698: This keeps the actual DECISION/MUTATION genuinely in pal - `asleep = !
1699: asleep`, `poop_count = 0`, the token-deduct + hunger-clamp math - not
1700: just a thin pal wrapper around a C op that does the real work. The C
1701: side's job shrinks to pure file-addressing ("which real piece does
1702: 'active_target.txt' currently alias") - the "sys call" part of the
1703: system, matching the direct framing this pattern was built under: "the
1704: spirit of pal is to be easier than c programming so the user can edit
1705: it and drag and drop ops... all the hard stuff is under the hood in
1706: ops" - kernel/file-IO/GL-touching concerns (opening a pet's GL window,
1707: generating a PNG trading card, scanning a skill registry) stay real C
1708: "sys" ops; the actual per-instance gameplay RULES move to pal.
1709: 
1710: 21.3 RESULT MESSAGES ARE BUILT IN C, AFTER THE PAL OP RETURNS - NOT
1711:      INSIDE THE PAL SCRIPT ITSELF
1712: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1713: `SYS_WRITE_LINE` takes a compile-time literal string too (same
1714: limitation as sec.21.1) - a pal script cannot construct "Claimed 10
1715: tokens! Balance: 47" with a live number spliced in the way a C op's
1716: `printf` can. Don't fight this: the dispatcher already read the
1717: BEFORE value during copy-in and reads the AFTER value during copy-out,
1718: in plain C - it builds the real user-facing message itself from those
1719: two known-good integers once the pal op has finished, instead of
1720: asking the pal op to report on itself. This is an honest division (pal
1721: owns the mutation, C owns describing it to the player), not a
1722: workaround hiding a gap.
1723: 
1724: 21.4 WHAT THIS PATTERN IS NOT FOR
1725: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1726: Don't reach for this when the target is already fixed/singular (a
1727: project's own single wallet/user state, matching how `claim_tokens.pal`
1728: and `coin_flip.pal` operate directly on `pieces/world_01/map_lobby/
1729: user_01/state.txt` with no indirection at all - there is only ever one,
1730: its path never varies, so there's nothing to indirect). Only use
1731: `active_target.txt` when the SAME op must apply to different real
1732: pieces depending on what the player selected.
1733: 
1734: 21.5 RANDOMNESS: NO NEW SYSCALL NEEDED - THE DISPATCHER SEEDS IT INTO
1735:      THE SCRATCH FILE, LIKE ANY OTHER PRE-COMPUTED VALUE A PAL OP CAN'T
1736:      DERIVE ITSELF (REVISED - an earlier draft of this section added a
1737:      new `SYS_RANDOM` ecall instruction; reverted per direct feedback
1738:      before it shipped, see below)
1739: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1740: `coin_flip.pal` needs a random bit, which `ecall`'s table has no
1741: primitive for. The first draft of this pattern grew the syscall table
1742: (`SYS_RANDOM`) to cover it - technically legitimate per
1743: PAL-VS-C-ARCHITECTURE.txt sec.4's own "extend ecall when a genuinely
1744: good candidate needs a syscall the table doesn't have," but overkill
1745: here: direct user question, "cant it be done as an op that gives arg
1746: io? or is that something else?", surfaced a real, already-available
1747: path that needs ZERO VM changes at all.
1748: 
1749: Checked directly (not assumed): an ordinary custom-op call from a
1750: `.pal` script (`generate_egg`, `tick_pets`, any bareword call) runs a
1751: C binary and only ever ECHOES its stdout back through - `exec_custom_op`
1752: never writes a called op's result into a register (`regs[i->rd] =
1753: ...` appears exactly ONCE in the whole file, inside a special-cased
1754: `layout_op` branch unrelated to this). So a plain custom op genuinely
1755: CANNOT hand a value back to the calling script through the CALL itself
1756: - but sec.21.2's own indirection file is already the answer: the C
1757: dispatcher that already does copy-in/copy-out around every
1758: `active_target.txt`-based pal op can, for `coin_flip.pal` specifically,
1759: generate the random bit ITSELF (plain C `rand()`, same as
1760: `ops/coin_flip.c` always did) and write it into the scratch file as a
1761: normal key BEFORE launching the pal script - which reads it back with
1762: the ALREADY-EXISTING `SYS_GET_KV_INT` ecall, no new syscall, no VM
1763: core change, same file-handoff shape as every other value crossing the
1764: C/pal boundary in this pattern. `system/prisc+x.c` is unmodified by
1765: this pattern entirely - not just "reverted," never actually needed a
1766: core change in the first place once the file-handoff option was
1767: considered.
1768: 
1769: ========================================================================
1770: 22. THE REFACTOR STANDARD - HOW TO REFACTOR ANY PROJECT'S INPUT/RENDER
1771:     MECHANISM (folded in from the former standalone PAL-REFACTOR-
1772:     STANDARD.txt, 2026-07-19 - THIS FILE IS THE ONE DEFINITIVE PAL
1773:     STANDARDS DOC, direct user instruction: "there should be one
1774:     definative pal std document. use that one" - do not start a second
1775:     standalone standards file again; add a numbered section here
1776:     instead)
1777: ========================================================================
1778: Written after a same-day refactor attempt (egg-pals' own menu, redone
1779: to look like pal-chain's own UI) violated nearly every rule below and
1780: had to be rolled back by the user. Read this BEFORE touching any
1781: project's own input/render layer, not after.
1782: 
1783: 22.0 THE ONE RULE EVERYTHING ELSE FOLLOWS FROM: A REFACTOR CHANGES HOW,
1784:      NEVER WHAT
1785: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1786: "Refactor project X to use chtpm like wsr-pal/pal-chain" means: replace
1787: the MECHANISM (how a keypress reaches an action, how a screen change
1788: happens) with a proven, real one. It does NOT mean: redesign the
1789: screens, rename anything, change wording, split or merge menus
1790: differently, add new steps, or remove features you didn't fully trace
1791: through first.
1792: 
1793: If you cannot point to the EXACT existing line of C code (or piece.pdl
1794: row, or chtpm markup) that already produces a given screen/label/flow,
1795: you do not have permission to invent a replacement for it - go find the
1796: real line first. A refactor with a byte-for-byte identical user-visible
1797: result (same screens, same wording, same option order, same messages)
1798: and a completely different internal mechanism is a SUCCESSFUL refactor.
1799: A refactor that "improves" wording or restructures screens along the
1800: way is not a refactor - it's a rewrite wearing a refactor's name, and
1801: it will be rejected, because nobody asked for new behavior, just a
1802: sturdier way to produce the OLD behavior.
1803: 
1804: 22.1 BEFORE WRITING ANY NEW CODE: EXTRACT GROUND TRUTH FROM THE REAL,
1805:      CURRENT SOURCE - NEVER FROM MEMORY, A SUMMARY, OR A SIMILAR PROJECT
1806: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1807: Read the actual input-dispatch op and the actual render/compose op for
1808: the project being refactored, in full, before writing a single line of
1809: new code. Extract and write down (in your own scratch notes, not
1810: guessed while coding):
1811:   - Every screen name, in the exact order the code checks them.
1812:   - Every option's exact label text, exact position/number, and exact
1813:     resulting action (which op it shells out to, with which args).
1814:   - Every navigation rule (which key goes where, any global shortcuts
1815:     like a "back to main" key, any special-cased keys like quit).
1816:   - Every piece of live data displayed (a token count, a stat, a status
1817:     line) and exactly which file/field it's read from.
1818: 
1819: If a similar, already-refactored sibling project exists (e.g. wsr-pal
1820: when refactoring egg-pals), it is a reference for the MECHANISM only -
1821: its own screen names, wording, and menu structure belong to ITS OWN
1822: game and must never leak into the project actually being refactored.
1823: Copying a sibling's shape is right; copying its content is a mistake
1824: that produces a different game, not a refactored one.
1825: 
1826: 22.2 VERIFY THE MECHANISM AGAINST THE REAL REFERENCE SOURCE, NOT AGAINST
1827:      AN EARLIER PASS'S OWN COMMENTS OR ASSUMPTIONS
1828: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1829: This family's own docs have, more than once, stated something as "how
1830: real chtpm/fuzz-op works" that turned out to be wrong when someone
1831: actually re-read the real reference source
1832: (1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0102.0028/pieces/chtpm/plugins/chtpm_parser.c and
1833: projects/fuzz-op/manager/fuzz-op_manager.c). A confidently-worded
1834: comment citing "real chtpm's own architecture" is a CLAIM, not a
1835: citation, until you've opened the actual file and the actual line
1836: yourself. Before relying on any such claim (especially ones governing
1837: blocking/non-blocking behavior, synchronous vs. async dispatch, or
1838: which process does what), re-verify it directly. If it conflicts with
1839: what you find, the real source wins, and the stale doc gets corrected
1840: in place (marked superseded, not silently deleted - the record of what
1841: was wrong and why it looked plausible has its own value for the next
1842: person).
1843: 
1844: 22.3 THE REAL MECHANISM, IN ORDER OF PREFERENCE
1845: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1846:   a. Real `href` between screens - each screen is its own `.chtpm` file;
1847:      switching screens is a literal `<button href="...">`, handled
1848:      entirely inside `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s own `process_key()`. Zero
1849:      custom code for navigation.
1850:   b. Real `${piece_methods}` for a screen's own numbered action list -
1851:      reads that screen's own `piece.pdl` METHOD rows, generates real
1852:      `onClick="KEY:n"` buttons automatically. A dynamic-length list
1853:      (a variable number of rows depending on live data) is handled by
1854:      regenerating that one screen's own `piece.pdl` file before each
1855:      render, not by hand-drawing a cursor over static markup.
1856:   c. A persistent `<module>` process (`prisc+x` running a real,
1857:      LOOPING pal script) is the correct, intended shape for dispatching
1858:      those `KEY:n` actions to real one-shot ops and re-composing - this
1859:      is NOT something to convert to a one-shot script "to match real
1860:      chtpm." Real chtpm's own reference project (fuzz-op) is ALSO a
1861:      persistent daemon with its own independent poll loop; a
1862:      one-shot-per-keypress model is not the real pattern.
1863:   d. Real, hand-authored `<cli_io target_id="...">` elements for any
1864:      genuine free-text entry (a name, a password, an amount) - chtpm
1865:      buffers those keystrokes entirely inside its own process; no
1866:      module round-trip needed per keystroke.
1867:   e. `onClick="INTERACT"` ONLY for genuinely continuous, free-form key
1868:      relay with no discrete per-key dispatch possible any other way -
1869:      real-time map/cursor movement, essentially. A pure, discrete menu
1870:      (every action is a single numbered choice) should NEVER use
1871:      INTERACT - use (b) instead. When INTERACT genuinely is needed, its
1872:      own dispatch must be plain, fire-and-forget (matching real
1873:      `chtpm_parser.c`'s own `inject_raw_key()` exactly) - never a
1874:      blocking call that waits for the module to finish, which hangs
1875:      the instant the module is (correctly) a persistent loop rather
1876:      than a one-shot script.
1877: 
1878: 22.4 DO THE CUTOVER CLEANLY - NEVER LEAVE TWO PARALLEL SYSTEMS HALF-WIRED
1879: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1880: A refactor is either fully done or not started - there is no safe
1881: middle state where an old entry point and a new one both exist,
1882: pointing at shared files (the same persistent module script, the same
1883: default_op.txt) that only one of them was actually updated for. If a
1884: new op/layout set needs to coexist temporarily with the old one for
1885: testing, they must not share any mutable file the old system also
1886: reads or writes (a shared `<module>` pal script rewired for the new
1887: op names will silently break the old system's own entry point the
1888: moment anything still points at it). Verify old and new don't collide
1889: BEFORE building the new one, not after something breaks.
1890: 
1891: If you cannot complete a full cutover in one pass, stop at a clean
1892: boundary (all old files untouched and still fully functional) and
1893: hand off what's left as a written, explicit next step - do not leave a
1894: half-migrated project as the working tree's default state.
1895: 
1896: 22.5 VERIFY LIVE, END TO END, AGAINST THE REAL ORIGINAL - NOT A SPOT
1897:      CHECK
1898: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
1899: Before considering a refactor done:
1900:   - Launch the refactored version and the original (or a frozen,
1901:     known-good baseline copy of it) side by side if one exists.
1902:   - Walk every screen, every option, in the same order, via real key
1903:     injection (append `KEY_PRESSED: N` lines to the actual keyboard
1904:     history file the parser reads, matching real keystroke timing -
1905:     not a direct op invocation that bypasses the parser, which proves
1906:     the op works but not that the UI wiring does).
1907:   - Confirm the rendered text is IDENTICAL where it should be
1908:     identical (same screen titles, same option wording, same live data
1909:     format) - a visual diff, not "looks about right."
1910:   - Confirm process hygiene: exactly one live process for the module
1911:     after any number of screen switches (no leaked/duplicate
1912:     persistent processes, no zombies, no runaway relaunch storms).
1913:   - Confirm no flicker: writing the render output must have exactly
1914:     ONE writer (the parser's own compose step reading a project's own
1915:     `view.txt`-equivalent bridge file) - a project's own compose op
1916:     writing the final rendered frame file directly, in addition to the
1917:     parser also writing it, is a real, confirmed bug class (two
1918:     writers racing on the same file), not a style choice.
1919: 
1920: If any of these don't hold, the refactor is not finished, regardless
1921: of how much of the new code has been written.
1922: 
1923: ========================================================================
1924: 23. SESSION ISOLATION - IMPLEMENTED (pal-forum, 2026-07-19), NEXT: PORT
1925:     TO pal-chat-irc, THEN BACKPORT TO pal-chain. "KILL WHATEVER'S
1926:     RUNNING" WAS THE WRONG FIX FOR CROSS-SESSION CONTAMINATION - THE
1927:     REAL FIX IS PER-SESSION FILE ISOLATION
1928: ========================================================================
1929: THE MISTAKE, LIVE-CAUGHT (pal-forum, 2026-07-19): every project's own
1930: `button.sh run` writes its ENTIRE ephemeral UI/input state (`pieces/
1931: keyboard/history.txt`, `pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt`,
1932: `pieces/display/current_frame.txt`, `projects/<id>/manager/
1933: gui_state.txt`, etc.) to ONE FIXED set of paths under the project root -
1934: there is no per-invocation identity at all. An agent testing the same
1935: project via direct, detached background process launches (`setsid
1936: nohup ... & disown`, deliberately outliving any single tool call) left
1937: several stray `chtpm_parser_pal`/`prisc+x` processes running
1938: concurrently with the user's own real, separate `button.sh run`
1939: session - ALL of them reading and writing the SAME shared files. The
1940: user's own live typing got corrupted by an unrelated agent test's own
1941: old keystrokes replaying into their session. A first-pass fix (this
1942: file's own earlier draft) had `run` unconditionally `pkill` any
1943: pre-existing process for the same project before starting - REJECTED
1944: by the user: "we dont want to kill processes just because they are
1945: running. we only want to kill them if the user is done running them."
1946: Correct call - that fix punishes the exact legitimate case (two
1947: genuinely concurrent, intentional sessions of the same project) it
1948: should support, not prevent.
1949: 
1950: THE REAL FIX, USER-SPECIFIED (not yet built - this section exists so
1951: the intent survives to whoever builds it): every `button.sh run`
1952: invocation generates a session_id (e.g. timestamp+pid) and creates a
1953: dedicated session directory (`pieces/sessions/<session_id>/` or
1954: equivalent) holding ONLY that invocation's own ephemeral, mutable
1955: runtime state - keyboard history, interact_relay, current_frame,
1956: gui_state, quit_flag, and any other file a live session writes many
1957: times per second. The session_id gets passed as a variable (an env var,
1958: e.g. `PRISC_SESSION_ROOT`, read the same way `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT`
1959: already is) so N concurrent sessions of the same project never see or
1960: touch each other's files at all - no locking, no coordination, no
1961: "is anyone else using this" check needed, because there is genuinely
1962: nothing shared to collide on.
1963: 
1964: WHAT STAYS SHARED (deliberately, not an oversight): the actual
1965: PERSISTENT DATA a project's own ops read/write (pal-chain's `wallets/`+
1966: `data/blockchain.txt`, pal-forum's `users/`, any future project's own
1967: equivalent) is the real, single-source-of-truth database every session
1968: of that project should see and mutate together - it must NOT be
1969: session-scoped, or two windows of "the same user" would show different
1970: balances/posts. Likewise the STATIC definitions (`system/*` binaries,
1971: `ops/*`, `pal/*.pal`, `pieces/chtpm/layouts/*.chtpm`, `projects/<id>/
1972: pieces/*/piece.pdl`, `default_op.txt`) never change per-session and
1973: should stay read-only shared references, not copied per session.
1974: 
1975: IMPLEMENTATION, AS ACTUALLY BUILT (`pal-forum/button.sh`'s own `run`
1976: action, 2026-07-19 - not a hypothetical shape anymore): `SESSION_ID=
1977: "$(date +%s)-$$"`, `SESSION_DIR="$SCRIPT_DIR/pieces/sessions/
1978: $SESSION_ID"`. Inside it: real, fresh, private dirs for `pieces/
1979: system/`, `pieces/display/`, `pieces/apps/player_app/`, `pieces/
1980: keyboard/`, `net/`, `projects/<id>/manager/` - and `ln -s` symlinks
1981: back to the real project root for `system`, `ops`, `pal`,
1982: `default_op.txt`, `pieces/chtpm`, `projects/<id>/pieces`, and `users/`
1983: (pal-forum's own persistent database - the one deliberate exception to
1984: "everything in the session dir is private"). `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT` is
1985: set to `$SESSION_DIR` for every process this invocation launches.
1986: 
1987: A REAL SUBTLETY THAT COST A DEBUGGING PASS TO GET RIGHT: chtpm_parser_
1988: pal's own spawned module (`system/prisc+x`) is always launched via a
1989: RELATIVE `./system/prisc+x` argv (the symlink target text is identical
1990: across every session), so `pkill -f "system/prisc\+x"` CANNOT tell
1991: sessions apart by argv alone - it would kill every OTHER concurrent
1992: session's own module too, silently reintroducing the exact cross-
1993: session damage this whole section exists to prevent. Fixed by matching
1994: each candidate PID's own REAL cwd (`readlink -f /proc/$pid/cwd`)
1995: against `$SESSION_DIR` before killing it - cwd is genuinely unique per
1996: session (the whole reason `SESSION_DIR` exists), argv text is not.
1997: 
1998: Deleted entirely on exit (`trap ... EXIT INT TERM` does `rm -rf
1999: "$SESSION_DIR"` after killing only this session's own processes) -
2000: "starts a new" every launch, nothing to ever accumulate or manually
2001: clean up.
2002: 
2003: LIVE-VERIFIED, not just built (two genuinely concurrent sessions,
2004: typing DIFFERENT text into the SAME login screen's own `user_id_input`
2005: field simultaneously): session A's own `gui_state.txt` held exactly
2006: "alice", session B's own held exactly "bobby" - zero cross-talk.
2007: Quitting session A (kill its own scoped processes, `rm -rf` its own
2008: `SESSION_DIR`) left session B's own directory, process, and typed
2009: state completely untouched, and left the shared `users/` directory
2010: (real signup data from either session) intact and visible from the
2011: real project root.
2012: 
2013: ROLLOUT (updated, 2026-07-19 - supersedes the original "irc first"
2014: plan the user themselves reversed after this bug bit a live debugging
2015: session mid-investigation): pal-forum is done. Next: build pal-chat-irc
2016: with this pattern from day one (never the old single-fixed-path
2017: convention at all). Then backport the identical pattern into pal-
2018: chain's own `button.sh`. A prescriptive doc for applying this same
2019: pattern was also written directly into `z0.muchi-pals-refx-13.00/`
2020: (a DIFFERENT, in-progress agent's own refactor of muchipals) so that
2021: work doesn't have to rediscover any of this independently.
2022: 
2023: ROLLOUT UPDATE (2026-07-20): user-pal built (session-isolated from day
2024: one, USER-PAL-STANDARD.txt) and pal-chat-irc built (session-isolated
2025: from day one, PAL-CHAT-IRC-STANDARD.txt) - sec. 26/27. Backported to
2026: pal-chain same day (own button.sh `run` action rewritten to the same
2027: shape, PRISC_NET_ROOT pinned correctly from the start rather than
2028: needing its own retrofit). Live-verified: two real concurrent pal-chain
2029: sessions typing different wallet_ids ("alicewallet"/"bobwallet") held
2030: exactly their own value with zero cross-talk; quitting one left the
2031: other's process, directory, and typed state untouched, and the real
2032: shared `wallets/`/`data/` (blockchain.txt, pending_tx.txt) stayed
2033: intact throughout. user-pal auto-fill was NOT carried into this
2034: backport - it was built, worked, then REMOVED from both pal-forum and
2035: pal-chat-irc on direct user correction the same day (USER-PAL-
2036: STANDARD.txt sec. 6 has the full story) - so pal-chain's own login
2037: screen starts blank, matching the other two projects' own current
2038: (reverted) behavior, not the earlier auto-fill design.
2039: 
2040: Session isolation is now live in EVERY chtpm-native project this
2041: family has built: pal-forum, pal-chat-irc, pal-chain, user-pal. No
2042: project in this family currently still has the single-fixed-path
2043: sharing bug.
2044: 
2045: A SECOND REAL BUG, LIVE-CAUGHT while building pal-chat-irc (2026-07-
2046: 20), CAUSED BY THIS SECTION'S OWN FIX: `shared-ops/palnet_peer.c`'s
2047: own `resolve_presence_root()` defaults to "one level above
2048: `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT`" (PAL-NET-STANDARD.txt sec. 1) - correct back
2049: when that env var pointed at a project's own real `SCRIPT_DIR`, but
2050: session isolation (this section) now points it at `SESSION_DIR`
2051: instead, so "one level up" silently resolved to
2052: `pieces/sessions/net/presence` - a directory private to that one
2053: session's own parent, NOT the real, shared `<muchi-verse-root>/net/
2054: presence` every other project's own node discovers peers through.
2055: Confirmed live: a stray `pal-forum/pieces/sessions/net/presence/`
2056: directory existed on disk, meaning P2P discovery had been silently
2057: broken for every session-isolated `pal-forum run` since this section
2058: was implemented, and nobody had re-tested cross-node propagation since
2059: (the two-instance UI test done earlier in that same work only proved
2060: UI isolation, not networking - a real gap in that verification pass).
2061: FIXED by explicitly exporting `PRISC_NET_ROOT="$SCRIPT_DIR/../net/
2062: presence"` (palnet_peer.c's own override env var, already existed,
2063: just never set) right before launching `palnet_peer.+x`, in both
2064: pal-forum/button.sh and pal-chat-irc/button.sh. ANY project adopting
2065: session isolation that also launches `palnet_peer.+x` MUST set this
2066: env var the same way, or its own P2P mesh silently stops working the
2067: moment `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT` stops being the real project root.
2068: 
2069: MANUAL/AGENT TESTING GOTCHA - SESSION ISOLATION IS NOT AUTOMATIC, IT IS
2070: SOMETHING `button.sh` ITSELF SETS UP - REAL CONTAMINATION, LIVE-CAUGHT
2071: (2026-07-20, building pal-chat-irc's own room-list feature): session
2072: isolation is entirely a CONVENTION `button.sh`'s own `run` action
2073: implements (mkdir a `pieces/sessions/<id>/` directory, symlink the
2074: static/shared pieces into it, export `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT` pointing AT
2075: it, THEN launch `chtpm_parser_pal`/`renderer`/etc.) - `chtpm_parser_pal`
2076: itself has zero concept of "a session," it just reads/writes whatever
2077: `pieces/...` paths sit under whatever `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT` happens to
2078: already be when it starts. `button.sh` cannot be driven directly by an
2079: agent (it blocks on a real interactive `system/keyboard_input`, no
2080: tty), so live-testing requires manually replicating that same session-
2081: directory setup - and if an agent (or a human debugging by hand)
2082: launches a SECOND `chtpm_parser_pal`/`renderer` pair against the SAME
2083: flat, non-session-isolated directory (or forgets to kill the FIRST
2084: pair before launching a second one for a "fresh" test), both instances
2085: share the exact same `pieces/keyboard/history.txt`, `interact_relay.txt`,
2086: `gui_state.txt`, etc. and their keystrokes/dispatches interleave and
2087: corrupt each other - confirmed live: a stray, nonsensical `rooms/carol`
2088: directory got created (a username, not a room) purely because two
2089: overlapping test processes cross-contaminated which cli_io field was
2090: "active" and what got typed into it. This looked exactly like a real
2091: product bug for several minutes before being traced to the test
2092: harness itself, not the feature under test.
2093: 
2094: THE RULE FOR ANY FUTURE AGENT LIVE-TESTING THIS FAMILY BY HAND: EITHER
2095: (a) create a real, unique session directory per test instance (mkdir +
2096: symlink system/ops/pal/pieces-chtpm/default_op.txt/projects pieces +
2097: real per-instance ephemeral state, matching `button.sh`'s own `run`
2098: action line for line - see any project's own `button.sh` for the exact
2099: shape to copy), and export `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT` at that new directory
2100: before launching anything, OR (b) if testing against one flat,
2101: non-session directory for simplicity, ALWAYS kill every previous
2102: `chtpm_parser_pal`/`renderer`/`prisc+x` process for that exact
2103: directory (match by `readlink -f /proc/$pid/cwd` against the test
2104: dir, same technique `button.sh`'s own `kill_own_module()` uses) BEFORE
2105: launching a new instance there - never assume "it's a new test, it
2106: must be a fresh environment," always verify by checking `ps aux` for
2107: anything already running against that path first.
2108: 
2109: ========================================================================
2110: 24. LOCAL, PER-PROJECT COPIES OF SHARED-OPS FILES ARE A DELIBERATE,
2111:     TEMPORARY DEVELOPMENT-PHASE CHOICE - NOT THE PERMANENT ARCHITECTURE.
2112:     DO NOT FORGET TO REVISIT THIS.
2113: ========================================================================
2114: Direct user instruction, recorded here so it survives context
2115: compaction and isn't silently forgotten: during this family's own
2116: active development phase, every project keeps a fully self-contained
2117: LOCAL copy of any file that also exists in `shared-ops/` (`system/
2118: prisc+x.c`, `keyboard_input.c`, `chtpm_parser_pal.c`,
2119: `chtpm_rgb_render.c`, `ops/palnet_peer.c`) - synced explicitly via
2120: `sync_shared_op.sh <op_name> <project>/<target_dir>` from the canonical
2121: `shared-ops/` source, never referenced live at build or run time (see
2122: `shared-ops-manifest.txt`). This is intentional, NOT a rejection of
2123: sharing code in general: it exists so each project can be modified,
2124: tested, and even temporarily broken in isolation without risking a
2125: live cross-project dependency breaking every OTHER project's own build
2126: mid-session, and so each project stays genuinely "solo-shippable" (a
2127: real, standing requirement - each project should zip up and run
2128: independently).
2129: 
2130: THIS IS A DEVELOPMENT-PHASE CHOICE, NOT THE PERMANENT END STATE. Once
2131: this family's own shared mechanisms (chtpm_parser_pal.c's own INTERACT/
2132: cli_io/href handling, prisc+x.c's own opcode set, palnet_peer.c's own
2133: wire protocol) stabilize and stop needing frequent, project-triggered
2134: fixes (the kind found live, repeatedly, this same day - the
2135: run_module_synchronous() revert, the cli_io space-trimming bug, the
2136: href module-leak fix, the frame-writer-marker fix), the plan is to move
2137: BACK toward genuinely shared code - fewer local copies, more live
2138: references or a proper package/module mechanism - rather than N
2139: independently-drifting forks of the same file accumulating undetected
2140: divergence forever. Revisit this decision once the shared-ops churn
2141: rate drops, not on a fixed calendar date - the local-copy convention's
2142: own job is to absorb exactly this kind of active-development
2143: instability; once that instability stops, the reason for paying its
2144: own real cost (every fix needing an explicit sync pass to N projects,
2145: confirmed tedious and error-prone across this same session) goes away
2146: too.
2147: 
2148: REAL MISTAKE, LIVE-CAUGHT (2026-07-20, removing the 'q'-quits-
2149: everything bug from every project's own keyboard_input.c - sec. 23's
2150: own pitfall entry): ran `sync_shared_op.sh keyboard_input` against a
2151: batch of project directories, including `wsr-pal/system/`, WITHOUT
2152: first checking `shared-ops-manifest.txt`'s own explicit note (right
2153: there, sec. 1's own consumer list: "keyboard_input is wsr-pal's own
2154: local fork, NOT synced from here") - overwrote a deliberately-
2155: different file with the canonical one, no diff taken first, no backup
2156: kept. wsr-pal's own fork "genuinely differs on quit-key handling" (this
2157: file's own §1 entry on `shared-ops/keyboard_input.c`) - exactly WHAT it
2158: did differently is now unknown and unrecoverable, since the sync
2159: happened before anyone looked.
2160: 
2161: REQUIRED CONVENTION, effective immediately, for ANY `sync_shared_op.sh`
2162: call, no exceptions: BEFORE overwriting a target file, run a real
2163: `diff` against the canonical source and actually read the output -
2164: not just check the manifest's own consumer notes (do that too, but a
2165: note can be stale or a fork's own reasons forgotten/undocumented
2166: elsewhere) - the diff itself is the only thing that can't lie about
2167: whether a target file has drifted for a REAL, still-relevant reason.
2168: If the diff shows more than the expected/already-understood
2169: divergence, STOP and ask before overwriting, don't assume the sync is
2170: safe just because a project "should" be on the canonical version.
2171: `sync_shared_op.sh` itself does not do this check - the agent running
2172: it must, every time, manually, before confirming the overwrite.
2173: 
2174: ========================================================================
2175: 25. system/renderer.c MUST DEFAULT TO SCROLLBACK/HISTORY MODE, NEVER
2176:     CLEAR THE SCREEN EACH FRAME - REAL TPMOS'S OWN renderer.c DOESN'T
2177: ========================================================================
2178: THE BUG, USER-REPORTED AND FIXED (2026-07-19): every local copy of
2179: `system/renderer.c` across the whole family (3 independently hand-
2180: edited variants existed before this fix - confirmed by direct read of
2181: wsr-pal/pal-chain/pal-forum/zoo_0000's copies vs. z0.muchi-pals-refx-
2182: 13.00/egg-pals+13-og's own separate "reduce flicker" attempt)
2183: unconditionally printed `\033[H\033[J` (ANSI clear + cursor-home)
2184: before every single frame. This meant: no terminal scrollback of past
2185: frames, no scrolling up to read what just happened, no copy-pasting a
2186: prior frame, and visible flicker on every menu transition or even
2187: every keystroke while typing into a `<cli_io>` field. Direct user
2188: report: "instead of refreshing the screen each time it would simply
2189: show old frame in terminal and allow me to scroll up... fix the way
2190: things are rendered to read from frame the way fuzz-op does in
2191: 1.tpmos."
2192: 
2193: THE REAL FIX, ported directly from `1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0102.0028/pieces/
2194: display/renderer.c` (not guessed): that file has an `is_history_on()`
2195: check, reading `pieces/display/state.txt` - and it DEFAULTS ON (a
2196: missing file, or one that doesn't literally contain "off", both mean
2197: "on"). In that default mode, it prints 5 blank lines + a
2198: `--- FRAME UPDATE at <timestamp> ---` marker, then the raw frame
2199: content - NO ANSI clear, NO cursor repositioning at all, relying
2200: entirely on the terminal's own native scrollback to keep every prior
2201: frame available. The clear-screen path only exists as an explicit
2202: OFF-mode opt-out real fuzz-op itself basically never exercises.
2203: Ported this exact shape (same `is_history_on()` name/logic, same
2204: default) into `system/renderer.c`'s own `render_frame()`.
2205: 
2206: PROMOTED TO A REAL SHARED OP while fixing this (`renderer` added to
2207: `shared-ops-manifest.txt`, canonical source now
2208: `shared-ops/renderer.c`) - it had never been part of the tracked
2209: sync rotation before, which is exactly how 3 different hand-edited
2210: variants were able to drift apart undetected in the first place. Re-
2211: synced to pal-chain/pal-forum/wsr-pal/muchipal-editor-0.0/mutaclsym/
2212: zoo_0000/egg-pals-13, all rebuilt clean. Live-verified (a standalone
2213: test harness, two frames rendered in sequence): output contains zero
2214: ANSI escape sequences, both frames' own full content present in the
2215: captured stream, matching real fuzz-op's own scrollback behavior
2216: exactly.

2218: ========================================================================
2219: 26. USER-PAL - CROSS-APP IDENTITY/LOGIN REGISTRY - SEE

2275: ========================================================================
2276: 28. NAV MODE vs INTERACT MODE vs POSSESSION - THREE SEPARATE CONCERNS,
2277:     CONFUSED TOGETHER THREE TIMES BEFORE THIS DOC WAS WRITTEN (angler-
2278:     empires / muchi-civ-pal, 2026-07-21 - written from a DIRECT READ of
2279:     the real shared engine's own source, not inferred from a frame
2280:     transcript alone - see sec. 28.6 for the transcript-only mistakes
2281:     this corrects)
2282: ========================================================================
2283: Written per direct instruction ("document it first in standards" -
2284: before writing the fix code, not after) once the REAL mechanism was
2285: finally read directly: pieces/chtpm/plugins/chtpm_parser.c (the shared
2286: UI/render engine, ~3100 lines, read in full this pass) and
2287: projects/fuzz-op/layouts/fuzz-op.chtpm (both under
2288: 1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0102.0028/), not just the raw play-session frame
2289: transcript ($.4x/!.interact-vs-xelector.txt) that three earlier
2290: attempts tried to reverse-engineer the model from alone. The transcript
2291: is real evidence of BEHAVIOR; these two files are the actual
2292: IMPLEMENTATION that produces it - reading only the former and guessing
2293: at the latter is what went wrong three times running, see sec. 28.6.
2294: 
2295: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2296: 28.1 THE THREE SEPARATE THINGS, NAMED PRECISELY
2297: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2298: 1. NAV MODE - a plain digit/Enter index-jump over a NUMBERED LIST of
2299:    real METHOD rows (this family's own pre-existing action-bar pattern,
2300:    e.g. ops/choice.c's action_cursor/digit_accum - unchanged by this
2301:    section, already correct everywhere it already existed). Marker
2302:    `[>]` in chtpm's own rendering (render_element()'s `pref` variable,
2303:    chtpm_parser.c ~line 2373-2379).
2304: 2. INTERACT MODE (chtpm's own name: `is_map_control`, stored in a
2305:    project's own session/state.txt, read back by
2306:    current_project_is_map_control_active(), chtpm_parser.c ~line 2289)
2307:    - a single on/off flag meaning "raw keystrokes currently route to
2308:    THIS project's own game-input pipeline instead of being intercepted
2309:    for NAV-list digit-jumping." ENTERED by pressing Enter on an
2310:    ORDINARY, REAL, NUMBERED nav-list row whose method/onClick is the
2311:    literal sentinel string "INTERACT" (fuzz-op.chtpm line 18:
2312:    `<button label="Control Map" onClick="INTERACT" />` - NOT a
2313:    dedicated keybinding like 'i'/'I', NOT a synthetic single-item list
2314:    invented outside the real method-row mechanism - it is exactly one
2315:    more row in the SAME numbered list every other real action already
2316:    lives in). EXITED by ESC (chtpm_parser.c ~line 2860,
2317:    `if (key == ESC_KEY ...)`), which pops back to NAV mode ONLY -
2318:    confirmed by direct read, ESC's own handler never touches any
2319:    piece-possession concept at all, it only manipulates active_index/
2320:    focus_index (chtpm's own NAV bookkeeping) and, via the project's own
2321:    ops (not chtpm itself), the project's own is_map_control field.
2322: 3. POSSESSION (fuzz-op's own project-local concept - chtpm_parser.c
2323:    has ZERO knowledge of this, confirmed by grep: "active_target_id"/
2324:    "possessed"/"xlector" never appear in chtpm_parser.c's own key-
2325:    handling code at all, only in var-substitution plumbing that reads
2326:    whatever the PROJECT already wrote to ITS OWN state) - which
2327:    specific piece (the free xlector cursor, or a possessed pet/unit)
2328:    currently receives movement/method-dispatch WHILE interact mode
2329:    (#2) is on. Tracked entirely by the project's own ops, in the
2330:    project's own state.txt, under whatever field name that project
2331:    picks (this family's own convention going forward: `possessed_id`,
2332:    "none" meaning the free cursor).
2333: 
2334: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2335: 28.2 THE ONE RULE THAT MATTERS MOST - #2 AND #3 NEVER TOUCH EACH OTHER
2336: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2337: Toggling interact mode OFF (ESC, #2) does NOT reset, clear, or
2338: otherwise touch possession (#3) - confirmed by direct read of chtpm's
2339: own ESC handler, which has no code path that could touch a project-
2340: local field it doesn't even know the name of. Re-entering interact mode
2341: (Enter on the "interact" nav row again) resumes control of WHATEVER
2342: possessed_id already was - if a unit was possessed before the last ESC,
2343: it's still possessed after re-entering, with zero extra steps. Relinquishing
2344: possession (returning control to the free cursor specifically) is its
2345: own SEPARATE action - in fuzz-op's own real xlector/piece.pdl-driven
2346: design, this is just another ordinary numbered METHOD dispatched via
2347: the SAME mechanism as any other verb (matches key '9' in the reference
2348: transcript producing "Returned to Xlector." while [ACTIVE] flips from
2349: pet_01 back to xlector, with interact mode itself never leaving `[^]`
2350: across that same transition - direct transcript evidence, now correctly
2351: read in light of the source above rather than guessed at cold).
2352: 
2353: Concretely, per-project fields (this family's naming convention from
2354: here forward - civ-pal/angler-empires are the first adopters):
2355:   interact_mode (0/1) - #2 above. 0 = NAV, dispatch against whichever
2356:     piece_id normally drives the top-level action bar (this project's
2357:     own pre-existing convention, e.g. "hero"). 1 = ACTIVE, dispatch
2358:     against whichever piece_id possession (#3) currently names.
2359:   possessed_id (string, "none" or an instance id) - #3 above. Written
2360:     ONLY by (a) a "possess"-flavored method on the free cursor's own
2361:     piece.pdl (fuzz-op's own "scan" - examine-and-possess-on-a-match,
2362:     matching examine_at()'s own already-correct behavior in this
2363:     family's own ports) or (b) a "release"-flavored method on the
2364:     currently-possessed piece's own piece.pdl. NEVER written by
2365:     anything that also touches interact_mode - if a code change ever
2366:     makes ESC or the interact-mode-entry method also reset
2367:     possessed_id, that change is wrong, full stop, re-read this section.
2368: 
2369: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2370: 28.3 HOW A ROW BECOMES "THE INTERACT MODE ROW" - A REAL METHOD, NOT A
2371:      BOUND KEY
2372: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2373: chtpm's own convention: any nav-list element whose onClick attribute is
2374: the literal string "INTERACT" gets this behavior automatically -
2375: ordinary digit-jump/Enter, no different from any other row, EXCEPT its
2376: Enter handler is chtpm's own built-in special case (chtpm_parser.c
2377: ~line 2846: `else if (strcmp(el->onClick, "INTERACT") == 0) {
2378: active_index = focus_index; ...}`) instead of exec'ing an external
2379: command. This family's own projects (civ-pal, angler-empires, ...) have
2380: no chtpm layout at all (PAL-CRAFT-DESIGN.txt's own dox already
2381: documents this architectural difference - "uses its own PDL METHOD-
2382: table action bar instead... CHTPM is unnecessary over-engineering for a
2383: single-game project like this one," a decision that still stands, not
2384: reversed by this section) - the faithful, proportionate adaptation is:
2385: add a real METHOD row named "interact" to whichever piece.pdl already
2386: drives the NAV-mode action bar (e.g. hero/piece.pdl), and have the
2387: project's OWN digit/Enter dispatcher special-case that METHOD NAME the
2388: same way it already special-cases other named methods by string match
2389: (this family's own pre-existing "craft"/"examine" panel-opening special
2390: cases in ops/choice.c are the exact same pattern, already proven, nothing
2391: new invented) - setting interact_mode=1 instead of exec'ing an external
2392: handler. A placeholder handler path in the piece.pdl row is fine (never
2393: actually executed, matches the existing craft/examine rows' own
2394: convention of a METHOD row whose handler is effectively decorative once
2395: special-cased).
2396: 
2397: Do NOT bind interact-mode entry to a dedicated raw key (e.g. 'i'/'I') -
2398: three earlier attempts at this exact fix did that, and it was wrong
2399: each time (direct user correction, verbatim: "interact mode isn't an
2400: 'i' selectable. its another indexed nav #1. there are other nav options
2401: on menu."). The "other nav options" are simply whatever OTHER real
2402: METHOD rows already exist (or get added later) on the same piece.pdl -
2403: this mechanism composes naturally with them for free, precisely because
2404: "interact" is an ordinary row and not a special parallel system.
2405: 
2406: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2407: 28.4 MOVEMENT ROUTING WHILE INTERACT MODE IS ON
2408: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2409: While interact_mode==0 (NAV), arrow keys are a pure no-op for game
2410: movement - there is no hero avatar to move in this control model (see
2411: angler-empires's own draft-3 correction, ANGLER-EXPANSES-DESIGN.txt),
2412: and NAV mode's own digit/Enter dispatch is a completely separate input
2413: path (choice.c) from movement (move_player.c) - the two ops already run
2414: independently every tick in this family's engine, this section doesn't
2415: change that split.
2416: While interact_mode==1 (ACTIVE), arrow keys move whichever piece
2417: possession (#3) currently names: the free cursor (possessed_id="none",
2418: free/uncollided movement, exactly this family's own pre-existing
2419: xlector-cursor convention) or a possessed unit (possessed_id names a
2420: real instance, walkable-checked movement against that instance's own
2421: state.txt, same shape as any other piece's own movement).
2422: 
2423: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2424: 28.5 WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT LEVEL OF PORTING - WHAT NOT TO COPY FROM THE
2425:      REFERENCE
2426: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2427: fuzz-op itself is NOT a standalone project the way civ-pal is - its own
2428: [ACTIVE]/is_map_control mechanics are additionally entangled with
2429: wraith-alpha_manager.c (a much bigger multi-window DESKTOP SHELL project
2430: fuzz-op happens to be hosted inside, confirmed by direct read of
2431: chtpm_parser.c's own current_project_is_map_control_active() comment
2432: block, which explicitly cites wraith-alpha_manager.c's
2433: dispatch_menu_index()/route_command() and a GL mouse-click path this
2434: family's own single-process TTY games have no equivalent of at all).
2435: The numbered "8. [Back to Selector (9)] / 9. [Exit to Menu]" footer
2436: rows visible in the reference transcript are that OUTER desktop shell's
2437: own always-present chrome (matches PIECECRAFT-WRAITH-ARCHITECTURE.md's
2438: own documented "indices 1-4 reserved for window chrome" convention for
2439: wraith-hosted projects) - NOT part of fuzz-op's own game logic, and NOT
2440: something a standalone project in this family (no desktop shell, no GL
2441: click routing) needs to replicate. Port the CONCEPT (sec. 28.1-28.4),
2442: not the literal multi-window plumbing it happens to run inside of.
2443:

1: # widget+plan.txt — File Menu Widget + Running-Process Registry
2: # House: 44.xyz  |  First widget: &.widgits/file-menu
3: # Primary consumer: 102.editor (AGY buffer / file_path)
4: # Pattern kin: #.drag-drop-test (file-mediated IPC), mutaclsym GL mirror
5: # UI LAW: xyzos-standards §35 — GL PRIMARY, ASCII SECONDARY
6: # Status: PLAN — revised 2026-07-27 (GL-first widgets, no second TTY)
7: # Date: 2026-07-27
8: 
9: ================================================================================
10: 0. THESIS (REVISED)
11: ================================================================================
12: 
13: **GL is how the user interfaces with the house.** ASCII/terminal is a
14: secondary surface (dev, harness, headless). Widgets are not "another
15: terminal you open" — they are **GL windows** spawned as a **widget
16: launch profile** of a program (ascii renderer off / nulled).
17: 
18: Widgets are small pal-native programs that **send commands** into a
19: **focused running program** through a file-mediated bus (drag-drop
20: kinship: exchange/xyzfs files, not sockets-first).
21: 
22:   Terminal A (optional): START_BUTTON or editor app (GL + maybe ASCII)
23:        |
24:        | user picks FILE in editor
25:        v
26:   file-menu process:  launch_profile=widget
27:                       gl_window=1   (PRIMARY UI — user clicks here)
28:                       ascii_renderer=0  (no TTY takeover, stdout /dev/null)
29:        |
30:        +—— cmds/inbox.txt ——> editor session (still running)
31:        +—— reads buffer_path / document_path from xyzfs registry
32: 
33: First widget: **file-menu** (NEW / SAVE / SAVE AS / LOAD + CHANGE FOCUS).
34: First host: **102.editor**.
35: 
36: Every future program should support the same **app vs widget** profiles
37: so any app can open any widget-capable project as a GL window.
38: 
39: See: #.haiku+/!.xyzos-standards+1.txt §35, PITFALL 49.
40: 
41: ================================================================================
42: 1. LAUNCH PROFILES (ALL PROGRAMS, GOING FORWARD)
43: ================================================================================
44: 
45:   PROFILE app (default `./button.sh run`)
46:     gl_window=1          # primary
47:     ascii_renderer=0|1   # secondary; often 1 in dev, optional in product
48:     registers in xyzfs runtime
49: 
50:   PROFILE widget (`./button.sh run-widget` or RUN_PROFILE=widget)
51:     gl_window=1          # REQUIRED — this IS the UI
52:     ascii_renderer=0     # do not fight parent TTY; renderer not started
53:                          # or stdout→/dev/null
54:     still: session files, current_frame.txt, rgb_frame.raw, frame_history
55:     registers as widget process; may receive --focus-pid / parent ctx
56: 
57: Editor FILE menu:
58:   spawn:  &.widgits/file-menu/button.sh run-widget
59:   not:    "open a second terminal and run file-menu"
60: 
61: START_BUTTON same-TTY handoff remains valid for **replacing** the loader
62: with a full app. It is **not** the model for widgets-alongside-apps.
63: 
64: ================================================================================
65: 2. DRAG-DROP KINSHIP (IPC, NOT UI)
66: ================================================================================
67: 
68: #.drag-drop-test proves **file-mediated, multi-process** IPC with GL
69: surfaces. Widgets reuse that IPC idea; UI law is stricter (GL primary).
70: 
71:   | Drag-drop                     | File-menu widget                      |
72:   |-------------------------------|----------------------------------------|
73:   | two processes + GL windows    | host app + widget process + GL window  |
74:   | exchange / drop files         | xyzfs runtime inbox + document paths   |
75:   | no shared memory              | same                                   |
76:   | harness asserts files         | harness asserts buffer/disk + optional rgb |
77: 
78: ================================================================================
79: 3. RUNNING PROCESS REGISTRY (xyzfs)
80: ================================================================================
81: 
82:   <login>/xyzfs/users/<uuid>/home/runtime/
83:     processes.pdl
84:     processes/<pid>-<slug>/
85:       meta.pdl
86:       cmds/inbox.txt
87:       cmds/status.txt
88:       heart.beat
89: 
90: meta.pdl minimum:
91:   project_id, display_name, install_path, session_path, kind, pid,
92:   capabilities (file_document,text_buffer,widget_capable),
93:   document_path, buffer_path, cmd_inbox, gl_window_title,
94:   launch_profile (app|widget), started_at
95: 
96: Register on run / run-widget; unregister on exit; GC dead pids.
97: 
98: KPI-R1: editor app run → registry entry <2s
99: KPI-R2: exit removes entry (or GC)
100: KPI-R3: guest-uuid tree works
101: KPI-R4: widget spawn also registers (kind=widget, parent_pid optional)
102: 
103: ================================================================================
104: 4. FILE-MENU PRODUCT SURFACE (GL WINDOW CHROME)
105: ================================================================================
106: 
107: Same information architecture as file-flow frames; **pixels in GL**, not
108: "must scroll a terminal."
109: 
110: Main:
111:   ACTIVE: <focused project document>
112:   FILE:   <path>
113:   1 NEW FILE | 2 SAVE | 3 SAVE AS... | 4 LOAD FILE... | 5 CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT
114:   status
115: 
116: CHANGE FOCUS PROJECT → focus layout (list **running** registry processes)
117: LOAD / SAVE AS → browser layout (DIR / [DIR] / [FIL] / BACK / confirm)
118: 
119: Input: GL key/mouse path (and/or same interact_relay from a non-TTY
120: keyboard source later). Harness may still inject keys via history files
121: without a visible ASCII renderer.
122: 
123: ================================================================================
124: 5. SHARED DOCUMENT / COMMAND BUS
125: ================================================================================
126: 
127: Editor sole writer of editor_buffer.txt.
128: Widget enqueues cmds only:
129: 
130:   NEW | SAVE | SAVE_AS:<path> | LOAD:<path> | PING
131: 
132: Editor idle (or tick) consumes inbox → status.txt ACK.
133: Widget GL shows status; does not need editor TTY.
134: 
135: ================================================================================
136: 6. STEP 1 — GL WIDGET + REGISTRY + EDITOR (USER-FACING)
137: ================================================================================
138: 
139: ### Phase 1.A — Process registry
140:   runtime_register / unregister / gc; editor hooks.
141:   KPIs: R1–R4
142: 
143: ### Phase 1.B — UI mode flags + run-widget on file-menu
144:   config: ui_primary=gl, gl_window, ascii_renderer, launch_profile
145:   button.sh run vs run-widget
146:   GL window shows FILE MENU chrome (rgb path); ASCII off in widget mode
147:   KPIs:
148:     KPI-G0: run-widget starts gl process, no interactive ASCII takeover
149:     KPI-G1: ascii_renderer=0 → no terminal frame spam / no TTY raw fight
150:     KPI-U1: GL or current_frame contains FILE MENU methods (harness via files)
151: 
152: ### Phase 1.C — Focus project (GL)
153:   focus list from registry; set ACTIVE
154:   KPIs: F1–F4 (empty list, list editor, select updates ACTIVE, back)
155: 
156: ### Phase 1.D — Cmd bus NEW/SAVE/LOAD with live editor
157:   KPIs: E1–E5 (load/save/new; no focus error; one pulse per action)
158: 
159: ### Phase 1.E — File browser LOAD/SAVE AS
160:   KPIs: B1–B5
161: 
162: ### Phase 1.F — Editor opens widget
163:   Editor method FILE → spawn file-menu run-widget (not in-process stub long-term)
164:   KPI-W1: from editor app, FILE opens GL file-menu without second terminal
165:   KPI-W2: LOAD from that widget updates editor buffer
166:   KPI-W3: closing widget leaves editor alive
167: 
168: ### Phase 1.G — Polish
169:   GC, capability filter, proof harness, frame_history (files always)
170:   KPI-P1: demo_file_menu_widget.sh ALL PASS
171:   KPI-P2: idle frame markers stable (pitfall 48)
172: 
173: ================================================================================
174: 7. STEP 2 — APP PROFILE GL DEFAULT + HOUSE ROLL-OUT
175: ================================================================================
176: 
177: Not "add GL someday" — **normalize all programs**:
178: 
179:   - 102.editor `run` defaults gl_window=1 (ASCII secondary)
180:   - START_BUTTON / login: document migration path to GL-primary
181:   - Standard button.sh flags copied into project templates
182:   - Optional: widget chrome theming, multi-widget z-order
183: 
184: KPIs:
185:   KPI-H1: editor run shows GL without extra flags
186:   KPI-H2: template/README for new projects lists app vs widget profiles
187:   KPI-H3: standards §35 cited in new project button.sh help
188: 
189: ASCII-only remains valid for harness-only CI (display=:none).
190: 
191: ================================================================================
192: 8. NON-GOALS (v1)
193: ================================================================================
194: 
195:   - Requiring a second human terminal for widgets
196:   - Full desktop WM
197:   - Network process list
198:   - Xdnd file drag from file-menu (nice later; drag-drop already exists for pets)
199: 
200: ================================================================================
201: 9. RISKS
202: ================================================================================
203: 
204:   | Risk | Mitigation |
205:   |------|------------|
206:   | Headless CI no display | harness uses files + optional xvfb; ascii optional |
207:   | Two GL + one keyboard | widget uses own input path; document focus |
208:   | Buffer dual-write | editor sole buffer writer |
209:   | Session path dies | registry GC; widget status "focus dead" |
210:   | Agents re-introduce TTY widgets | PITFALL 49 + §35 checklist |
211: 
212: ================================================================================
213: 10. SUCCESS SNAPSHOT
214: ================================================================================
215: 
216:   1. User runs editor (GL primary).
217:   2. User activates FILE → file-menu **GL window** appears (no new TTY).
218:   3. CHANGE FOCUS if needed → editor selected.
219:   4. LOAD FILE browser in widget GL → editor buffer updates.
220:   5. Edit in editor → SAVE via widget → disk updates.
221:   6. Close widget GL → editor still running.
222:   7. Agents read frame_history / status files for proof.
223: 
224: ================================================================================
225: 10b. HARNESS PLACEMENT (this feature)
226: ================================================================================
227: 
228:   Unit (file-menu chrome / browser / focus UI alone):
229:     &.widgits/file-menu/test-harn-same/

235:   Integration (widget LOAD/SAVE against live editor):

237:     (widget owns the cross-story). If many hosts appear later, promote
238:     to house-level #.file-menu-integration-test/ like #.drag-drop-test.
239: 
240:   Law for agents: xyzos-standards §36.
241: 
242: ================================================================================
243: 11. DOC POINTERS FOR AGENTS

253: ================================================================================
