2445: 28.6 THE THREE WRONG ATTEMPTS THIS CORRECTS (kept here, not deleted, so
2446:      the same mistakes aren't repeated by a future session that only
2447:      skims this file)
2448: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2449: Full blow-by-blow (verbatim user quotes, my own investigation gaps
2450: admitted in detail) lives in $.4x/interact-fix-j21.txt - not
2451: reproduced in full here, but the short version: attempt 1 bound
2452: interact-mode entry AND exit to a dedicated 'i'/'I' key; attempt 2 made
2453: ESC also relinquish possession (conflating #2 and #3 above); attempt 3
2454: invented a synthetic single-item "Game Map" NAV list instead of a real,
2455: extensible METHOD row. All three were built by reasoning about the
2456: $.4x/!.interact-vs-xelector.txt frame transcript ALONE - real evidence,
2457: but not authoritative about implementation, which only reading
2458: chtpm_parser.c/fuzz-op.chtpm directly (this section) actually
2459: resolved. Lesson for any future case in this family where a reference
2460: project's OWN behavior needs porting: find and read the reference
2461: project's actual source before building a second, third, or fourth
2462: guess from its observed behavior alone - this was asked for directly,
2463: twice, before it was actually done.
2464: 
2465: ========================================================================
2466: 29. MULTI-MODULE chtpm_parser_pal.c SUPPORT, AND SIX LIVE-CAUGHT
2467:     GOTCHAS FROM BUILDING muchi-pal-agent's "*" PATH-COMPLETION PICKER
2468:     (2026-07-20/21)
2469: ========================================================================
2470: Full task was porting gem-dev's "*" wildcard completion into muchi-pal-
2471: agent, but as a genuinely live, auto-focusing, auto-closing "+-" tree
2472: picker (not gem-dev's flat 5-slot menu) - new files: manager/
2473: path_nav_manager.c, chat.chtpm's second <module> line. Along the way,
2474: several REAL bugs in the shared engine itself got found and fixed, not
2475: just this project's own code. Each is its own trap for the next person
2476: touching chtpm_parser_pal.c or building a second persistent helper
2477: process for any project in this family.
2478: 
2479: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2480: 29.1 <module> TAGS WERE SINGLE-SLOT, NOT MULTI - FIXED, NOW GENUINELY
2481:      CONCURRENT ("like html/js", direct instruction)
2482: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2483: launch_module()'s own guard (`cleanup_module()` before every fork) meant
2484: a SECOND <module> line in one layout silently KILLED the first one on
2485: every parse - confirmed live: chat.chtpm's own system/prisc+x got
2486: reaped the instant a second <module> for path_nav_manager.+x was added,
2487: with zero error surfaced anywhere. Fixed by adding a SEPARATE registry
2488: (`g_extra_modules[]`, `launch_extra_module()`) for every <module> tag
2489: AFTER the first: same liveness-guarded dedupe as launch_module()'s own
2490: guard, but never evicts a different slot to make room. The first
2491: <module> tag in a layout still goes through the original single primary
2492: slot completely unchanged (current_module_pid/current_module_path,
2493: run_module_synchronous(), cleanup_module() on href navigation - zero
2494: behavior change for every existing single-module project). Ordinal-
2495: tracked in parse_chtm()'s own tag loop (module_ordinal, 0-indexed: 0 ->
2496: primary slot, 1+ -> extra registry). While fixing this, ALSO fixed a
2497: pre-existing latent bug this exposed: the inner-text form of <module>
2498: (`<module>path</module>`, used by every layout in this family) was
2499: being double-dispatched - once via the TOKEN_TEXT branch, once via a
2500: redundant fallback inside the TOKEN_OPEN_TAG branch - previously masked
2501: because both calls hit launch_module()'s own single dedupe-by-path
2502: guard; would have broken ordinal counting for the multi-module case.
2503: Removed the redundant fallback. Ported to shared-ops/chtpm_parser_pal.c,
2504: 1.muchi-pal-agent's own copy, and 1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0102.0028/pieces/
2505: chtpm/plugins/chtpm_parser.c (the original reference implementation) -
2506: NOT yet propagated to the other sibling projects' own copies
2507: (pal-chat-irc/pal-chain/mutaclsym/etc.) - that's a separate sync pass,
2508: do it deliberately, not as a side effect of reading this section.
2509: 
2510: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2511: 29.2 NEVER cp A "SHARED" FILE WITHOUT DIFFING FIRST - LOCAL COPIES DRIFT
2512:      FOR REAL REASONS (see sec. 24), AND shared-ops/ CAN BE THE STALE ONE
2513: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2514: Live mistake, caught and recovered mid-session: assumed shared-ops/
2515: chtpm_parser_pal.c and 1.muchi-pal-agent's own copy were identical,
2516: `cp`'d shared-ops's version over muchi-pal-agent's own without diffing
2517: first. They were NOT identical - `diff` afterward (too late) showed
2518: real divergence. Recovered by diffing 5 OTHER sibling copies
2519: (mutaclsym/pal-chat-irc/pal-chain/user-pal/muchipal-editor-0.0) against
2520: each other first: all 5 were byte-identical to each other, meaning
2521: shared-ops/ itself was the outlier/stale one, and muchi-pal-agent (same
2522: family, same build.sh convention referencing "mutaclsym's own standing
2523: bar") had almost certainly matched that same family version before
2524: being clobbered. Restored from mutaclsym's copy, reapplied the intended
2525: edit on top of THAT instead. Lesson, restated from sec. 24 but worth
2526: repeating given how easy this was to get wrong live: `diff` BEFORE
2527: `cp`, every time, even (especially) for files this repo's own
2528: convention calls "shared" - the canonical shared-ops/ copy is not
2529: guaranteed to be the freshest one.
2530: 
2531: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2532: 29.3 gui_state.txt IS A FLAT, LINE-DELIMITED key=value STORE - NEVER PUT
2533:     A LITERAL NEWLINE IN A VALUE
2534: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2535: get_var()/load_state_file() read this file via `fgets()` per line,
2536: splitting on the FIRST '='. A value written with a real '\n' byte
2537: embedded in it (e.g. a naively-concatenated multi-line markup blob)
2538: gets silently truncated to whatever's before that first newline on
2539: read-back - the rest either vanishes or gets misparsed as bogus
2540: keyless lines. gem-dev's OWN g_menu_area (the precedent this project's
2541: own nav_menu was modeled on) never has this problem because it
2542: concatenates `<button ...><br/>` fragments with NO '\n' between them,
2543: relying purely on `<br/>` tags for visual line breaks - copy that
2544: convention exactly for any future dynamically-generated multi-element
2545: markup written into a gui_state.txt value.
2546: 
2547: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2548: 29.4 TWO SEPARATE PULSE FILES, TWO SEPARATE JOBS - PULSING ONLY ONE
2549:     LEAVES gui_state.txt UPDATES INVISIBLE FOREVER
2550: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2551: `pieces/apps/player_app/state_changed.txt` growth is what makes the
2552: main loop call `load_vars()` + `parse_chtm()` again - the ONLY thing
2553: that re-reads gui_state.txt and rebuilds the element tree.
2554: `pieces/display/frame_changed.txt` growth ONLY triggers a redraw of
2555: whatever tree is ALREADY parsed - it does NOT re-read anything. gem-
2556: dev's own pulse_state_changed() + trigger_render() (always both, never
2557: just one) is the precedent; an external process that writes a new
2558: gui_state.txt value and pulses ONLY frame_changed.txt will render
2559: forever from stale data with zero errors anywhere to indicate why.
2560: 
2561: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2562: 29.5 active_gui_index.txt vs active_index - ONE HAS A REAL SYNC PATH,
2563:     THE OTHER HAD NONE (had to add one)
2564: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2565: Focus (`focus_index`) has a real, working external-write channel:
2566: `pieces/display/active_gui_index.txt`, read back via
2567: `sync_focus_from_saved_active_index()` - BUT that function was DEAD
2568: CODE before this session (read `get_var("active_gui_index")`, a
2569: variable NOTHING in the file ever calls set_var() for - always empty,
2570: silent no-op for every project, gated to wraith-alpha layouts only on
2571: top of that). Fixed to read the file directly (matching gem-dev's own
2572: proven read_active_gui_index() pattern), and the wraith-alpha gate was
2573: removed as harmless (nothing depended on the previous no-op). Whether a
2574: cli_io is genuinely ACTIVE/typing (`active_index`, distinct from mere
2575: focus) had NO external write channel at all before this session -
2576: `export_active_index()` unconditionally overwrites
2577: `active_gui_index.txt` AND a companion `active_gui_is_typing.txt` from
2578: its own in-memory state every render, so an external process asking
2579: for deactivation has to go through the SAME growth-tracked-marker
2580: idiom as every other *_changed.txt file (added
2581: `pieces/display/force_deactivate.txt`, checked in the main loop right
2582: after the state_ch block, forcing `active_index = -1` and - critically
2583: - re-exporting UNCONDITIONALLY, not gated behind "if already active",
2584: or a stale export from earlier in the SAME pass never gets corrected).
2585: `active_gui_is_typing.txt` (1 = genuinely active/typing, 0 = focus-
2586: only) was already being written every render but never READ by
2587: anything - safe to poll externally as a plain state signal with zero
2588: engine changes.
2589: 
2590: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2591: 29.6 send_command()'s "KEY:n" WRITES THE DECIMAL ASCII CODE AS TEXT, NOT
2592:     THE LITERAL DIGIT CHARACTER - THE #1 GOTCHA FOR ANY EXTERNAL LISTENER
2593: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2594: `onClick="KEY:1"` -> send_command() converts 1 to its ASCII digit
2595: CHARACTER first (`'0'+1` = 49) -> `inject_raw_key(49)` -> writes
2596: `fprintf(fp, "%d\n", 49)` = the two-byte text "49", NEVER the single
2597: byte "1". This is true for EVERY key value inject_raw_key() ever
2598: writes, not just KEY:0-9. An external process parsing
2599: interact_relay.txt/history.txt for a specific key must decode the line
2600: as a decimal integer and compare against the ASCII value ('0'..'9' =
2601: 48..57), never compare the line's raw TEXT against a literal digit
2602: string. Live consequence of getting this wrong: an entire picker
2603: feature (path_nav_manager.c) tested as "working" via hand-written
2604: single-digit-character test files for two full rounds of Layer-1
2605: testing, while every REAL selection (real keyboard Enter, real mouse
2606: click, anything actually going through send_command()) was being
2607: silently rejected the whole time - passed testing, failed in real use.
2608: Generalizes: Layer-1 file-injection tests that hand-write the SAME file
2609: format the real dispatch path produces are only trustworthy if that
2610: format was itself verified against the real producer's code, not
2611: assumed from a struct name or convention.
2612: 
2613: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2614: 29.7 A SECOND PERSISTENT <module> NEEDS ITS OWN SESSION SYMLINK AND ITS
2615:     OWN REAP - button.sh's OWN bootstrap WILL SILENTLY OMIT BOTH
2616: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2617: button.sh's `run` action symlinks a fixed list of directories
2618: (system/, ops/, pal/, pieces/chtpm, pieces/registry, pieces/world_01,
2619: sandbox) into each fresh session dir - a NEW top-level directory a
2620: second module's binary lives in (here: manager/, containing
2621: path_nav_manager.+x) is NOT covered by that list and has to be added
2622: by hand. Missing it fails completely silently: launch_extra_module()'s
2623: own fork+execv has no output path on failure (`_exit(1)` in the child,
2624: nothing written anywhere), so the symptom is just "the feature does
2625: nothing," indistinguishable from a dozen other possible bugs, in EVERY
2626: test EXCEPT one that happens to symlink the directory manually (which
2627: is exactly how this stayed hidden through several rounds of manual
2628: test-session construction in this same session, each of which had
2629: manually added the missing symlink without realizing button.sh itself
2630: never would). `kill_own_module()` (cwd-matched process reaping on
2631: session exit) also only covered system/prisc+x by name - a second
2632: persistent module needs its own identical cwd-matched pgrep/kill block
2633: added there too, or it leaks as an orphaned background process on every
2634: single `run`/exit cycle. Checklist for adding ANY second persistent
2635: <module> to a project in this family: (1) symlink its binary's own
2636: directory into button.sh's session bootstrap, (2) add its own
2637: cwd-matched reap to kill_own_module(), (3) add it to the `kill` action's
2638: own pkill list too.
2639: 
2640: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2641: 29.8 SUPPRESSING A RE-TRIGGER NEEDS IDENTITY, NOT PRESENCE - "IS THE
2642:     TRIGGER CHAR STILL THERE" IS THE WRONG QUESTION
2643: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2644: Auto-closing the picker when the user reactivates the cli_io (direct
2645: request: "if activated again, close picker") needed to suppress
2646: REOPENING for the SAME dismissed '*' while the user kept typing past
2647: it - but a first-cut boolean flag, cleared only once NO '*' existed
2648: anywhere in the buffer, was wrong: typing a second, genuinely NEW '*'
2649: later in the same message (e.g. "pieces/* hi how are u *") left the
2650: FIRST '*' still present earlier in the string, so the flag never
2651: cleared and the second, deliberate trigger was wrongly suppressed too
2652: - live-reported as "didn't retrigger picker." Fixed by tracking the
2653: DISMISSED star's own POSITION (`strrchr(...) - g_last_typed`, an
2654: offset, not a bool) and comparing it against the CURRENT last-found
2655: star's position on each check: same offset (user is still typing past
2656: the one they already dismissed) stays suppressed; any different offset
2657: (a '*' appeared somewhere new, earlier or later) is a distinct
2658: occurrence and triggers normally. General lesson: when suppressing a
2659: repeat of a dismissed event that's keyed on "does X still exist in
2660: some larger state," track WHICH specific occurrence of X was dismissed,
2661: not merely whether X-in-general is still present - the two diverge the
2662: moment a second, legitimate instance of X can coexist with the leftover
2663: first one.
2664: 
2665: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2666: 29.9 DEBUG LOGGING LEFT IN A HOT POLL LOOP IS ITS OWN BUG, NOT JUST MESS
2667: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2668: Temporary `fopen(...,"a")`/`fprintf`/`fclose` debug tracing added to
2669: diagnose 29.5/29.8 above was left running inside path_nav_manager.c's
2670: own 40-150ms poll loop across several test/fix iterations - real disk
2671: I/O on every tick, live-reported as visible redraw "flicker" while
2672: typing, and only PARTLY explained by the actual logic bugs it was
2673: added to diagnose. Removed as its own fix, separate from whatever logic
2674: bug prompted adding it. Any debug instrumentation added to a
2675: persistent, tightly-looped process (any *_manager.c/*_pal_manager.+x
2676: in this family) needs to be treated as a real perf regression the
2677: moment it ships in a poll loop, not just cosmetic clutter to clean up
2678: "eventually."
2679: 
2680: ========================================================================
2681: 30. FRAME HEIGHT PADDING — UI CONVENTION FOR SCROLLBACK (2026-07-21)
2682: ========================================================================
2683: Each rendered frame should pad to exactly 1 screen height (terminal rows).
2684: This ensures only ONE full frame is visible at a time, and scrolling shows
2685: the previous frame cleanly (not two frames cramped on one screen).
2686: 
2687: IMPLEMENTATION (C):
2688:   1. Detect terminal height: LINES env var, ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ), or default 24
2689:   2. After writing frame content to view.txt:
2690:      - Count actual lines in the file
2691:      - Calculate padding needed: terminal_height - line_count
2692:      - Append that many blank lines
2693:   3. Do this AFTER frame content is written, BEFORE frame history dump
2694: 
2695: PATTERN:
2696:   static void pad_frame_to_screen(const char *view_path) {
2697:       int terminal_height = 24;
2698:       const char *lines_env = getenv("LINES");
2699:       if (lines_env) terminal_height = atoi(lines_env);
2700: 
2701:       struct winsize w;
2702:       if (ioctl(STDOUT_FILENO, TIOCGWINSZ, &w) == 0 && w.ws_row > 0)
2703:           terminal_height = w.ws_row;
2704: 
2705:       FILE *f = fopen(view_path, "r");
2706:       if (!f) return;
2707:       int line_count = 0;
2708:       char buf[MAX_LINE];
2709:       while (fgets(buf, sizeof(buf), f)) line_count++;
2710:       fclose(f);
2711: 
2712:       if (line_count < terminal_height) {
2713:           f = fopen(view_path, "a");
2714:           if (f) {
2715:               for (int i = line_count; i < terminal_height; i++)
2716:                   fprintf(f, "\n");
2717:               fclose(f);
2718:           }
2719:       }
2720:   }
2721: 
2722: VERIFIED WORKING (2026-07-21):
2723:   ✅ muchi-pal-agent: pad_frame_to_screen() in compose_frame.c
2724: 
2725: BENEFIT:
2726:   - User scrolls up in terminal, sees ONE complete previous frame
2727:   - Not two frames crammed into view with partial content visible
2728:   - Cleaner scrollback experience, matches typical terminal paging
2729:   - Each frame takes exactly 1 screen, makes navigation predictable
2730: 
2731: ========================================================================
2732: 30. CORRECTION TO SEC. 28 - REAL fuzz-op_manager.c's own route_input()
2733:     READ IN FULL (2026-07-21, civ-pal) - POSSESSION ENTRY/EXIT ARE NOT
2734:     NUMBERED METHODS AT ALL, THEY'RE UNCONDITIONAL HARDCODED CHECKS
2735: ========================================================================
2736: Direct user instruction, twice: "look at those fuzz op file[s] too."
2737: Sec. 28 was written from chtpm_parser.c (the shared, generic desktop-
2738: hosting NAV/ACTIVE engine) and fuzz-op.chtpm ALONE - real, but it is
2739: only the OUTER layer (how you enter/exit "controlling this project at
2740: all" inside a wraith-alpha desktop window). The INNER layer - what
2741: actually happens once raw keys reach fuzz-op's own game logic - lives
2742: in a completely different file, projects/fuzz-op/manager/
2743: fuzz-op_manager.c's own route_input(), read in full this pass for the
2744: first time. It changes two concrete, load-bearing details sec. 28 got
2745: wrong by inference rather than by reading this file:
2746: 
2747: 30.1 POSSESSING IS NOT A NUMBERED METHOD - IT'S AN UNCONDITIONAL CHECK
2748:      ON EVERY ENTER WHILE UNPOSSESSED
2749: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2750: route_input() (fuzz-op_manager.c ~line 559): `if (strcmp(active_target_
2751: id, "xlector") == 0 && (key == 10 || key == 13 || key == 2000))` - ANY
2752: Enter, while xlector is the active target, ALWAYS scans every piece
2753: under the project's own pieces/ tree for one whose pos_x/pos_y exactly
2754: matches xlector's CURRENT position (excluding xlector itself and
2755: anything with "zombie" in its name - a hardcoded, project-specific
2756: exclusion, not a generic mechanism) and possesses the first match.
2757: There is NO numbered-method dispatch involved in triggering this at
2758: all - it does not go through pdl_reader.c, does not check a
2759: "scan"-named row on xlector's own piece.pdl, does not require
2760: action_cursor to be pointing anywhere in particular. Sec. 28's own
2761: design (this session's FIRST attempt at porting this) gated possession
2762: behind a real "scan" METHOD row that had to be digit-selected (or rely
2763: on an auto-preview default landing on index 2) before Enter would do
2764: anything - WRONG, and a real, load-bearing bug: any state where
2765: action_cursor wasn't exactly the "scan" row's own index made Enter a
2766: complete no-op, silently. Corrected: Enter, while unpossessed, should
2767: ALWAYS attempt a possess-at-cursor check, unconditionally, exactly
2768: like the reference - not routed through the numbered method-dispatch
2769: mechanism at all.
2770: 
2771: 30.2 RELINQUISHING IS BOUND TO ESC AND THE LITERAL KEY '9' DIRECTLY -
2772:      NOT A NUMBERED "release" METHOD EITHER
2773: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2774: route_input() ~line 550: `if (key == '9' || key == 27 || key == 2008)
2775: { if (strcmp(active_target_id, "xlector") != 0) { strcpy(active_target_
2776: id, "xlector"); log_resp("Returned to Xlector."); ... return; } }` -
2777: BOTH ESC and the bare '9' key relinquish possession directly, as a
2778: hardcoded key check at the very top of route_input(), before ANY other
2779: key handling runs. Sec. 28's own design put this behind a numbered
2780: "release" METHOD on the possessed piece's own piece.pdl (dispatched via
2781: the same digit+Enter mechanism as any other method) - WRONG for the
2782: same reason as 30.1: it required the player to first digit-select the
2783: right row, rather than a single, always-available key working
2784: immediately regardless of any pending digit_accum/action_cursor state.
2785: 
2786: THIS DOES NOT CONTRADICT chtpm_parser.c's OWN ESC handler (sec. 28.1
2787: #2) - it's a DIFFERENT ESC, at a DIFFERENT layer. chtpm's own ESC_KEY
2788: check (chtpm_parser.c ~line 2860) fires FIRST and is fully consumed
2789: there whenever the OUTER desktop-hosting active_index is what's
2790: currently engaged (browsing/entering a project window) - a raw ESC
2791: never reaches route_input() at all in that state, chtpm owns it
2792: entirely. route_input()'s OWN ESC handling only ever runs once you're
2793: "inside" the project's own embedded frame with input already being
2794: raw-injected there (sec. 28.3's own "raw-key-injection" mechanism) -
2795: i.e., fuzz-op_manager.c's ESC check is the INNER layer's own
2796: relinquish-only behavior, chtpm's is the OUTER layer's own NAV-vs-
2797: ACTIVE toggle. A project with no outer desktop host at all (civ-pal,
2798: angler-empires - confirmed architectural difference, PAL-CRAFT-
2799: DESIGN.txt's own dox) only has the INNER layer to build, which is what
2800: sec. 28.3's "interact" METHOD row was standing in for at the OUTER
2801: layer's absence - that part of sec. 28 stands. What's corrected here is
2802: ONLY what happens once you're already "inside" (ACTIVE/interact_mode
2803: ==1): relinquish should be a fixed, always-available action (matching
2804: route_input()'s own '9'/ESC dual binding), not a numbered method.
2805: 
2806: 30.3 WHAT STAYS CORRECT FROM SEC. 28 (verified AGAINST route_input(),
2807:      not just re-asserted)
2808: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2809: - Arrows always move whichever piece active_target_id/possessed_id
2810:   currently names (route_input() ~line 790, dispatches to
2811:   move_entity.+x with active_target_id as an argument) - matches sec.
2812:   28.4 exactly.
2813: - Digit hotkeys 2-9, ONCE something is possessed (or for xlector's own
2814:   real methods), DO dispatch through pdl_reader.c against
2815:   active_target_id's own piece.pdl (route_input() ~line 708-788) -
2816:   matches sec. 28.3's own dispatch_id mechanism exactly. This part of
2817:   sec. 28 was right and needs no change - ONLY entry (Enter-to-possess)
2818:   and exit (ESC/'9'-to-release) were wrong about requiring a NUMBERED
2819:   row for something that's actually a fixed, unconditional key check.
2820: - Possession persisting independently of any outer NAV/interact
2821:   toggle (sec. 28.2) - route_input() has no concept of an outer
2822:   NAV mode at all (that's chtpm's job, a layer up) and never resets
2823:   active_target_id except via the explicit '9'/ESC/re-possess paths -
2824:   confirms sec. 28.2's own core claim was right, just needed the
2825:   correct trigger mechanism (30.1/30.2) plugged in underneath it.
2826: 
2827: 30.4 CONCRETE FIX NEEDED IN civ-pal's ops/choice.c (not yet applied as
2828:      of this doc being written - do this next)
2829: ------------------------------------------------------------------------

2842:   - ops/spawn_unit.c's own "release" METHOD row (added this session)

2847: 31. THE COMPLETE INTERACT/POSSESSION PACKAGE - EVERY REAL BUG FOUND
2848:     THIS SESSION (civ-pal), CONSOLIDATED SO IT NEVER HAS TO BE RE-
2849:     DERIVED AGAIN - PORT ALL OF THIS, IDENTICALLY, TO EVERY PROJECT
2850:     BUILT ON THE tempt-island/PDL-action-bar ENGINE (not chtpm-based
2851:     projects - those already have the real mechanism, sec. 28/30 IS
2852:     that mechanism read from source)
2853: ========================================================================
2854: Direct user instruction: "port it, and port the interact mode stuff to
2855: all projects... include all this stuff in xyzos-standards document as
2856: well pls. id hate to repeat it again." This section is the single
2857: checklist to work from - sec. 28/30 explain WHY, this section is WHAT,
2858: concretely, file by file. If you're porting this to a new project, copy
2859: this section's own checklist top to bottom, don't re-derive it.
2860: 
2861: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2862: 31.1 THE FULL MODEL, ONE MORE TIME, TERSELY
2863: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2864: Three fields on the top-level control piece (conventionally "hero",
2865: even though it never moves - it's the NAV-mode dispatch anchor, not an
2866: avatar):
2867:   interact_mode (0/1): 0=NAV ("[>]", digit/Enter dispatches hero's own
2868:     numbered METHOD list), 1=ACTIVE ("[^]", arrows move whichever piece
2869:     possession names, digit/Enter dispatches THAT piece's own list).
2870:   possessed_id (string, "none" or an instance id): which piece receives
2871:     ACTIVE-mode movement/dispatch. "none" = the free xlector cursor.
2872:   xlector_pos_x/xlector_pos_y (ints, already existed pre-session): the
2873:     free cursor's own position, separate from any possessed piece's.
2874: 
2875: Entry to ACTIVE: a real numbered METHOD row named "interact" on hero's
2876: own piece.pdl (NOT a bound key - direct user correction, twice: "isnt
2877: an 'i' selectable... another indexed nav"). Special-cased by NAME in the
2878: dispatch op, same pattern as pre-existing "craft"/"examine" specials.
2879: 
2880: Exit/relinquish: ESC. Two-step, never both in one press (matches real
2881: fuzz-op_manager.c's route_input(), read in full, sec. 30):
2882:   - possessed_id != "none": relinquish only (-> "none"), interact_mode
2883:     UNCHANGED (stays ACTIVE, controlling the free cursor now).
2884:   - possessed_id == "none": deactivate interact_mode (-> NAV), restore
2885:     action_cursor to "interact"'s own real index (never hardcode it -
2886:     look it up fresh, method order can change) so "[>]" reappears
2887:     immediately instead of vanishing.
2888: 
2889: Possessing: Enter, UNCONDITIONALLY, whenever dispatch_id=="xlector" (NOT
2890: gated behind selecting a numbered "scan" method first - real, load-
2891: bearing bug found and fixed this session, sec. 30.1). Scans real
2892: instances (a units/ or anglers/ or whatever-the-project-calls-it/
2893: directory) for a pos_x/pos_y match at the free cursor's CURRENT
2894: position, possesses the first match.
2895: 
2896: dispatch_id, computed identically everywhere this matters (choice.c's
2897: own dispatch AND compose_frame.c's/compose_rgb_frame.c's own footer -
2898: all three must compute it the SAME way or the visible footer can lie
2899: about what Enter/digits will actually do):
2900:   interact_mode==0            -> "hero"
2901:   interact_mode==1, unpossessed -> "xlector"
2902:   interact_mode==1, possessed   -> possessed_id itself
2903: 
2904: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2905: 31.2 EVERY REAL BUG, FILE BY FILE - THE ACTUAL PORT CHECKLIST
2906: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
2907: ops/choice.c:
2908:   [ ] ARROW_LEFT(1000)/ARROW_RIGHT(1001) defined and checked - the
2909:       ORIGINAL bug in this whole thread: only up/down existed, LEFT/
2910:       RIGHT fell through to the catch-all and silently wiped
2911:       action_cursor the moment the player moved horizontally (needed to
2912:       reach almost any real target on a real map).
2913:   [ ] NAV-mode (interact_mode==0) arrow handling: up/down/left/right ALL
2914:       cycle action_cursor among hero's real methods (2..total-1),
2915:       wrapping - real chtpm only ever used up/down for this (confirmed
2916:       by direct grep of chtpm_parser.c); left/right doing the same is a
2917:       genuine, deliberate EXTENSION beyond the reference (direct user
2918:       request), not a port - implement it anyway, it's now the family
2919:       standard.
2920:   [ ] ACTIVE-mode (interact_mode==1) arrow handling: ALL FOUR directions
2921:       must be a complete no-op in this op (arrows belong to
2922:       move_player.c entirely) - not just up/down excluded, LEFT/RIGHT
2923:       too, or the same marker-wiping bug recurs in the other mode.
2924:   [ ] "interact" METHOD special-case (dispatch_id=="hero"): sets
2925:       interact_mode=1, resets xlector_x/y to hero's own (static) spawn
2926:       position, sets a "just entered" flag so action_cursor defaults to
2927:       2 (xlector's own first real method) instead of -1 - matches
2928:       chtpm's own "ACTIVATE moves focus to first navigable child."
2929:   [ ] Enter-to-possess special-case (dispatch_id=="xlector"):
2930:       UNCONDITIONAL, checked BEFORE the normal action_cursor-gated
2931:       dispatch, calls the project's own examine-and-possess op (this
2932:       project's examine_at()) which sets possessed_id on a match.
2933:   [ ] NO "release" numbered method - sec. 30.1/30.2's correction.
2934:       Relinquish is the ESC handler (below), not a method row.
2935:   [ ] ESC handler: relinquish-then-move-xlector-to-possessed-position
2936:       (see 31.1 above AND the NEW correction below), OR deactivate+
2937:       restore-action_cursor-by-lookup, never both in one press.
2938:   [ ] NEW 2026-07-21 (later same session, direct user clarification -
2939:       see 31.3 below for the full citation): on relinquish, xlector's
2940:       OWN position must be moved to the possessed piece's CURRENT
2941:       pos_x/pos_y BEFORE clearing possessed_id - read the possessed
2942:       instance's own state.txt directly for this, don't reuse a stale
2943:       xlector_x/y.
2944: 
2945: ops/move_player.c:
2946:   [ ] `if (interact_mode == 0) return 0;` - arrows are a hard no-op in
2947:       NAV mode, right after camera-pan handling, before any map/
2948:       movement setup. There is no avatar to move in this model.
2949:   [ ] Possession-redirect block: `if (interact_mode==1 &&
2950:       possessed_id!="none" && (dx||dy))` - walkable-checked move of the
2951:       possessed instance's OWN state.txt pos_x/pos_y (read the
2952:       project's own instances/ dir, e.g. units/<possessed_id>/
2953:       state.txt). Deliberately the SMALLEST correct slice - no attack,
2954:       no map-transition handling, only what's actually designed.
2955:   [ ] `facing = key;` must ALSO be set inside the free-cursor movement
2956:       block (not only the old, now-dead hero-movement path) - or the 3D
2957:       view direction never tracks cursor movement, only position.
2958: 
2959: ops/compose_frame.c AND ops/compose_rgb_frame.c (BOTH - two separate
2960: rendering pipelines, PIECECRAFT-WRAITH-ARCHITECTURE.md sec. 2's own
2961: documented gotcha, a fix to one NEVER automatically applies to the
2962: other - confirmed real multiple times this session):
2963:   [ ] A real render loop for the project's own instances/ directory
2964:       (units/, anglers/, whatever) - THE SINGLE BIGGEST GAP FOUND THIS
2965:       SESSION: the whole possession system was built, tested, and
2966:       believed working via direct state.txt inspection for a long time
2967:       before anyone noticed spawned instances were NEVER ACTUALLY DRAWN
2968:       ON THE MAP AT ALL. Same shape as an existing npcs/ or monsters/
2969:       loop if the project has one (glyph read straight from each
2970:       instance's own state.txt field, no registry re-lookup needed if
2971:       spawn already wrote it there).
2972:   [ ] Xlector drawn ONLY when `possessed_id=="none"` - NOT unconditional
2973:       (an earlier fix this same session made it unconditional/always-

2976:       at all, only the possessed piece's own glyph - see 31.3).
2977:   [ ] The OLD static avatar glyph ('@' or equivalent), if the project
2978:       inherited one from a pre-possession-model era, drawn
2979:       unconditionally at a position that never moves - DELETE the
2980:       drawing code entirely, don't just leave it disabled. It actively
2981:       misleads players into trying to interact with something dead
2982:       (real, live-caught: a user tried to possess '@' itself, standing
2983:       xlector right next to it, got "you see nothing here" because '@'

2985:       full removal, not a toggle).
2986:   [ ] Footer/action-bar's own method-list source must use the SAME
2987:       3-way dispatch_id rule as choice.c (31.1) - a hardcoded "hero"
2988:       here (pre-session default) makes the footer lie about what
2989:       digits/Enter will actually do once ACTIVE.
2990:   [ ] Debug aid (direct user request, after a real "possession does
2991:       nothing" report that couldn't be reproduced via synthetic single-
2992:       op testing): the footer's own last-key display should DECODE
2993:       arrow/Enter/ESC into readable names (not just a raw number) and
2994:       show current Mode (NAV[>]/ACTIVE[^]) and Possessed id on the SAME
2995:       line, ALWAYS, unconditionally, "no matter interact or not" -
2996:       verbatim user words. This is what actually let the real bug
2997:       (30.1/30.2) get found and fixed at all.
2998:   [ ] 3D camera anchor (render_3d_view()'s own position parameter, if
2999:       the project has a 3D mirror): follow xlector_x/xlector_y, not a
3000:       static hero position (direct user request: "the 3d camera needs
3001:       to move with the xelector"). Does NOT yet follow a possessed
3002:       piece's own position instead once one exists - not built, flagged
3003:       each time, not silently assumed done.
3004:   [ ] Mansion-sim leftover text (direct user request: "remove the
3005:       mansion text from screen") - a `room: <name>` header field sourced
3006:       from a locations.txt lookup, AND/OR literal room-name text baked
3007:       directly into map.txt's own ASCII art as wall decoration. Neither
3008:       means anything once the project has no "rooms" concept - strip
3009:       both, don't just hide them behind a flag.
3010: 
3011: ops/end_turn.c (or whatever advances the turn counter/any per-turn
3012: stat):
3013:   [ ] Gate the whole thing on `interact_mode==1` - a NAV-mode keypress
3014:       (pure menu navigation) must never advance the game clock. Real
3015:       bug, live-caught: hunger/thirst were rising on arrow presses that
3016:       move_player.c already correctly no-op'd for, because end_turn.c
3017:       ran unconditionally regardless of what (if anything) actually
3018:       happened that tick.

3020: ops/title_input.c (or wherever New Game/Load Game resets happen):
3021:   [ ] Check for a DOUBLE sentinel-keycode append (this project's own
3022:       999 for "start gameplay") - a real, live-caught bug: BOTH an
3023:       internal reset_world()-style helper AND its own caller appended
3024:       the sentinel separately, so every New Game commit queued it
3025:       TWICE. The stray second one reached the real gameplay loop as an
3026:       unrecognized key and silently reset action_cursor via the catch-
3027:       all - looked exactly like broken NAV-cycling on the very first
3028:       frame of a fresh game, actual cause was upstream. Grep for the
3029:       sentinel string/append call; there should be exactly ONE site
3030:       that writes it per commit path, not two.
3031:   [ ] Truncate the message/response log file (message_log.txt or
3032:       equivalent) as part of every New Game/Load Game reset, not just
3033:       the world directory. Real, live-caught confusion: stale log lines
3034:       from a PREVIOUS session/testing run were still visible on the
3035:       VERY FIRST rendered frame of a brand new game, looking exactly
3036:       like something (a possess, a release) had already auto-happened
3037:       before the player pressed a single key.
3038:

3040:   [ ] Truncate message_log.txt at process-start time too (covers the

3043:       fix above, belt-and-suspenders, not redundant (one covers process
3044:       start, the other covers New-Game-mid-session without restarting
3045:       `run`).

3064: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
3065: 31.3 XLECTOR VISIBILITY - THE FULL, FINAL RULE (two corrections landed
3066:      in sequence, same session - both kept here so the SECOND doesn't
3067:      get silently un-done by someone only reading the FIRST)
3068: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
3069: Correction 1 (direct user words): "we dont need to erase xelector
3070: position from view, its enough that it stay still" - made xlector's own
3071: glyph draw UNCONDITIONALLY (previously gated on interact_mode==1, which
3072: made it vanish on ESC back to NAV - wrong, it should just sit still,
3073: visible, wherever it was left).
3074: 
3075: Correction 2, LATER the same session (direct user words): "during
3076: possession xelector isnt visible on the map, till relinquishment of
3077: possession where it will appear at the new location of the possessed
3078: entity like before" - narrows correction 1: xlector is visible
3079: UNCONDITIONALLY EXCEPT while `possessed_id != "none"` (fully hidden
3080: then - the possessed piece's own glyph, from the instances/ render loop
3081: in 31.2, is the only visible marker while actually possessing
3082: something). The FINAL, correct gate is therefore:
3083:   draw xlector IF AND ONLY IF possessed_id == "none"
3084: (unconditional-on-interact_mode from correction 1, further narrowed by
3085: correction 2 - NOT "only in ACTIVE mode," that was the ORIGINAL, wrong
3086: gate correction 1 already replaced).
3087: On relinquish (choice.c's ESC handler, 31.2's own checklist item),
3088: xlector_x/y get set to the possessed piece's OWN current position
3089: BEFORE possessed_id clears, so it reappears exactly where the player
3090: ends up, not some stale pre-possession spot.
3091: 
3092: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
3093: 31.4 "FIXED TO ONE CHARACTER" - A CONFIG-DRIVEN MODE, NOT A CODE FORK
3094:      (direct user instruction: "for certain projects possession should
3095:      be fixed to one character... make it something done in a config
3096:      file, dont not port the code" - i.e. port the FULL, IDENTICAL
3097:      possession machinery to every project regardless of genre, and let
3098:      a config file decide whether it behaves as free multi-target swap
3099:      (angler-empires/civ-pal - many real units, DF/Civ-style) or locked
3100:      to a single fixed target (pal-craft/muchimon-pal - one player
3101:      character, Minecraft/Pokemon-style) - so ANY project can be
3102:      switched into free-swap mode later for debugging with ZERO new
3103:      code, just a config edit)
3104: ------------------------------------------------------------------------
3105: New registry-shaped config file, same pipe-delimited/key=value
3106: convention as everything else in this family - NOT a new format:
3107:   pieces/system/possession_config.txt:
3108:     mode=free|fixed
3109:     fixed_target_id=<instance_id>   (only meaningful when mode=fixed -
3110:       e.g. muchimon-pal's own single player character's instance id)
3111: 
3112: Wiring (minimal - reuses 31.1's existing dispatch_id/possessed_id
3113: mechanism entirely, does NOT branch the dispatch logic itself):
3114:   - On process/session start (or simplest: read fresh every choice.c
3115:     invocation, same "no persistent state beyond plain files" doctrine
3116:     as everywhere else in this family), if mode==fixed: possessed_id is
3117:     FORCED to fixed_target_id every tick (a one-line clamp, applied
3118:     right after reading possessed_id off hero/state.txt, before any
3119:     dispatch logic runs) - self-healing, so even if something else
3120:     wrote "none" or a different id, it snaps back immediately.
3121:   - ESC's own relinquish branch becomes structurally a no-op in fixed
3122:     mode (possessed_id can never actually leave fixed_target_id, so the
3123:     "possessed_id != none" check is always true and always re-clamps to
3124:     the SAME id instead of "none" - same code path, no new branch).
3125:   - interact_mode's own NAV<->ACTIVE toggle STILL WORKS NORMALLY in
3126:     fixed mode (a fixed-target project still benefits from "ESC to stop
3127:     controlling anything, browse the menu" - it's only the SWAP-WHICH-
3128:     PIECE part of possession that's locked, not interact mode itself).
3129:   - Flipping mode=fixed -> mode=free in the config file, with NO code
3130:     changes, immediately unlocks full multi-target possession (Enter-
3131:     to-possess-anything-at-cursor starts working again the instant
3132:     possessed_id is no longer force-clamped) - this is the whole point:
3133:     a debugging escape hatch that costs nothing to keep wired in.
3134: 
3135: Per-project defaults (design intent, not yet all built as of this
3136: section being written - check each project's own dox before assuming):
3137:   civ-pal, angler-empires: mode=free (many real units/anglers, swapping
3138:     between them is the actual core mechanic of a DF/Civ-style game).
3139:   pal-craft, muchimon-pal: mode=fixed, fixed_target_id=<the project's
3140:     own single player character instance> (Minecraft/Pokemon-style -
3141:     one player-controlled character, no reason to ever swap in normal
3142:     play, but the SAME full machinery is present underneath, config-
3143:     unlockable for debugging without touching code).
3144:

3149: ------------------------------------------------------------------------

3161:     proof that "scan" and "possess" are two DIFFERENT actions in the
3162:     reference (scan_op.c never possesses anything, confirmed by direct

3164:     into one function as a simplification, a real, acknowledged

3166:   $.4x/interact-fix-j21.txt, $.4x/handoff-possession-4x.txt - this
3167:     session's own blow-by-blow history of getting this wrong three-plus
3168:     times before converging here, kept for the embarrassing-but-useful
3169:     paper trail, not deleted once superseded.
3170: 
3171: ========================================================================
3172: 32. "POSSESS-AND-MOVE" IS NOT ALWAYS RIGHT - REAL DWARF FORTRESS ONLY
3173:     EVER "FOCUSES" A DWARF, IT NEVER LETS YOU PUPPET ONE (direct user
3174:     catch, 2026-07-21, right after the sec. 31 port to angler-empires)
3175: ========================================================================
3176: Direct user question that caught this: "there is no way to actually
3177: possess characters in dwarf fortress... just a way to 'focus menu on
3178: them', does that change things so far?" Yes - a real, project-specific
3179: correction, not a global one.
3180: 
3181: THE MISTAKE: sec. 28/30/31's own possession model (Enter-to-possess,
3182: arrows directly move whichever piece possessed_id names) was read from
3183: fuzz-op_manager.c's own route_input() - real, verified UI INFRASTRUCTURE
3184: for "select a piece, then directly control it." That mechanism is
3185: genre-neutral machinery, not a design decision about whether direct
3186: control is APPROPRIATE for a given project. It was ported into angler-
3187: empires uncritically, without checking it against real Dwarf Fortress's
3188: own actual game design - which does NOT let the player directly walk a
3189: dwarf around at all. DF's own "select a dwarf" only ever opens an info/
3190: menu panel (current job, needs, inventory, priorities) - the dwarf
3191: keeps moving autonomously under its own job/AI assignment regardless of
3192: what's selected. This directly contradicts what got built (arrows move
3193: the possessed angler directly) and reintroduces exactly what angler-
3194: empires's OWN original design doc (ANGLER-EXPANSES-DESIGN.txt sec. 4,
3195: written BEFORE any of this session's possession work) already called
3196: for: job designation + idle-labor AI pathing, angler movement is NEVER
3197: player-direct-controlled there either.
3198: 
3199: THE FIX IS PER-PROJECT, NOT A REVERSION OF SEC. 31 EVERYWHERE - checked
3200: against each project's own actual genre:
3201:   - angler-empires (DF-style, no direct unit control in the reference
3202:     genre) - WRONG as built, needs correcting: Enter-on-an-angler
3203:     should open a FOCUS/INFO panel (matches this family's own existing
3204:     active_panel mechanism already used for craft/inventory - not new
3205:     machinery, same "active_panel != none suspends movement input,
3206:     compose_frame draws it as an overlay" pattern), NOT set
3207:     possessed_id to something arrows then move. The angler itself
3208:     should keep moving via its own decision_mode/job-assignment AI
3209:     (angler-expanses design doc sec. 4, not yet built) - possession-as-
3210:     direct-movement should be REMOVED for this project specifically,
3211:     not just left dormant. NOT YET FIXED as of this section being
3212:     written - flagged as real, open work, not done.
3213:   - civ-pal (Civilization-style) - CORRECT as built. Real Civilization
3214:     DOES let the player directly command a unit's movement each turn -
3215:     this is a genuine, load-bearing genre difference from DF, not an
3216:     oversight. Keep possess-and-move exactly as sec. 31 built it.
3217:   - muchimon-pal (tactics-on-map battles, sec. 8 of MUCHIMON-DESIGN.txt)
3218:     - CORRECT as designed. Direct control of a deployed entity during
3219:     an engagement is how tactics games work; sec. 8.3's own "manual
3220:     control = simply possessing it" framing already assumed this
3221:     correctly.
3222:   - pal-craft (single player character, Minecraft-style) - CORRECT.
3223:     Ordinary direct player movement is the entire point.
3224: 
3225: THE REAL LESSON, more general than this one instance: a mechanism read
3226: from a REFERENCE PROJECT's own source (fuzz-op_manager.c, sec. 30) is
3227: proof that the MECHANISM works and matches that project's OWN genre -
3228: it is not automatically proof that the mechanism is the right DESIGN
3229: CHOICE for every project being ported to, even within the same family
3230: and even when the underlying UI plumbing (xlector, interact_mode,
3231: possessed_id) is correctly shared. Check the target project's own
3232: design doc / actual genre conventions before assuming "port the
3233: mechanism" means "port the mechanism's every behavior unchanged." Sec.
3234: 31's own file-by-file checklist should get a per-project genre-
3235: appropriateness pass, not just a mechanical copy-and-substitute, before
3236: being declared complete for a given project.
3237: 
3238: STILL OPEN, NAMED explicitly for whoever picks this up: build the
3239: FOCUS/INFO panel for angler-empires (replacing its own current
3240: possess-and-move behavior), and build the actual job-designation +
3241: idle-labor AI system angler-expanses' own design doc sec. 4 already

1932: =======================================================================
1933: DATE: 2026-07-30
1934: FOUND WHILE: building `102.agy-txt` (a new self-contained editor
1935: project, Phase T2 of its own PLAN.md - proving layout switching before
1936: wiring real functionality behind it).
1937: 
1938: THE MISTAKE, STATED PLAINLY: `chtpm_parser_pal.c`'s own `launch_module()`
1939: has a real guard - `if (strcmp(launch_str, current_module_path) == 0
1940: && current_module_pid > 0) return;` - with a long, detailed comment
1941: explaining a real, past bug it fixed (a path-only version of this guard
1942: used to kill and relaunch an already-alive persistent module on every
1943: single href navigation). Reading that comment alone, it is completely
1944: reasonable to conclude "href navigation between layouts now keeps the
1945: same persistent module process alive, that bug is fixed." That
1946: conclusion is WRONG, and was written into a planning document
1947: (102.agy-txt/PLAN.md) before being tested.
1948: 
1949: WHAT'S ACTUALLY TRUE: built a real Phase T2 stub - three real `.chtpm`
1950: layouts, each declaring the identical `<module>system/prisc+x pal/
1951: main_loop_chtpm.pal</module>` line, a real op that writes the
1952: COMPOSING PROCESS'S OWN PID into the rendered frame. Launched it for
1953: real, injected three real `[TS] KEY_PRESSED: 13` keystrokes via
1954: `pieces/keyboard/history.txt` to walk editor -> file_menu ->
1955: file_browser, checked the module's own PID via `ps aux` after each:
1956: 
1957:     PID before nav 1: 1821693  ->  after: 1822388  (DIFFERENT)
1958:     PID before nav 2: 1822388  ->  after: 1823813  (DIFFERENT)
1959: 
1960: Reproducible on both transitions, not a fluke. Root cause, found by
1961: reading `cleanup_module()` (called unconditionally by the href click
1962: path): it kills+reaps the current module AND clears
1963: `current_module_path` BEFORE `launch_module()`'s own guard is ever
1964: checked - so by the time the guard runs, `current_module_path` is
1965: already empty, and the guard can never match. The bug fix the comment
1966: describes was real and correctly fixed a DIFFERENT problem (a stale
1967: module leaking/double-killing) - it was never a fix that makes href
1968: avoid a respawn, and the comment's own surrounding prose can easily be
1969: read as claiming the opposite if you don't test it.
1970: 
1971: WHY THIS DIDN'T ACTUALLY BREAK ANYTHING: the respawn-per-href behavior
1972: turns out to be FINE, not a bug to work around - it's the same real
1973: behavior the actual reference architecture being ported
1974: (1.TPMOS_c_+rmmp.0103.0001/projects/agy-text-editor) already has and
1975: ships with (its own href transitions respawn its manager too, per that
1976: project's own real code, confirmed same session) - because real state
1977: lives on disk (document.txt/cursor.txt equivalents), never in a
1978: process-resident variable that would need to survive. This house's own
1979: "files are the API" law already means nothing SHOULD depend on
1980: process continuity across a screen switch. The corrected lesson isn't
1981: "fix the respawn," it's "don't assume state survives in memory across
1982: an href transition - keep it on disk, which this house's own
1983: conventions already push you toward anyway."
1984: 
1985: LESSON, GENERALIZED: a detailed, technically accurate comment
1986: describing a real past bug fix does not by itself prove the CURRENT
1987: behavior does what you infer from it - the comment can be 100% honest
1988: about what it fixed and still support a wrong inference about what
1989: that leaves true today, especially for a guard whose short-circuit
1990: depends on a precondition (`current_module_path` being non-empty) that
1991: a DIFFERENT function, called earlier in the same code path, always
1992: clears. When a claim like this is about to go into a planning document
1993: that other work will build on, TEST it with a real running process and
1994: a real PID check - exactly the same discipline PITFALL 58/60 already
1995: established for "does the op-level pass mean the real feature works,"
1996: applied here to "does a code comment describing a fix mean the thing
1997: it sounds like it fixed is actually fixed for my specific case."

2004: layout_changed.txt` in chtpm_parser_pal.c - a separate pulse mechanism

2009: 
2010: =======================================================================
2011: PITFALL 62: `mkdir -p` pre-creating a symlink's own target path makes
2012:             `ln -sfn` nest the symlink one level too deep instead of
2013:             replacing it — a real, previously-shipped bug in
2014:             102.editor-📄️00.00's own button.sh, found via 102.agy-txt's
2015:             own Phase T4 save/load testing
2016: =======================================================================
2017: DATE: 2026-07-30
2018: FOUND WHILE: building 102.agy-txt's own real SAVE_AS/LOAD flow (PLAN.md
2019: Phase T4) and testing it via real key injection through the real
2020: running app - a real save reported "Saved 11 bytes to docs/foo.txt",
2021: but the file never appeared under the durable house-level docs/
2022: directory afterward.
2023: 
2024: ROOT CAUSE: `button.sh run` builds a fresh session directory and does:
2025: 
2026:     mkdir -p ".../pieces/system" ".../pieces/display" ... ".../docs"
2027:     ...
2028:     ln -sfn "$SCRIPT_DIR/docs" "$SESSION_DIR/docs"
2029: 
2030: `mkdir -p` includes `$SESSION_DIR/docs` in its own list, creating it as
2031: a REAL directory BEFORE the `ln -sfn` call runs. `ln -sfn SRC DST`,
2032: when DST already exists as a real directory, does NOT replace it with
2033: a symlink - it creates the symlink INSIDE that directory, using SRC's
2034: own basename (`ln`'s own standard, POSIX-documented behavior for this
2035: exact case - not this house's own bug, but hitting it unknowingly is).
2036: Net effect: `$SESSION_DIR/docs/docs -> <real house-level docs/>` (one
2037: level too deep), while `$SESSION_DIR/docs` ITSELF stays a real,
2038: separate, EMPTY directory - exactly the directory the session's own
2039: `rm -rf "$SESSION_DIR"` cleanup trap deletes on exit.
2040: 
2041: `do_save_to()`/`do_load_from()` (agy_widget_cmds.c / editor_widget_
2042: cmds.c, both identical logic - copied verbatim, PLAN.md §2) call plain
2043: `fopen(path, ...)`/`fopen(path, "r")` with NO project_root prefixing at
2044: all - the path is resolved against the PROCESS'S CURRENT WORKING
2045: DIRECTORY, which is set to project_root (the session dir) via the
2046: `cd '%s' &&` in the shell command that invokes `agy_widget_cmds.+x`/
2047: `editor_widget_cmds.+x`. So a relative `docs/foo.txt` SAVE_AS path
2048: silently landed in the buggy, ephemeral `$SESSION_DIR/docs/`, not the
2049: durable one - the save reported success (it genuinely did write 11
2050: real bytes, just to the wrong, doomed location) and the bug produced
2051: NO error anywhere, exactly the "quiet by design" failure shape this
2052: house's own file-mediated architecture keeps producing when something
2053: IS wrong (see PITFALL 60's own §37 citation for the general pattern).
2054: 
2055: CONFIRMED THIS ALREADY AFFECTED THE SHIPPED, REAL, HARNESS-PROVEN
2056: 102.editor-📄️00.00 PROJECT TOO, undiscovered until now: its own
2057: button.sh has the byte-identical bug (`docs` in the same `mkdir -p`
2058: list, same `ln -sfn` line right after). It was never caught by this
2059: project's own real level-2 harness (`test-harn-ed-app`) because that
2060: harness's own `demo_save.sh` always used an ABSOLUTE save path
2061: (`$HARNESS/workdir/saved_$$.txt`), which never touches this code path
2062: at all - only a RELATIVE `docs/...` path (the natural, expected way a
2063: real user would actually use the SAVE AS flow, typing just a filename
2064: under the project's own docs/ convention) exercises it. A harness that
2065: never exercises the realistic/default path shape can stay green
2066: forever while that exact shape is broken - worth remembering as its
2067: own lesson, not just this specific bug.
2068: 
2069: FIX: removed `docs` from the `mkdir -p` argument list in BOTH
2070: button.sh files (102.agy-txt and 102.editor-📄️00.00) - `ln -sfn` then
2071: creates the symlink directly at `$SESSION_DIR/docs` as originally
2072: intended, since nothing pre-exists there to force nesting.
2073: 
2074: VERIFIED LIVE, real running app, both directions: killed all sessions,
2075: relaunched fresh, confirmed via `stat -L` that `$SESSION_DIR/docs` and
2076: the house-level `docs/` now share the same inode (proving the symlink
2077: resolves correctly, not just "looks like a symlink" - `stat` without
2078: `-L` reports the symlink's OWN inode, a real mistake made once during
2079: this exact investigation before switching to `-L`). Real SAVE_AS via
2080: real key injection (navigate FILE MENU -> SAVE AS -> real INTERACT
2081: path typing -> SAVE) produced a real file at the durable
2082: `102.agy-txt/docs/t4-fixed.txt` containing the real buffer content.
2083: Real LOAD afterward (buffer first cleared via a real NEW, then LOAD of
2084: the same path) correctly restored the buffer from that same real file.
2085: Both directions proven, not just save.
2086:

2088: docs/ removed from the mkdir -p list)

2091: pattern this is another real instance of)
2092: 
2093: PITFALL 63: "level-2" harnesses that only ever reach their assertions
2094:             via PASTE-mode / digit-jump-to-confirm shortcuts give a
2095:             FALSE POSITIVE on interaction-order bugs — three real
2096:             examples, same week, same root pattern
2097: =======================================================================
2098: DATE: 2026-07-30
2099: FOUND WHILE: live human testing of @.apps/text-editor-xyz (the real
2100: widget pair) and 102.agy-txt, AFTER both projects' own harnesses were
2101: already green and both were considered proven by this house's own
2102: §36.6 discipline.
2103: 
2104: ROOT CAUSE: §36.6's own "real key injection through the real running
2105: app" bar was being satisfied by harnesses that still took the
2106: MECHANICALLY SHORTEST path to their assertion - an op's own PASTE mode
2107: for typed text, and a direct digit-jump straight to a "confirm" button
2108: - rather than the NATURAL interaction order a real human actually uses:
2109: type character by character, then press the domain-natural terminator
2110: (Enter) expecting it to submit; press Esc expecting it to only stop
2111: typing, not leave the whole screen. Real key injection through a real
2112: running process is still real key injection - §36.6's own bar was
2113: genuinely met - but "real" was never the same claim as "exercises every
2114: interaction path a human would actually take." This is a narrower,
2115: newly-identified gap in a rule that was otherwise working exactly as
2116: intended.
2117: 
2118: THREE REAL INCIDENTS, SAME PATTERN, SAME WEEK:
2119:   1. chtpm_parser_pal.c/gl_mirror.c dual-capture doubling bug (see
2120:      !.xyzos-standards+1.txt §38 for the fix; the bug itself predates
2121:      this pitfall's own discovery of the METHODOLOGY gap - listed here
2122:      because it's the same root shape: synthetic injection structurally
2123:      cannot reproduce what a real capture RACE produces, same as it
2124:      cannot reproduce a real Enter/Esc interaction sequence).
2125:   2. 102.agy-txt's own SAVE AS path-field display bug - STILL OPEN at
2126:      time of writing (see 102.agy-txt/xyz-agy-handoff-j30.txt for the
2127:      full, still-unresolved account). test-harn-agy-txt's own
2128:      demo_save_load.sh used `ag_paste()` for every path-typing
2129:      assertion - real single-keystroke typing into that specific field
2130:      was never exercised by anything before a live human tried it and
2131:      found the on-screen PATH line never updated.
2132:   3. &.widgits/file-menu's own file_browser mode - three confirmed real
2133:      bugs, all invisible to the ALREADY-GREEN, ALREADY-SHIPPED
2134:      test-harn-ed-app: (a) Enter while the FILE field is selected does
2135:      nothing (`fm_menu_input.c` lines 318-321, both branches empty -
2136:      you must separately arrow-navigate to a distinct confirm row and
2137:      press Enter THERE), (b) Esc always exits the whole file_browser
2138:      back to main_menu unconditionally (line 312-316) because there is
2139:      no "actively typing" state distinct from "this row is merely
2140:      selected" anywhere in `fm_state.txt` for Esc to narrowly undo, (c)
2141:      no cursor/focus indicator is ever rendered (`fm_compose_frame.c`
2142:      lines 103-110 only ever emit `[>]`/`[ ]`, never a third "engaged"
2143:      state, never a trailing cursor glyph). test-harn-ed-app's own
2144:      demo_save.sh used `fm_paste()` for the path AND jumped straight to
2145:      SAVE_AS's own confirm index by digit - it never once pressed Enter
2146:      on the file field, never pressed Esc mid-typing. Full account,
2147:      including the direct comparison to this house's own real `<cli_io>`
2148:      mechanism (which DOES submit-on-Enter and DOES have a real focus
2149:      state, confirmed via chtpm_parser_pal.c's own `strcmp(el->type,
2150:      "cli_io")` branch) at &.widgits/file-menu/todo-how2-j30.txt.
2151: 
2152: WHY THIS IS ESPECIALLY SHARP WHEN A REFERENCE ARCHITECTURE EXISTS: a
2153: port/mirror of a named reference (TPMOS's own agy-text-editor, in this
2154: house's case) can produce byte-identical, harness-PROVEN-correct output
2155: DATA while having an entirely different, and broken, interaction
2156: CONTRACT from the reference it was supposedly mirroring. Proving the
2157: data shape matches is a real, necessary check - it is not the same
2158: claim as proving the interaction matches, and this house's own tooling
2159: had no separate check for the second claim until this pitfall.
2160: 
2161: FIX / STANDING GUARD: !.xyzos-standards+1.txt §38 (new section, same
2162: date) - every level-2 harness scenario involving typed input must
2163: exercise the natural interaction order (real per-keystroke injection
2164: ending in the field's own domain-natural terminator) at least once, not
2165: only PASTE/digit-jump shortcuts; and when the code under test is an
2166: explicit port of a named reference architecture, that reference's own
2167: interaction contract for typed fields (does Enter submit-in-place, does
2168: Esc deactivate-only-or-exit, is there a focus indicator) must be read
2169: and written down BEFORE a PASTE-only-proven harness is trusted as
2170: "done."
2171: 
2172: STATUS: guidance written and recorded here + §38; the actual FIXES for
2173: incidents #2 and #3 above are NOT yet implemented (see each project's
2174: own handoff doc, cited above, for the concrete next steps) - this
2175: pitfall entry documents the METHODOLOGY gap and the guard against it
2176: recurring, independent of whether every individual bug it already found
2177: has been fixed yet.
2178: 
2179: FILES: !.xyzos-standards+1.txt §38 (new); 102.agy-txt/
2180: xyz-agy-handoff-j30.txt; &.widgits/file-menu/todo-how2-j30.txt
2181: SEE ALSO: PITFALL 60/61/62 (the earlier real-app-testing-finds-real-bugs
2182: pattern this pitfall is a refinement of, not a departure from - §36.6
2183: itself stays correct, this narrows what "real key injection" must cover
2184: to actually satisfy it)
2185: 
2186: PITFALL 64: two more real file-menu bugs a synthetic harness could not
2187:             see - selecting a real listed file required an unsignaled
2188:             SECOND Enter to load, and a digit keypress activated the
2189:             main menu option in the same keystroke instead of only
2190:             navigating to it
2191: =======================================================================
2192: DATE: 2026-07-30
2193: FOUND WHILE: live human testing of file-menu's own real UI, the SAME
2194: day PITFALL 63's own three bugs were fixed and their fix was verified
2195: via a NEW real black-box harness (test-harn-ed-app/scenarios/
2196: demo_fm_typing_ux.sh) - that harness itself still could not see either
2197: bug below, because it always typed/pasted paths directly and always
2198: sent a digit press immediately followed by a real Enter, never once
2199: selecting a listed entry or checking state between the digit and the
2200: Enter separately.
2201: 
2202: BUG A - selecting a real file from the directory listing didn't load
2203: it: `fm_menu_input.c`'s own file-entry-selection branch (inside
2204: `handle_file_browser()`) only ever wrote `path_buffer` and moved
2205: `cursor_pos` to the FILE field - it never submitted. A real user
2206: arrow-navigating to a real listed file and pressing Enter ONCE (the
2207: obvious, expected action - "select this file") saw nothing happen; a
2208: SECOND Enter, now on the FILE field, was required, with no on-screen
2209: signal that a second press was needed. FIX: selecting a file entry
2210: while `browse_mode == "load"` now calls `enqueue_cmd_with_path("LOAD",
2211: full_path)` directly - real, immediate load on one real Enter.
2212: SAVE_AS's own identical-looking selection deliberately stays as
2213: pre-fill-then-confirm (protects against an accidental overwrite from
2214: one stray keypress) - not an oversight, a real, considered asymmetry.
2215: 
2216: BUG B - digit press on the main menu activated immediately, skipping
2217: Enter entirely: `handle_main_menu()`'s own digit-key branch
2218: (`key >= 49 && key <= 54`) moved the cursor AND called
2219: `execute_menu_option()` on the SAME keystroke. Real chtpm digit-jump
2220: (`isdigit(key)` -> `do_jump()`, the standard every chtpm-native
2221: buttoned layout in this house already follows - confirmed this same
2222: week for 102.editor/102.agy-txt) moves focus only; a separate Enter
2223: activates. File-menu's own hand-built nav never matched this standard
2224: - direct report: "when i press nav index # of load option it goes
2225: straight to new window, doesn't wait for enter - this isn't std chtpm
2226: behavior." FIX: digit press now only writes `cursor_pos`, matching
2227: arrow-nav's own existing behavior; the pre-existing Enter branch
2228: already did the activation half correctly.
2229: 
2230: BUG B SIDE-EFFECT - a digit press inside file_browser mode (SEARCH/
2231: FILE fields) was previously indistinguishable from typed text:
2232: pressing "2" while on the SEARCH field (the default landing row) typed
2233: a literal "2" into the search box instead of doing anything nav-
2234: shaped, because file_browser mode had NO digit-jump concept at all -
2235: direct report: "when i press number to nav to file index it instead
2236: inputs into search." FIX: `field_active` (PITFALL 63's own real focus-
2237: state) is exactly the right disambiguator - a digit pressed while NOT
2238: actively engaged in a field now jumps `cursor_pos` to that row (1-based
2239: on screen -> 0-based internally, confirmed via a live off-by-one catch:
2240: the first version of this fix jumped to the WRONG row, landing on BACK
2241: instead of FILE when the user pressed the on-screen "2." - fixed by
2242: converting `key - '0' - 1`); a digit pressed WHILE actively typing
2243: still types normally (needed for real filenames containing digits,
2244: e.g. "file2.txt").
2245: 
2246: VERIFIED LIVE: new harness scenario, test-harn-ed-app/scenarios/
2247: demo_fm_click_to_load.sh - real digit-press-alone state assertion
2248: (mode stays main_menu, cursor moves), real separate-Enter activation,
2249: real SEARCH-filter down to a unique fixture (deterministic listing
2250: index regardless of whatever else is in the user's own real documents/
2251: folder), real arrow-nav to the listed entry, ONE real Enter, real
2252: editor_buffer.txt + current_frame.txt assertions - 5/5 PASS. Full
2253: four-scenario regression (demo_save.sh, demo_load.sh,
2254: demo_fm_typing_ux.sh, demo_fm_click_to_load.sh) re-run clean.

2270: DATE: 2026-07-30
2271: FOUND WHILE: rebuilding 102.agy-txt's own file browser (PLAN.md §4
2272: Phase T4/T6 follow-up), caught directly by the user's own domain
2273: knowledge, not by any verification pass of mine.
2274: 
2275: ROOT CAUSE: the file browser's directory listing/SEARCH/FILE fields
2276: were built as fully buttonless (matching &.widgits/file-menu's own
2277: real architecture) with the OP hand-composing "[>]"/"[^]"/"[ ]" text
2278: markers itself, based on cursor_pos tracked in a custom state file.
2279: This directly violates a real, already-cited, already-followed
2280: in-house standard: `101.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️️+18.01/ops/compose_frame.c`'s own
2281: `write_panel_gui_state()` (a REAL, shipped implementation, read in
2282: full) cites `DYNAMIC_SUBMENUS_FUNCTIONAL_STD.md` directly: "the PARSER
2283: owns nav/focus/highlighting entirely; the MANAGER only ever projects
2284: raw button markup... never hand-draws its own '[>]'/'[ ]' markers."
2285: mutaclysm already does this correctly (real `<button onClick="KEY:n">`
2286: rows, dynamically generated, `${var}`-injected) - this was sitting in
2287: the SAME house, one project away, and was never checked before
2288: building on file-menu's shape instead, because file-menu was the
2289: model explicitly asked to be reused for its OWN ops (a real, correct
2290: instruction) - the mistake was treating that as license to also copy
2291: file-menu's overall SCREEN ARCHITECTURE without separately checking
2292: whether a more directly-comparable in-house precedent (same PAL-native
2293: family, not a cross-project widget) did it differently.
2294: 
2295: FURTHER FINDING, SAME INVESTIGATION: the STANDARDS DOC itself
2296: (`DYNAMIC_SUBMENUS_FUNCTIONAL_STD.md`) turned out to be partially
2297: inapplicable to this house's own runtime - it says to avoid
2298: `onClick="KEY:n"` and use string commands (`OP:`/`SET_` prefixes)
2299: instead, correctly describing TPMOS's own real reference manager
2300: (confirmed: `agy-text-editor_manager.c` really does this). But THIS
2301: house's own `prisc+x` VM (`014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/system/prisc+x.c`)
2302: has `OP_READ_HISTORY` hard-coded to `fscanf(hf, "%d", &key)` - integer
2303: ONLY, a real, verified VM limitation, not a stylistic choice - string
2304: commands genuinely cannot pass through this specific opcode as it
2305: exists today. mutaclysm's own `KEY:n`-based deviation from the
2306: "official" doc was itself CORRECT for this constraint, not an error -
2307: confirmed by reading the VM source directly rather than trusting
2308: either the doc or the comment. See `#.haiku+/jul30-house-refactor.txt`
2309: for the full chain of findings and the two real, separately-scoped
2310: paths forward (a native C manager per screen that needs string
2311: commands - low risk, project-scoped, real precedent already exists in
2312: `101.ledger-player-npc-simple+3/system/game_manager.c` - vs. extending
2313: prisc+x itself - real, but house-wide blast radius, deliberately
2314: deferred).
2315: 
2316: LESSON: "the standard doc says X" and "the comparable shipped code
2317: does X" are two different claims - when they disagree, trust the
2318: shipped code, and go find out WHY before assuming the doc is simply
2319: wrong (here, the doc wasn't wrong for ITS OWN reference runtime, it
2320: was incomplete for describing a DIFFERENT runtime's own real, verified
2321: constraint). And before adopting an architecture from a REUSED
2322: reference (file-menu, reused correctly per explicit instruction for
2323: its OWN ops), separately check whether a MORE directly comparable
2324: in-house precedent (same project family, not a cross-category widget)
2325: already solved the exact same problem a different way.
2326: 
2327: STATUS: not yet fixed - 102.agy-txt's file browser is being rebuilt on
2328: the real pattern (native C manager, real dynamic button markup,
2329: mirroring TPMOS's own agy-text-editor_manager.c + mutaclysm's own real
2330: `${var}`-markup convention), not patched in place. See
2331: `#.haiku+/jul30-house-refactor.txt` §6 for the concrete rebuild steps
2332: and `!.xyzos-2do.txt` for the deferred prisc+x string-command
2333: extension (Path B).

2352: FOUND WHILE: prototyping a per-screen module split in 01.muchi-pals-
2353: 🥚️-13.01 (dox/06-per-screen-module-standard.md), live-caught directly
2354: by the user during real testing: selecting egg_7 from the pets list

2372:   041.pal-chain⛓️, 041.pal-forum👥️, 044.pal-chat-irc👥️+2,

2375:   300.rpg-xyz, 300.rtp-xyz

2378: `yz.muchiverse/2.muchi-verse/shared-ops/`) doesn't exist anywhere in

2392: is house-wide, check whether the target project's own copy of that
2393: file is a live sync or an independently-diverged local fork - this
