2402: =======================================================================
2403: PITFALL 67: GL-mode typing doubling (hi -> hhii) - a redundant
2404:              double-writer into interact_relay.txt that survived for
2405:              DAYS because the app's real binaries never got rebuilt,
2406:              and the launcher's own EDITOR_DIR glob points at a
2407:              different project than the handoffs claimed
2408: =======================================================================
2409: DATE: 2026-07-31 (fixed same day after the long-running investigation
2410: documented in #.haiku+/editor-app-gl-dub-j32.txt)
2411: FOUND WHILE: @.apps/text-editor-xyz, `bash button.sh run`, real DISPLAY
2412: set (real GLUT/GL window). Typing "hi" into the editor's INTERACT-
2413: engaged canvas landed as "hhii" in editor_buffer.txt, confirmed by the
2414: user multiple times. Headless (`NO_GL=1`) never doubled. That GL-only /
2415: GL-off split was the one reliable behavioral clue, and it pointed the
2416: entire (long, exhausting, initially unsuccessful) investigation at
2417: gl_mirror.c - which was correct, but only half the story.
2418: 
2419: SYMPTOM:
2420:   - GL enabled -> every character doubles (hi -> hhii).
2421:   - NO_GL=1 headless -> no doubling.
2422:   - file-menu's own GL widget also "acting crazy" alongside the editor
2423:     (separate symptom, same family of stale-process/file-sharing bugs).
2424: 
2425: ROOT CAUSE (two bugs stacked, plus one that poisoned the investigation):
2426:   1. REDUNDANT DOUBLE-WRITER DESIGN. Two independent code paths could
2427:      each write a keystroke into pieces/apps/player_app/
2428:      interact_relay.txt, which is the editor's typing channel:
2429:        a) gl_mirror.c's append_key() originally wrote interact_relay.txt
2430:           DIRECTLY (unconditional), and
2431:        b) chtpm_parser_pal.c's INTERACT branch also inject_raw_key()'d
2432:           the same key into interact_relay.txt.
2433:      The only thing keeping that from doubling was a lock:
2434:        c) gl_focus.lock - created by gl_mirror at startup, removed at
2435:           exit; chtpm_parser_pal.c:3547-3550 checked access(lock)==0
2436:           and SKIPPED its own forward whenever the lock existed
2437:           ("gl is alive, so gl already delivered this key").
2438:   2. THE LOCK IS A LIVENESS FLAG, NOT A FOCUS FLAG - and it's resolved
2439:      through each process's OWN cwd/PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT. gl_mirror
2440:      builds the path from ITS root (gl_mirror.c:196); the parser from
2441:      ITS root. Any wrong-cwd gl_mirror, any stale duplicate from an old
2442:      session, or any SIGHUP-killed gl_mirror whose atexit() never ran
2443:      (fixed later, gl_mirror.c:475-484) leaves the lock absent/misplaced
2444:      while gl_mirror is still alive and delivering -> BOTH writers fire
2445:      per keystroke -> hi -> hhii. In NO_GL mode there is exactly ONE
2446:      writer (keyboard_input.c), so it can never double - the observed
2447:      GL-only split.
2448:   3. THE INVESTIGATION-POISONING BUG (why "huge confusion for a long
2449:      time"): @.apps/text-editor-xyz/button.sh resolves its editor via
2450:      EDITOR_DIR="$(ls -d "$HOUSE_DIR/102."*"editor"* | head -1)" which
2451:      matches **102.editor-📄️00.00**, NOT 102.agy-txt. 102.editor keeps
2452:      its OWN local chtpm_parser_pal.c (independent, already-gate-free
2453:      lineage) AND was running a Jul-30 gl_mirror binary that STILL had
2454:      the direct interact_relay.txt write. The earlier investigation
2455:      rebuilt/fixed only wsr-pal + agy-txt + file-menu and declared the
2456:      lock the culprit - but the app's REAL binaries (under 102.editor)
2457:      were never rebuilt, so the doubling survived. The handoff
2458:      (editor-app-gl-dub-j32.txt §7) claimed the app reads wsr-pal's
2459:      "live copy, no local copy of their own" - that was false for
2460:      102.editor, which has its own source AND stale binaries.
2461: 
2462: FIX (Option B - single writer, delete the lock entirely):
2463:   - 014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/system/chtpm_parser_pal.c: deleted the 4-line
2464:     gl_focus.lock gate in the INTERACT branch; inject_raw_key(eff) is
2465:     now unconditional. The parser relay is the SOLE path into
2466:     interact_relay.txt; doubling is structurally impossible.
2467:   - Rebuilt wsr-pal, then redeployed the whole copy chain:
2468:       102.agy-txt      (bash scripts/build.sh - copies from wsr-pal)
2469:       102.editor-📄️00.00 (bash scripts/build.sh - copies gl_mirror,
2470:                         builds its OWN parser from its own source)
2471:       &.widgits/file-menu (bash scripts/build.sh - copies from wsr-pal)
2472:   - Verified: all 4 parsers have gl_focus string count 0; all 4
2473:     gl_mirror binaries identical (md5 8107eb78e9...), append_key() has
2474:     NO interact_relay fopen (remaining "interact_relay" strings are
2475:     only the unused buffer var + snprintf init, gl_mirror.c:121/195).
2476:   - No stale house processes were running; run `bash EMERGENCY_KILL.sh`
2477:     before any retest so a rebuilt binary isn't still locked in memory
2478:     ("Text file busy").
2479: 
2480: LESSON: this is the second time (PITFALL 66) this family burned hours
2481: because a fix made in a "canonical" file didn't actually reach the
2482: project the user runs. TWO independent traps compound here:
2483:   a) The redundant double-writer + liveness-lock "arbiter" is a
2484:      minefield by design. Single-writer beats clever lock logic: if
2485:      exactly one code path can write to a file, doubling is impossible
2486:      by construction, and focus/ownership questions go away.
2487:   b) Before trusting any handoff or "live copy" claim, resolve the
2488:      launcher's OWN path resolution and check the ACTUAL binaries the
2489:      app runs (md5, mtime, strings). A stale binary on disk - even one
2490:      whose source was "fixed" elsewhere - reproduces the bug forever.
2491: FILES: 014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/system/chtpm_parser_pal.c (gate removed);
2492: 014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/system/gl_mirror.c (direct write already removed
2493: 2026-07-31, now verified propagated); 102.editor-📄️00.00/system/ (the
2494: real EDITOR_DIR target, stale gl_mirror replaced); @.apps/text-editor-
2495: xyz/button.sh (EDITOR_DIR glob = 102.editor-📄️00.00, not agy-txt);
2496: #.haiku+/double-wr-grok-j31.txt (full root-cause report + options A/B)

2500: 
2501: =======================================================================
2502: PITFALL 68: a manager daemon's OWN custom gui_state.txt variable
2503:             renders empty forever, even though the file has correct
2504:             fresh content on disk - bare ${var} raw-markup injection
2505:             points only re-resolve when piece_methods/active_target_id/
2506:             input_text specifically change, not on any gui_state.txt
2507:             write
2508: =======================================================================
2509: DATE: 2026-07-31
2510: SEVERITY: MEDIUM (silent - no error anywhere, the daemon appears to
2511:            work perfectly by every other measure: file writes succeed,
2512:            the process stays alive, other ${var}-wrapped-in-<text>
2513:            substitutions on the SAME layout update correctly)
2514: 
2515: SYMPTOM: building rtp_manager.c (300.rtp-xyz's own new save/load
2516: daemon, modeled on slop-ed-dev_manager.c), a bare `${save_list_markup}`
2517: injection point (same shape as `${piece_methods}`/`${game_map}` -
2518: outside any `<text label="...">` wrapper, meant to inject real
2519: `<button>` markup) rendered as literally nothing on first screen
2520: arrival, and NEVER updated after a real save was created either -
2521: despite `pieces/apps/player_app/manager/gui_state.txt` having the
2522: correct, fresh `save_list_markup=...` line on disk every single time
2523: it was checked. Meanwhile a SEPARATE variable on the exact same
2524: layout, `${last_message}` (wrapped in `<text label="  ${last_message}"
2525: />`), updated correctly on every real frame.
2526: 
2527: ROOT CAUSE: chtpm_parser_pal.c's own compose_frame() only re-runs
2528: parse_chtm() (the pass that re-does raw/bare markup substitution -
2529: i.e. rebuilds the parsed XML tree with fresh injected content) when
2530: ONE of three SPECIFIC variables changes: `piece_methods`,
2531: `active_target_id`, or `input_text` (a hardcoded dirty-check, not
2532: generic to "any gui_state.txt variable changed"). A custom variable
2533: name like `save_list_markup` DOES get loaded into the in-memory var
2534: table via load_vars()/load_state_file()/set_var() every frame - `get_
2535: var("save_list_markup")` would return the fresh value if you asked for
2536: it - but nothing ever asks, because the ALREADY-PARSED XML element
2537: tree (built once, at the last real parse_chtm() call) is what actually
2538: gets rendered, and that tree's own copy of the bare injection point is
2539: frozen at whatever it resolved to the first time. Variables used
2540: INSIDE an existing `<text label="${var}">` wrapper don't have this
2541: problem - substituting `${var}` into an already-existing text element
2542: happens on every frame, no reparse needed; it's specifically the RAW/
2543: BARE injection shape (used for multi-element dynamic content like a
2544: button list) that's gated behind the three-variable dirty-check.
2545: 
2546: FIX: publish the real dynamic markup AS `piece_methods` itself, not a
2547: custom variable name - already wired to force a reparse whenever its
2548: value changes. Safe for any layout with no active_target_id/hero
2549: context of its own (load_dynamic_methods() only overwrites
2550: piece_methods when active_target_id is non-empty, so a layout with no
2551: such context never fights your daemon for that variable). Confirmed
2552: live: renaming rtp_manager.c's own `save_list_markup=` gui_state.txt
2553: line to `piece_methods=` and project_menu.chtpm's own `${save_list_
2554: markup}` reference to `${piece_methods}` made the real save list
2555: render correctly on first arrival AND update correctly after a real
2556: save was created, with zero other changes.
2557: 
2558: LESSON: before assuming a new manager daemon's own custom dynamic-
2559: markup variable will "just work" because `${piece_methods}` does,
2560: check WHICH SPECIFIC variables actually trigger a reparse in compose_
2561: frame() - it's a hardcoded short list, not a generic mechanism. If you
2562: need a NEW kind of dynamic multi-element content on a layout with no
2563: competing active_target_id, riding on `piece_methods` is the real,
2564: proven way to get it to redraw; inventing a new variable name for raw/
2565: bare injection will compile, run, and silently never update.
2566: FILES: 300.rtp-xyz/manager/rtp_manager.c (write_gui_state(), the
2567: piece_methods= line + its own header comment with this citation);
2568: 300.rtp-xyz/pieces/chtpm/layouts/project_menu.chtpm (${piece_methods}
2569: reference); 300.rtp-xyz/system/chtpm_parser_pal.c compose_frame()
2570: (read directly to find this - the methods_changed/active_changed/
2571: input_changed dirty-check, ~line 2650-2665)
2572: SEE ALSO: 300.rtp-xyz/save-load-report-j31.txt (full build report,
2573: 4 other real bugs found in the same daemon build - onClick prefix
2574: requirement is PITFALL #99 already documented elsewhere in this file,
2575: search for it)

2577: =======================================================================
2578: PITFALL 69: A SMALL LOCAL LLM (gemma3:270m) CANNOT RELIABLY SELF-CLASSIFY
2579:             INTO A BINARY VERDICT ZERO-SHOT, EVEN WHEN IT DEMONSTRABLY
2580:             HAS THE UNDERLYING KNOWLEDGE — DECOUPLE "DESCRIBE" (WORKS)
2581:             FROM "CLASSIFY" (DOESN'T) INTO A DETERMINISTIC SCORER
2582:             BEFORE REACHING FOR A BIGGER MODEL
2583: =======================================================================
2584: DATE: 2026-08-02
2585: SEVERITY: HIGH (any project asking a small local LLM to render a judge/
2586:           approve-reject/win-lose verdict will silently get near-
2587:           meaningless output unless this pattern is followed)
2588: PROJECT FOUND: @.apps/my-biotech (its own "FDA_REVIEW" mechanic - a
2589:           Gemma call meant to read a real research dossier and render
2590:           APPROVED/REJECTED)
2591: 
2592: SYMPTOM: A prompt asking gemma3:270m to directly classify content into
2593: a binary verdict ("Answer with just APPROVED or REJECTED") produces
2594: answers that look plausible but are NOT actually responsive to the
2595: content - live-tested against two deliberately extreme dossiers (one
2596: describing a compound that "causes immediate cardiac arrest in 100% of
2597: test subjects... banned as a chemical weapon," one describing Vitamin
2598: C), 270m answered APPROVED for the LETHAL compound 2 times out of 3,
2599: and when the question was reframed as SAFE/DANGEROUS (still binary,
2600: more concrete framing) it answered SAFE for the lethal compound 3/3
2601: times - even with the danger-option listed FIRST in the prompt (ruling
2602: out simple order/anchoring bias). Asking it to also explain its verdict
2603: produced ZERO real explanatory content ("Approved", "AN APPROVED" - no
2604: substance at all), the clearest signal that it was not reasoning about
2605: the content, just completing toward a default-positive-sounding token.
2606: 
2607: ROOT CAUSE: this is NOT the same failure mode as Pitfall (see
2608: 045.muchi-pal-agent🤖️+1's own gemma_strategy.c precedent, "gemma is too
2609: small to reliably follow a TOOL: format") - that finding was about
2610: STRUCTURED OUTPUT FORMAT reliability. This is different and, at first,
2611: counter-intuitive: 270m demonstrably HAS real, accurate knowledge about
2612: the exact same content when asked to freely describe/recall it (the
2613: SAME two test dossiers, when 270m was asked "Describe the safety
2614: concerns of this compound in one sentence" instead of "APPROVED or
2615: REJECTED," answered "highly toxic and potentially fatal... severe side
2616: effects" for the lethal compound and "generally safe... mild stomach
2617: upset" for Vitamin C, 6/6 trials both directions, real content-aware
2618: language every time). The model's OPEN-ENDED recall/description
2619: capability is genuinely good; its ZERO-SHOT SELF-CLASSIFICATION
2620: capability (collapsing that same knowledge into a binary decision) is
2621: not - a real, measured capability gap between "say what you know" and
2622: "judge what you know," specific to a model this small.
2623: 
2624: FIX (the real, working pattern, live-verified 2026-08-02): do NOT ask
2625: the small model to classify. Ask it an OPEN-ENDED DESCRIBE question
2626: (the thing it's actually good at), then run your OWN deterministic
2627: keyword/sentiment scorer over that description text to derive the
2628: verdict - the exact same "deterministic dispatch over free text, never
2629: another LLM self-judgment" doctrine gemma_strategy.c already
2630: established for tool-detection, now confirmed to generalize to
2631: judge/verdict tasks too:
2632: 
2633:   1. Prompt: "Describe the safety concerns of <X> in one sentence."
2634:      (open-ended, not "APPROVED/REJECTED" or "SAFE/DANGEROUS")
2635:   2. Run a small, auditable, hand-written keyword scorer over the real
2636:      response text (danger-words: toxic/fatal/lethal/dangerous/deadly/
2637:      poison/banned/severe/risk/harm/hazard/death/unsafe vs. safe-words:
2638:      safe/mild/beneficial/well-tolerated/none known/rare - count
2639:      occurrences, danger_count > safe_count -> REJECTED, tie defaults
2640:      to REJECTED per this house's own "honest default on inconclusive"
2641:      convention)
2642:   3. Verdict is now derived from OUR OWN code, not the model's own
2643:      unreliable self-judgment - fully auditable, matches this whole
2644:      project family's transparency ethos, and the real description
2645:      text is itself the player-visible "explanation" for the verdict,
2646:      not a fabricated one.
2647: 
2648: TRIED AND REJECTED before landing on the above (documented so a future
2649: agent doesn't re-waste the same effort): switching the classify call to
2650: a bigger model (gemma3:1b) DID work (6/6 correct, real content-aware
2651: explanations) - but the describe+deterministic-score approach on the
2652: SAME small 270m model also reached 6/6 correct with real explanations,
2653: without needing the bigger model at all. Direct user framing, worth
2654: repeating as a standing rule: "i think its a big deal that we try to
2655: make it work b4 going up 2 larger model" - always try this
2656: decouple-describe-from-classify pattern FIRST, on whatever model is
2657: already in use, before reaching for a bigger one. A bigger model is a
2658: real, valid fallback if this pattern still doesn't work for a given
2659: task, not the first thing to reach for.
2660: 
2661: FILES: @.apps/my-biotech/ops/mybiotech_fda_verdict.c (own header
2662:        comment has the full three-approach experiment trail),
2663:        @.apps/my-biotech/ops/mybiotech_research_worker.c (FDA_REVIEW
2664:        step), @.apps/my-biotech/test-harn-same/scenarios/
2665:        demo_fda_review_discrimination.sh (the real, repeatable,
2666:        live-gemma-call harness this finding was measured with - reuse
2667:        its shape for any other project's own judge/verdict mechanic)
2668: SEE ALSO: @.apps/my-lawyer/MY_LAWYER_DESIGN.md (its own judge mechanic,
2669:        §4, reads two real documents and picks a winner - the SAME
2670:        decouple-describe-from-classify pattern applies directly there:
2671:        ask gemma to describe/compare the two cases in prose, THEN
2672:        deterministically score which side's description reads
2673:        stronger, rather than asking it to directly output "A or B")
2674: 
2675: =======================================================================
2676: END OF XYZOS-PITFALLS
2677: Add new pitfalls above this line as they are discovered.
2678: ========================================================================

<content>
1: ================================================================================
2: LPNS +3 VERSIONS - FULL CHTPM INTEGRATION ARCHITECTURE
3: Template for all future apps/games
4: Fixed: 2026-07-23
5: ================================================================================
6: 
7: THIS IS THE CORRECT ARCHITECTURE TO USE GOING FORWARD
8: =====================================================
9: 
10: SIGNAL FLOW (DATA THROUGH FILES)
11: ================================
12: 
13:   User Types 'w'
14:        ↓
15:   system/keyboard_input
16:        ↓ (writes byte as decimal keycode)
17:   pieces/keyboard/history.txt
18:        ↓ (read by)
19:   system/chtpm_parser_pal
20:        ↓ (parses layout, injects matching key)
21:   pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt
22:        ↓ (read by prisc+x via read_history)
23:   pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal
24:        ↓ (decodes keycode 119='w', dispatches action)
25:   ops/* (game_turn_input, word_turn_input, etc)
26:        ↓ (updates ledger, state)
27:   data/master_ledger.txt
28:        ↓ (read by)
29:   ops/game_compose_frame
30:        ↓ (writes rendered output)
31:   pieces/display/current_frame.txt
32:        ↓ (grows renderer_pulse.txt to signal)
33:   pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt
34:        ↓ (watched by)
35:   system/renderer
36:        ↓ (reads and outputs to terminal)
37:   Terminal
38: 
39: PROCESS ARCHITECTURE (FOUR PROCESSES)
40: ======================================
41: 
42: 1. system/keyboard_input (FOREGROUND, BLOCKING)
43:    - Puts stdin in raw mode (no echo, no line buffering)
44:    - Reads every keystroke
45:    - Writes decimal keycode to pieces/keyboard/history.txt
46:    - Exits on Ctrl+C (keycode 3), sets pieces/system/quit_flag.txt
47:    - Restores terminal on exit
48: 
49:    Why foreground: Owns the terminal exclusively, must not share it
50: 
51: 2. system/renderer (BACKGROUND)
52:    - Loop: watch pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt file size
53:    - When file grows: read pieces/display/current_frame.txt
54:    - Output to terminal with "--- FRAME UPDATE ---" markers
55:    - Exits when pieces/system/quit_flag.txt becomes non-empty
56: 
57:    Why background: Runs independently, freed from terminal duties
58: 
59: 3. system/chtpm_parser_pal (BACKGROUND)
60:    - Parses .chtpm layout file (XML-like syntax)
61:    - Extracts <module> tag → launches prisc+x with that path
62:    - Reads pieces/keyboard/history.txt continuously
63:    - Filters/relays keycodes to pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt
64:    - Manages pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt (active_target_id, etc)
65: 
66:    Why background: Runs independent loop, manages module lifecycle
67: 
68: 4. system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal (BACKGROUND, auto-launched by chtpm_parser_pal)
69:    - Event loop: reads from interact_relay.txt
70:    - Decodes keycodes → dispatches to ops (game_turn_input, etc)
71:    - Ops update data/master_ledger.txt
72:    - Calls compose_frame op → writes current_frame.txt
73:    - Grows renderer_pulse.txt to signal frame ready
74:    - Never exits on its own (persistent across screen changes)
75: 
76:    Why background: Runs game logic loop independently
77: 
78: LAUNCH ORDER (CRITICAL)
79: =======================
80: 
81: The sequence in button.sh matters:
82: 
83: 1. Initialize all state files to known state (empty)
84: 2. Launch renderer FIRST (so it's ready when frames appear)
85: 3. Launch chtpm_parser_pal SECOND (it auto-launches prisc+x via <module> tag)
86: 4. Launch keyboard_input LAST (takes foreground, blocking)
87: 
88: This ensures the rendering pipeline is ready before any input is processed.
89: 
90: CHTPM LAYOUT FILE STRUCTURE (.chtpm format)
91: ===========================================
92: 
93:   <panel>
94:     <module>system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal</module>
95:     <interact src="pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt" />
96: 
97:     <text label="Display static text" /><br/>
98: 
99:     <button label="[W] Say Word" onClick="ACTIVATE_WORD">
100:       <text label="Description" /><br/>
101:     </button><br/>
102: 
103:     <button label="[E] End Turn" onClick="ACTIVATE_END">
104:       <text label="End your turn" /><br/>
105:     </button><br/>
106: 
107:     <text label="${game_map}" /><br/>
108:   </panel>
109: 
110: Key elements:
111: 
112: - <module>PATH</module>: Path to prisc+x and .pal file to launch
113: - <interact src="PATH" />: File to relay keycodes to
114: - <text label="..." />: Static or dynamic (${placeholder}) output
115: - <button label="..." onClick="ACTION">: Menu item (sent to prisc+x as keycode)
116: - ${game_map}, ${game_recent}, etc: Placeholder substitution (handled by prisc+x ops)
117: 
118: KEYCODE DISPATCH (.pal file)
119: ============================
120: 
121: The .pal file reads keycodes and dispatches:
122: 
123:   read_history pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt x2, x1
124:   # x2 now contains the keycode as a decimal number (119 = 'w')
125: 
126:   li x3, 119        # 'w'
127:   beq x2, x3, do_word
128: 
129:   li x3, 101        # 'e'
130:   beq x2, x3, do_end
131: 
132:   # Dispatch to op with action code:
133: do_word:
134:   game_turn_input 1  # action=1 (word)
135: 
136: do_end:
137:   game_turn_input 3  # action=3 (end_turn)
138: 
139: COMMON ASCII KEYCODES
140: =====================
141: 
142: - 'w' = 119 (word)
143: - 'm' = 109 (move)
144: - 'e' = 101 (end turn)
145: - 'q' = 113 (quit - handled by keyboard_input, doesn't reach prisc+x)
146: - '1'-'9' = 49-57 (menu selections)
147: - Arrow Up    = 1002 (ARROW_UP)
148: - Arrow Down  = 1003 (ARROW_DOWN)
149: - Arrow Left  = 1000 (ARROW_LEFT)
150: - Arrow Right = 1001 (ARROW_RIGHT)
151: 
152: STATE FILES (The Source of Truth)
153: ==================================
154: 
155: pieces/keyboard/history.txt
156:   Format: "KEY_PRESSED: <keycode>\n" (one per line)
157:   Written by: system/keyboard_input
158:   Read by: system/chtpm_parser_pal
159:   Cleared at: startup (but chtpm_parser reads from position, not rewind)
160:   Purpose: Raw keystroke log for chtpm to filter
161: 
162: pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt
163:   Format: "<keycode>\n" (decimal, one per line)
164:   Written by: system/chtpm_parser_pal (via inject_raw_key())
165:   Read by: pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal (via read_history)
166:   Cleared at: startup
167:   Purpose: Filtered keycodes that prisc+x processes
168: 
169: pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt
170:   Format: KEY=VALUE (one per line)
171:   Contains: module_path, project_id, active_target_id, etc
172:   Written by: button.sh at startup, chtpm_parser_pal during runtime
173:   Read by: prisc+x ops (for placeholders like ${game_map})
174:   Purpose: Session state that persists across screen changes
175: 
176: pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt
177:   Format: "<keycode>\n" (decimal, one per line)
178:   Written by: system/keyboard_input (same as interact_relay, for compat)
179:   Purpose: Backup key log (can be used if interact_relay processing fails)
180: 
181: pieces/display/current_frame.txt
182:   Format: Multi-line text (game frame output)
183:   Written by: game_compose_frame.c, word_compose_frame.c (prisc+x ops)
184:   Read by: system/renderer
185:   Purpose: The rendered game state to display
186: 
187: pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt
188:   Format: Each byte is "x" (marker file, size is what matters)
189:   Written by: game_compose_frame.c (appends 'x') after writing current_frame.txt
190:   Watched by: system/renderer (detects size growth)
191:   Purpose: Signal that a new frame is ready
192: 
193: pieces/display/wsr_screen_changed.txt
194:   Format: Each byte is "x" (marker file, size is what matters)
195:   Written by: wsr_menu_input op when active_target_id changes
196:   Watched by: pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal (detects size growth)
197:   Purpose: Signal that screen navigated (href) to a different layout
198: 
199: pieces/system/quit_flag.txt
200:   Format: Any non-empty content
201:   Written by: system/keyboard_input (on Ctrl+C)
202:   Watched by: system/renderer (exits loop when non-empty)
203:   Purpose: Global quit signal
204: 
205: data/master_ledger.txt
206:   Format: timestamp|epoch|player|turn|action_data|action_type
207:   Written by: game_turn_input op (appends new turn records)
208:   Read by: game_compose_frame (reconstructs game state)
209:   Purpose: Ledger-driven authoritative game state
210: 
211: OPS ARCHITECTURE (Self-Contained Binaries)
212: ===========================================
213: 
214: Each op is a standalone C program, called by prisc+x:
215: 
216: game_compose_frame (map game)
217:   Input: Reads config.txt, data/master_ledger.txt
218:   Output: Writes pieces/display/current_frame.txt
219:   Signal: Appends 'x' to pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt
220:   Purpose: Render current game state (map, actors, turn info)
221: 
222: game_hit_frame
223:   Input: None
224:   Output: Marks frame as ready
225:   Purpose: Placeholder (usually empty)
226: 
227: game_turn_input <action>
228:   Input: action code (1=word, 2=move, 3=end_turn)
229:   Output: Appends to data/master_ledger.txt
230:   Side effects: Updates config.txt (current_turn, etc)
231:   Purpose: Process player action and advance game state
232: 
233: word_compose_frame (word game)
234:   Input: Reads config.txt, data/master_ledger.txt
235:   Output: Writes pieces/display/current_frame.txt
236:   Signal: Appends 'x' to pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt
237:   Purpose: Render word game state (turn, player, recent words)
238: 
239: word_turn_input <action>
240:   Input: action code (1=word, 2=end_turn)
241:   Output: Appends to data/master_ledger.txt
242:   Side effects: Updates config.txt
243:   Purpose: Process player action
244: 
245: BUTTON.SH INITIALIZATION
246: =======================
247: 
248: The launcher must:
249: 
250: 1. Compile all ops (gcc)
251: 2. Create directory structure:
252:    - data/
253:    - pieces/display/
254:    - pieces/system/
255:    - pieces/apps/player_app/
256:    - pieces/keyboard/
257:    - pieces/chtpm/layouts/
258: 
259: 3. Initialize state files:
260:    - pieces/display/current_frame.txt (empty, will be written by first op)
261:    - pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt (empty)
262:    - pieces/display/wsr_screen_changed.txt (empty)
263:    - pieces/system/quit_flag.txt (empty)
264:    - pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt (empty)
265:    - pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt (empty)
266:    - pieces/keyboard/history.txt (empty)
267:    - pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt (with module_path, project_id, active_target_id)
268: 
269: 4. Initialize data:
270:    - data/master_ledger.txt (header row only: timestamp|epoch|player|turn|...)
271: 
272: 5. Export environment variables:
273:    - PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT=<absolute path>
274:    - PRISC_PROJECT_ID=<project name>
275: 
276: 6. Launch processes in order:
277:    a. ./system/renderer & → save PID
278:    b. ./system/chtpm_parser_pal pieces/chtpm/layouts/MAIN_LAYOUT.chtpm & → save PID
279:    c. trap to kill PIDs on EXIT/INT/TERM
280:    d. ./system/keyboard_input (blocking foreground)
281: 
282: ERROR HANDLING
283: ==============
284: 
285: Common issues and symptoms:
286: 
287: 1. "No frame output"
288:    - Check: pieces/display/current_frame.txt exists and has content
289:    - Check: pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt is growing (file size increases)
290:    - Check: renderer process is running (ps aux | grep renderer)
291:    - Fix: Ensure game_compose_frame is being called by prisc+x
292: 
293: 2. "Keys don't work"
294:    - Check: pieces/keyboard/history.txt has keycodes
295:    - Check: pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt has keycodes
296:    - Check: chtpm_parser_pal is running (ps aux | grep chtpm_parser)
297:    - Fix: Verify chtpm_parser_pal parsed layout correctly
298: 
299: 3. "Stale frames"
300:    - Check: renderer_pulse.txt is NOT growing
301:    - Cause: game_compose_frame not being called, or not appending to pulse file
302:    - Fix: Verify compose_frame op appends to pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt
303: 
304: 4. "Ctrl+C doesn't work"
305:    - Check: keyboard_input process is running (owns tty)
306:    - Check: terminal is in raw mode (no echo when typing)
307:    - Fix: Ensure keyboard_input wasn't killed prematurely
308: 
309: DEPLOYMENT CHECKLIST
310: ====================
311: 
312: ✓ Create .chtpm layout file (pieces/chtpm/layouts/NAME.chtpm)
313: ✓ Create .pal event loop (pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal)
314: ✓ Create ops (game_turn_input.c, game_compose_frame.c, etc)
315: ✓ Create button.sh launcher with full initialization
316: ✓ System/renderer compiled (gcc -o system/renderer system/renderer.c)
317: ✓ System/keyboard_input compiled (gcc -o system/keyboard_input system/keyboard_input.c)
318: ✓ System/chtpm_parser_pal copied from reference
319: ✓ Prisc+x binary exists (compiled from shared-ops)
320: ✓ All directories created at runtime (data, pieces/*, etc)
321: ✓ All marker files initialized to empty
322: ✓ State file initialized with module_path and project_id
323: ✓ Environment variables exported (PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT, etc)
324: ✓ Trap handler catches EXIT/INT/TERM
325: ✓ Cleanup script for manual process kills
326: 
327: TEMPLATE FILES TO USE
328: ====================
329: 
330: For a new game/app, copy from LPNS +3:
331: 
332: 1. button.sh (modify paths and op names)
333: 2. system/renderer.c (no changes needed)
334: 3. system/keyboard_input.c (no changes needed)
335: 4. pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal (modify op names and keycodes)
336: 5. pieces/chtpm/layouts/NAME.chtpm (customize layout and placeholders)
337: 6. ops/* (create game-specific ops)
338: 
339: REFERENCE IMPLEMENTATIONS
340: =========================
341: 
342: Working examples:
343: - 00.lpns+map+3 (map game with movement)
344: - 0.ledger-player-npc-simple+3 (word game)
345: - /path/to/wsr-pal (financial game - production reference)
346: - /path/to/mutaclsym (mutation game - complex reference)
347: 
348: ================================================================================
349: END CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt

(End of file - total 349 lines)
</content>

**Output:**

<path>/home/no/Desktop/🤖️🪤️🏠️/🥡️🪜️/🪜️-00.00/NNEST_CLEAN_PARENT/NNEST-11.15/x0.parent-level-dev-env-04.04/yz.muchiverse/44.xyz❤️‍🔥️00.10/404.architecture-bible/AI-ARCHITECTURE.txt</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: MUTACLSYM AI ARCHITECTURE
2: Registry + Self-Contained Ops pattern applied to monster behavior
3: ================================================================================
4: This follows the muchiverse pattern: REGISTRY (what behaviors exist) + SWITCH
5: (pick one) + SELF-CONTAINED OPS (do the work). Designed before built, as per
6: family convention.
7: 
8: ================================================================================
9: 1. STRATEGY REGISTRY
10: ================================================================================
11: 
12: File: pieces/registry/ai/ai_strategies.txt
13: Format: id|name|description
14: Example:
15:   0|preset_chase|Always step directly toward hero, no hesitation
16:   1|weighted_flee|Chase normally but flee if health drops below flee_hp_pct
17:   2|wander_with_detection|Random walk until hero detected, then chase
18: 
19: Each row is a STRATEGY TEMPLATE. A row defines:
20:   - id: integer code (0, 1, 2, ...) used in monster state.txt
21:   - name: human-readable label
22:   - description: what this strategy does
23: 
24: ADDING A NEW STRATEGY (the full process):
25:   1. Edit ai_strategies.txt: add a new row with an unused id
26:   2. Edit pick_ai_strategy.c:
27:      - Write a decide_<strategy_name>() function with the behavior
28:      - Add a case in main()'s switch statement that calls it
29:   3. Rebuild: gcc -o +x/pick_ai_strategy.+x ops/pick_ai_strategy.c
30:   4. No state.txt changes needed - monsters using the old ai_mode values keep
31:      working, only monsters that set ai_mode to the new id will use it.
32: 
33: RESULT: new strategy available immediately, no hardcoded constants,
34:   no recompilation of other ops needed.
35: 
36: ================================================================================
37: 2. SWITCH OPERATION
38: ================================================================================
39: 
40: File: ops/switch_ai_mode.c
41: Purpose: change a monster's ai_mode without editing state.txt by hand
42: Usage: switch_ai_mode.+x <monster_state_path> <strategy_id>
43: 
44: Example:
45:   switch_ai_mode.+x pieces/world_01/map_start/monsters/zombie_02/state.txt 2
46:   → Looks up strategy id=2 in the registry ("wander_with_detection")
47:   → Updates zombie_02/state.txt: ai_mode=2
48:   → Monster now uses wander_with_detection behavior next tick
49: 
50: INPUT CONTRACT:
51:   - monster_state_path: path to pieces/.../monsters/<id>/state.txt
52:   - strategy_id: integer from pieces/registry/ai/ai_strategies.txt
53: 
54: OUTPUT CONTRACT:
55:   - Reads registry to verify strategy_id exists
56:   - If found: rewrites ai_mode field in state.txt, returns 0
57:   - If not found: prints error to stderr, returns 1
58: 
59: DESIGN: Exactly parallel to switch_model.c in muchi-pal-agent - a dedicated
60:   op that changes one state field by registry lookup, nothing more.
61: 
62: ================================================================================
63: 3. STRATEGY OPS (the actual behavior)
64: ================================================================================
65: 
66: Files:
67:   ops/decide_chase.c
68:   ops/decide_weighted_flee.c
69:   ops/decide_wander.c
70: 
71: DESIGN: Each strategy is its OWN COMPLETE OP, not a function inside a larger file.
72: 
73: Each strategy op:
74:   - Takes command line args: state_path, hero_x, hero_y, map_path
75:   - Reads monster state from state_path (pos, hp, etc.)
76:   - Computes decision (where to move)
77:   - Outputs to stdout: "target_x target_y action_type reason"
78:   - Exit 0 on success
79: 
80: DECIDE_CHASE (ops/decide_chase.c):
81:   One-step toward hero. Simplest strategy.
82:   Usage: decide_chase.+x <state_path> <hero_x> <hero_y> <map_path>
83:   Output: "25 10 move chasing"
84: 
85: DECIDE_WEIGHTED_FLEE (ops/decide_weighted_flee.c):
86:   Chase if health > threshold, flee if health < threshold.
87:   Checks hp, max_hp, flee_hp_pct from state.txt.
88:   Usage: decide_weighted_flee.+x <state_path> <hero_x> <hero_y> <map_path>
89:   Output: "23 8 move fleeing" or "24 10 move chasing"
90: 
91: DECIDE_WANDER (ops/decide_wander.c):
92:   Random walk; detect hero by line-of-sight or sound, then chase.
93:   Most complex: needs map_path to check LOS.
94:   Usage: decide_wander.+x <state_path> <hero_x> <hero_y> <map_path>
95:   Output: "18 5 move wandering" or "24 10 move detected_and_chasing"
96: 
97: DISPATCHER: ops/pick_ai_strategy.c
98:   Main entry point. Reads ai_mode, looks up strategy in registry,
99:   shells to the appropriate decide_*.+x op, validates result,
100:   writes action.txt. Does NOT contain strategy logic itself.
101: 
102: PROPERTIES:
103:   - Each strategy is self-contained (own file, own binary, own logic)
104:   - Registry maps ai_mode → op_name (e.g., "0" → "decide_chase")
105:   - Dispatcher is generic, doesn't need to change when strategies change
106:   - New strategy = new .c file + one registry row. No other edits needed.
107: 
108: ADDING A NEW STRATEGY:
109:   1. Write ops/decide_myname.c with your decision logic
110:   2. Compile: gcc -o ops/+x/decide_myname.+x ops/decide_myname.c -lm
111:   3. Add row to pieces/registry/ai/ai_strategies.txt:
112:      3|my_strategy_name|decide_myname|description
113:   4. Done. No pick_ai_strategy.c changes needed.
114: 
115: ================================================================================
116: 4. INSTANCE STATE (monsters in the game world)
117: ================================================================================
118: 
119: File: pieces/world_01/map_start/monsters/<id>/state.txt
120: Format: key=value
121: 
122: Example (zombie_02):
123:   monster_type=zombie
124:   pos_x=23
125:   pos_y=8
126:   hp=20
127:   max_hp=20
128:   damage=3
129:   ai_mode=1
130:   flee_hp_pct=0
131: 
132: CRITICAL FIELDS FOR AI:
133:   - ai_mode: integer 0-2 (or higher if you add strategies), registry lookup
134:   - pos_x, pos_y: current location on map
135:   - hp, max_hp: health (needed by weighted_flee strategy)
136:   - flee_hp_pct: health threshold (needed by weighted_flee strategy)
137: 
138: To change a monster's strategy at runtime:
139:   switch_ai_mode.+x <path> <strategy_id>
140:   OR
141:   edit state.txt by hand and set ai_mode=<new_id>
142: 
143: No need to rebuild anything.
144: 
145: ================================================================================
146: 5. FULL FLOW (one tick example with dispatcher)
147: ================================================================================
148: 
149: Scenario: zombie_02 is at (23,8) with ai_mode=1 (weighted_flee), hp=5/20.
150: Hero is at (20,10).
151: 
152: Tick sequence:
153: 
154:   1. Game calls pick_ai_strategy.+x (the dispatcher):
155:      ./ops/+x/pick_ai_strategy.+x pieces/.../zombie_02/state.txt 20 10 map.txt
156: 
157:   2. pick_ai_strategy DISPATCHER:
158:      - Reads ai_mode=1 from state.txt
159:      - Looks up ai_mode=1 in pieces/registry/ai/ai_strategies.txt
160:      - Finds row: 1|weighted_flee|decide_weighted_flee|...
161:      - Extracts op_name: "decide_weighted_flee"
162: 
163:   3. pick_ai_strategy SHELLS TO THE STRATEGY OP:
164:      popen("./ops/+x/decide_weighted_flee.+x pieces/.../zombie_02/state.txt 20 10 map.txt", "r")
165: 
166:   4. decide_weighted_flee.+x (the strategy op):
167:      - Reads pos_x=23, pos_y=8 from state.txt
168:      - Reads hp=5, max_hp=20, flee_hp_pct=0 from state.txt
169:      - Calculates: current_hp_pct = (5*100)/20 = 25%
170:      - Checks: is 25 < 0 (flee_hp_pct)? NO
171:      - Decides: chase instead
172:      - Calculates: one step toward hero at (20,10)
173:      - Outputs to stdout: "22 8 move chasing"
174:      - Exits 0
175: 
176:   5. pick_ai_strategy READS STRATEGY OUTPUT:
177:      - Reads from pipe: "22 8 move chasing"
178:      - Parses: target=(22,8), action_type="move", reason="chasing"
179: 
180:   6. pick_ai_strategy VALIDATES:
181:      - Is (22,8) walkable? Check map.txt via glyph_walkable()
182:      - Is (22,8) the hero's position? No
183:      - All good, write action.txt: "22 8 move chasing"
184: 
185:   7. tick_monsters.c reads action.txt:
186:      - Moves zombie_02 to (22,8)
187:      - game_log records the move
188:      - Next tick, pick_ai_strategy runs again
189: 
190: KEY SEPARATION: Each strategy op is independent. The dispatcher just looks up
191:   "which op to call" in the registry and passes through the result. If you add
192:   a new strategy op, the dispatcher doesn't need to change.
193: 
194: ================================================================================
195: 6. FUTURE EXTENSION: NPC BANK
196: ================================================================================
197: 
198: CURRENT: Monster types are defined in pieces/registry/monsters/monster_types.txt
199:   (zombie, ogre, ghost, etc. - templates). Each type has stats (hp, damage,
200:   asset_id) and a default ai_mode. Individual monsters then get a state.txt
201:   with instance-specific values.
202: 
203: PLANNED (per section 4.4 of $.zest-er-summary-FIN):
204:   For broader NPC support (not just monsters), create pieces/registry/npc/
205:   npc_bank.txt with the SAME structure as monster_types.txt, but reused
206:   for any entity that has behavior (NPCs, talking NPCs, merchants, etc.).
207: 
208:   Format would be: id|type|glyph|hp|default_ai_mode|...
209: 
210:   Then pick_ai_strategy.c becomes pick_entity_strategy.c and works for
211:   any entity, not just glyphs 'Z', 'O', '?'. The ai_mode values stay the
212:   same (0=chase, 1=flee, 2=wander - or whatever the domain needs).
213: 
214: BENEFIT: Same REGISTRY + SWITCH + DECISION pattern works for humans,
215:   animals, NPCs, not just monsters. The strategy logic doesn't change,
216:   only the entity types that use it.
217: 
218:   See also: pieces/registry/terrain/, furniture/ - same pattern applied to
219:   environment. All registries follow the same pipe-delimited structure.
220: 
221: NOT YET BUILT: This is designed but not coded. Wait for an explicit request
222:   to build the NPC bank before adding it. monster_types.txt works fine for
223:   now.
224: 
225: ================================================================================
226: 7. DESIGN DECISIONS
227: ================================================================================
228: 
229: Why separate switch_ai_mode.c instead of baking it into pick_ai_strategy.c?
230:   - Follows muchiverse convention: one verb, one binary, no multi-purpose ops
231:   - Easier to test in isolation (change ai_mode, verify state.txt changes)
232:   - Easier to wire into a UI command (/ai-strategy <monster> <id>) later
233:   - Allows a game/admin tool to call it without running the full strategy
234:     decision
235: 
236: Why is the strategy logic in C, not a script?
237:   - Performance: monster AI ticks every frame, script overhead adds up
238:   - Current C code is simple enough to maintain in one file
239:   - If a strategy becomes complex, extract it to a separate op (no pattern
240:     change needed, still registry-driven)
241: 
242: Why ai_mode as integer, not string?
243:   - State file is key=value, integers are easier to parse/store
244:   - Registry maps id→name, so the human-readable part stays there
245:   - Pattern matches switch_model.c (provider_kind/api_url/model_name
246:     are strings, but current_model_id is an id in state.txt)
247: 
248: Why not hardcode the strategy names as #defines?
249:   - Defeats the whole point of registry-driven behavior
250:   - If id=3 suddenly becomes an error (because row 3 was deleted), the registry
251:     catches it, not silent undefined behavior
252:   - Adding a new strategy doesn't require touching header files
253: 
254: ================================================================================
255: 8. TESTING (file-injection style, per $.zest-er-summary-FIN sec. 3B)
256: ================================================================================
257: 
258: Test that strategy switching works:
259: 
260:   # Scenario: zombie_02 is currently in chase mode (ai_mode=0)
261:   # Goal: switch it to flee mode (ai_mode=1) and verify state.txt changed
262: 
263:   1. Check initial state:
264:      grep ai_mode pieces/world_01/map_start/monsters/zombie_02/state.txt
265:      → ai_mode=0
266: 
267:   2. Run switch_ai_mode:
268:      ./ops/+x/switch_ai_mode.+x pieces/world_01/map_start/monsters/zombie_02/state.txt 1
269: 
270:   3. Verify change:
271:      grep ai_mode pieces/world_01/map_start/monsters/zombie_02/state.txt
272:      → ai_mode=1 (changed)
273: 
274: Test that strategy execution works:
275: 
276:   1. Set a monster to wander mode (ai_mode=2):
277:      switch_ai_mode.+x pieces/.../ghost_001/state.txt 2
278: 
279:   2. Simulate a tick by calling pick_ai_strategy directly:
280:      ./ops/+x/pick_ai_strategy.+x pieces/.../ghost_001/state.txt 20 10 pieces/.../map.txt
281: 
282:   3. Check action.txt:
283:      cat pieces/.../ghost_001/action.txt
284:      → Should show a move (not toward hero if hero not in LOS/sound range)
285:      → Should show reason like "wandering"
286: 
287: HONEST LIMIT: file-injection testing verifies the DATA (registry lookup,
288:   state.txt changes) but not the RENDERING (whether a real terminal displays
289:   the monster moving correctly). For that, run a real game session and watch.
290: 
291: ================================================================================
292: 9. SUMMARY: THE PATTERN (WITH DISPATCHER)
293: ================================================================================
294: 
295: ✓ REGISTRY (ai_strategies.txt): what strategies exist (id|name|op_name|description)
296: ✓ SWITCH OP (switch_ai_mode.c): look up id in registry, update state.txt
297: ✓ DISPATCHER (pick_ai_strategy.c): read ai_mode, look up op_name, shell to it
298: ✓ STRATEGY OPS (decide_*.c): independent binaries that decide where to move
299: ✓ INSTANCE STATE (monster state.txt): which strategy is active (ai_mode=X)
300:

1: # Drag-and-Drop Pet Import Test Guide
2: 
3: ## Overview
4: 
5: This guide explains how to test the X11 Xdnd drag-and-drop functionality for importing pets from muchi-pals (egg_window) into mutaclsym (gl_mirror).
6: 
7: ## Features Implemented
8: 
9: ### Visual Feedback
10: - **Green border highlight** on gl_mirror when drag is over
11: - **Semi-transparent green overlay** after successful drop (2 seconds)
12: - **Window close** after successful import
13: 
14: ### Data Transfer
15: - **XdndSelection** transfers pet ID from egg_window to gl_mirror
16: - **Automatic import** triggers pet_import on drop
17: - **Close request** signals egg_window to close after import
18: 
19: ### Test Harness
20: - **Config file**: `drag_drop_test.pdl` (PDL format)
21: - **Automated test**: `drag_drop_test.sh`
22: - **Window positioning**: Windows poll config for positions
23: - **AI testing**: Write to config file to position windows and trigger drops
24: 
25: ## Prerequisites
26: 
27: 1. Both projects must be compiled:
28:    ```bash
29:    cd 01.muchi-pals-🥚️-13.01 && ./button.sh compile
30:    cd 101.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️️+18.00 && ./button.sh compile
31:    ```
32: 
33: 2. Ensure exchange directory exists:
34:    ```bash
35:    mkdir -p /home/no/Desktop/.../yz.muchiverse/44.xyz❤️‍🔥️00.07/exchange
36:    ```
37: 
38: ## Test Procedure
39: 
40: ### Manual Test
41: 
42: #### Step 1: Start mutaclsym with GL mirror
43: 
44: ```bash
45: cd 101.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️️+18.00
46: ./button.sh run
47: ```
48: 
49: This starts:
50: - Terminal game (ASCII view)
51: - GL mirror window ("mutaclsym RGB mirror")
52: 
53: Wait for the GL mirror window to appear.
54: 
55: #### Step 2: Start muchi-pals and open pet window
56: 
57: In a separate terminal:
58: 
59: ```bash
60: cd 01.muchi-pals-🥚️-13.01
61: ./button.sh run
62: ```
63: 
64: From the menu, select "Open Window" for a pet (e.g., egg_1).
65: 
66: This opens:
67: - Terminal menu
68: - Pet GL window (shaped window showing pet sprite)
69: 
70: #### Step 3: Test drag-drop
71: 
72: 1. **Click and hold** on the pet GL window (left mouse button)
73: 2. **Drag** the window over the mutaclsym GL mirror window
74: 3. **Observe** the green border highlight on gl_mirror
75: 4. **Release** the mouse button while over the GL mirror
76: 5. **Observe** the green overlay flash (2 seconds)
77: 6. **Watch** the pet window close automatically
78: 
79: #### Step 4: Verify the drop
80: 
81: Check for these indicators:
82: 
83: ##### Success indicators:
84: - Green border appears when dragging over gl_mirror
85: - Green overlay appears after drop
86: - Pet window closes automatically
87: - Terminal output from gl_mirror:
88:   ```
89:   gl_mirror: importing pet 'egg_1'
90:   ```
91: - Pet appears in mutaclsym's world (check terminal)
92: 
93: ##### Partial success:
94: - If you see "XdndDrop received" but the pet doesn't appear, the import may have failed
95: - Check exchange directory for the pet:
96:   ```bash
97:   ls /home/no/Desktop/.../yz.muchiverse/44.xyz❤️‍🔥️00.07/exchange/
98:   ```
99: 
100: ### Automated Test with Test Harness
101: 
102: #### Step 1: Configure test
103: 
104: Edit `drag_drop_test.pdl` to set window positions:
105: 
106: ```
107: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_x        | 100
108: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_y        | 100
109: WINDOW       | egg_window_x       | 800
110: WINDOW       | egg_window_y       | 100
111: ```
112: 
113: #### Step 2: Start applications
114: 
115: Start mutaclsym and muchi-pals as described above.
116: 
117: #### Step 3: Run test harness
118: 
119: ```bash
120: ./drag_drop_test.sh
121: ```
122: 
123: The test harness will:
124: 1. Find both windows by name
125: 2. Position them according to the config
126: 3. Simulate drag-drop using xdotool
127: 4. Verify results (pet imported, window closed, etc.)
128: 5. Take screenshots at key points
129: 
130: #### Step 4: Check results
131: 
132: - Log file: `/tmp/drag_drop_test.log`
133: - Screenshots: `/tmp/drag_drop_screenshots/`
134: 
135: ### AI Test Mode
136: 
137: For AI testing, write to the config file:
138: 
139: ```bash
140: # Update window positions
141: cat > drag_drop_test.pdl << 'EOF'
142: SECTION      | KEY                | VALUE
143: ----------------------------------------
144: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_x        | 50
145: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_y        | 50
146: WINDOW       | egg_window_x       | 700
147: WINDOW       | egg_window_y       | 50
148: EOF
149: 
150: # Wait for windows to update (polls every second)
151: sleep 2
152: 
153: # Use xdotool to simulate drag-drop
154: xdotool mousemove 740 90  # Move to egg_window center
155: xdotool mousedown 1       # Start drag
156: xdotool mousemove 370 202  # Move to gl_mirror center
157: xdotool mouseup 1         # Drop
158: ```
159: 
160: ## Config File Format
161: 
162: The `drag_drop_test.pdl` file uses pipe-delimited format:
163: 
164: ```
165: SECTION      | KEY                | VALUE
166: ----------------------------------------
167: CONFIG       | test_mode          | manual
168: CONFIG       | poll_interval_ms   | 100
169: 
170: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_x        | 100
171: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_y        | 100
172: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_w        | 640
173: WINDOW       | gl_mirror_h        | 304
174: 
175: WINDOW       | egg_window_x       | 800
176: WINDOW       | egg_window_y       | 100
177: WINDOW       | egg_window_w       | 80
178: WINDOW       | egg_window_h       | 80
179: 
180: PET          | pet_id             | egg_1
181: PET          | pet_emoji          | 🐛
182: 
183: DRAG         | start_x            | 840
184: DRAG         | start_y            | 140
185: DRAG         | end_x              | 420
186: DRAG         | end_y              | 252
187: DRAG         | steps              | 20
188: DRAG         | step_delay_ms      | 50
189: 
190: EXPECTED     | drop_accepted      | 1
191: EXPECTED     | pet_imported       | 1
192: EXPECTED     | source_closed      | 1
193: ```
194: 
195: ### Key Fields
196: 
197: - **WINDOW**: Position and size of windows
198:   - `gl_mirror_x/y`: Position of mutaclsym GL mirror
199:   - `egg_window_x/y`: Position of muchi-pals pet window
200:

  "content": "---\ntitle: \"KILOPEDIA — Anchored Summary of the Muchiverse / xyz00.10 Codebase\"\n---\n\n# KILOPEDIA — Anchored Summary of the Muchiverse Codebase\n\nThis document is an **anchored summary**: a structured index of key concepts, files,\npatterns, and conventions found in the `44.xyz00.10` muchiverse project. It exists so\nthat a fresh context window (or a new LLM agent) can rapidly orient itself without\nre-reading every file.\n\nEach entry below anchors to a real file path, line range, or code snippet. When you\nupdate the codebase, update this file in the same commit.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Project Overview\n\n```\n44.xyz00.10/\n├── #.DOX/                         # Documentation & guides\n│   ├── CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt   Full CHTPM signal-flow & process architecture\n│   ├── drag-drop-how2.md              X11 drag-drop pet import test guide\n│   └── (GPT-4o.md)                    AI assistant conversation (if present)\n├── 404.architecture-bible/\n│   └── AI-ARCHITECTURE.txt            Mutaclsym AI: Registry + Dispatcher + Strategy Ops\n├── 014.wsr-pal💸️📌️+2/               WSR-PAL financial game (production reference)\n├── 101.mutaclsym🧟‍♂️+18.01/             Mutation game (complex reference)\n├── 101.ledger-player-npc-simple+3/    LPNS word game (word-game reference)\n├── 202.snes-civ/                      SNES Civ emulator\n├── 203.gb-pokemon/                    Game Boy Pokemon emulator\n├── @.apps/                            App widgets, strategies\n├── %.harnesses/                       Test harnesses\n├── #.notes/                           Notes\n├── #.ref/                             References\n├── #.resume/                          Resume / context summaries\n├── index.html                         Web UI (GPT-4o conversation viewer)\n└── ... (many more game modules)\n```\n\n### Naming Conventions\n\n| Prefix | Meaning |\n|--------|---------|\n| `014.wsr-pal` | WSR-PAL financial trading game |\n| `101.mutaclsym` | Mutation game (monsters, DNA, evolution) |\n| `101.lpns` | Ledger Player NPC Simple (word game) |\n| `202.snes-civ` | SNES Civ emulator port |\n| `203.gb-pokemon` | Game Boy Pokemon emulator port |\n| `204.sw-battlefront` | Star Wars Battlefront emulator |\n| `205.ttg-tactics` | Turn-based tactics |\n| `+2` / `++1` / `🧟‍♂️+18` | Version modifiers (increment count) |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Core Architecture: CHTPM Pattern\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt`\n\n### 2.1 Signal Flow (Data Thro Files)\n\n```\nUser types 'w' (keycode 119)\n  → system/keyboard_input writes \"119\" to pieces/keyboard/history.txt\n  → system/chtpm_parser_pal reads & filters → pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt\n  → pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal reads interact_relay.txt, dispatches to ops\n  → ops/game_turn_input <action> appends to data/master_ledger.txt\n  → ops/game_compose_frame reads ledger → writes pieces/display/current_frame.txt\n  → appends 'x' to pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt\n  → system/renderer watches pulse file → reads current_frame.txt → outputs to terminal\n```\n\n### 2.2 Process Architecture (Four Processes)\n\n| Process | Mode | Purpose |\n|---------|------|---------|\n| `system/keyboard_input` | Foreground (blocking) | Raw-mode stdin, writes keycodes |\n| `system/renderer` | Background | Watches pulse file, renders frames |\n| `system/chtpm_parser_pal` | Background | Parses .chtpm layout, relays keycodes |\n| `system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal` | Background | Event loop, dispatches to ops |\n\nLaunch order (critical):\n1. Initialize state files (empty)\n2. Launch renderer (so it's ready)\n3. Launch chtpm_parser_pal (auto-launches prisc+x)\n4. Launch keyboard_input (blocking foreground)\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt` lines 39–88\n\n### 2.3 CHTPM Layout Format (.chtpm)\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt` lines 90–116\n\n```\n<panel>\n  <module>system/prisc+x pal/main_loop_chtpm.pal</module>\n  <interact src=\"pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt\" />\n  <text label=\"[W] Say Word\" />\n  <button label=\"[E] End Turn\" onClick=\"ACTIVATE_END\" />\n  <text label=\"${game_map}\" />\n</panel>\n```\n\nElements:\n- `<module>PATH</module>` — prisc+x binary + .pal event loop\n- `<interact src=\"PATH\" />` — file to relay keycodes to\n- `<text label=\"...\" />` — static or `${placeholder}` output\n- `<button label=\"...\" onClick=\"ACTION\">` — menu item\n\n### 2.4 Keycode Dispatch (.pal file)\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt` lines 118–150\n\n```\nread_history pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt x2, x1\nli x3, 119        # 'w'\nbeq x2, x3, do_word\nli x3, 101        # 'e'\nbeq x2, x3, do_end\ndo_word:\n  game_turn_input 1\ndo_end:\n  game_turn_input 3\n```\n\n| Key | Keycode | Action |\n|-----|---------|--------|\n| `w` | 119 | say word |\n| `m` | 109 | move |\n| `e` | 101 | end turn |\n| `q` | 113 | quit (handled by keyboard_input) |\n| `1`-`9` | 49–57 | menu selection |\n| Arrow Up | 1002 | — |\n| Arrow Down | 1003 | — |\n| Arrow Left | 1000 | — |\n| Arrow Right | 1001 | — |\n\n---\n\n## 3. State Files (Source of Truth)\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt` lines 152–209\n\n| File | Format | Written By | Read By | Purpose |\n|------|--------|------------|---------|---------|\n| `pieces/keyboard/history.txt` | `KEY_PRESSED: <keycode>` | keyboard_input | chtpm_parser_pal | Raw keystroke log |\n| `pieces/apps/player_app/interact_relay.txt` | `<keycode>` | chtpm_parser_pal | main_loop_chtpm.pal | Filtered keycodes |\n| `pieces/apps/player_app/state.txt` | `KEY=VALUE` | button.sh, chtpm_parser | prisc+x ops | Session state |\n| `pieces/apps/player_app/history.txt` | `<keycode>` | keyboard_input | — | Backup key log |\n| `pieces/display/current_frame.txt` | multiline text | game_compose_frame | renderer | Rendered game state |\n| `pieces/display/renderer_pulse.txt` | marker file (size grows) | game_compose_frame | renderer | Frame-ready signal |\n| `pieces/display/wsr_screen_changed.txt` | marker file | wsr_menu_input | main_loop | Navigation signal |\n| `pieces/system/quit_flag.txt` | non-empty | keyboard_input | renderer | Global quit signal |\n| `data/master_ledger.txt` | `timestamp\\|epoch\\|player\\|turn\\|action_data\\|action_type` | game_turn_input | game_compose_frame | Authoritative game state |\n\n---\n\n## 4. OPS Architecture (Self-Contained Binaries)\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt` lines 211–275\n\nEach op is a standalone C program called by prisc+x:\n\n| Op | Input | Output | Purpose |\n|----|-------|--------|---------|\n| `game_compose_frame` | config.txt, master_ledger.txt | current_frame.txt (+ pulse) | Render map game state |\n| `game_turn_input <action>` | action code (1=word, 2=move, 3=end) | Appends to master_ledger.txt | Process player action |\n| `word_compose_frame` | config.txt, master_ledger.txt | current_frame.txt (+ pulse) | Render word game state |\n| `word_turn_input <action>` | action code (1=word, 2=end) | Appends to master_ledger.txt | Process word game action |\n| `game_hit_frame` | none | Signals frame ready | Placeholder |\n\nPattern: **Ledger-driven state** — game state is reconstructed from the append-only\nledger (`data/master_ledger.txt`), not stored in memory or mutable state files.\n\n---\n\n## 5. button.sh Initialization\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt` lines 245–281\n\nLauncher script must:\n1. Compile all ops (`gcc`)\n2. Create directory structure: `data/`, `pieces/display/`, `pieces/system/`,\n   `pieces/apps/player_app/`, `pieces/keyboard/`, `pieces/chtpm/layouts/`\n3. Initialize marker files to empty\n4. Initialize `data/master_ledger.txt` with header row\n5. Export env vars: `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT`, `PRISC_PROJECT_ID`\n6. Launch processes in order (renderer → chtpm_parser → keyboard_input)\n7. Set trap to kill PIDs on EXIT/INT/TERM\n\n---\n\n## 6. Registry + Dispatcher + Strategy Pattern\n\nFile: `404.architecture-bible/AI-ARCHITECTURE.txt`\n\nThis pattern appears across the codebase for pluggable, data-driven behavior:\n\n### 6.1 General Pattern\n\n```\nREGISTRY (what behaviors exist)\n  → SWITCH OP (look up id, update state.txt)\n  → DISPATCHER (read mode, look up op_name, shell to it)\n    → STRATEGY OPS (independent binaries that compute results)\n```\n\n### 6.2 Concrete Examples\n\n| Domain | Registry | Switch Op | Dispatcher | Strategy Ops |\n|--------|----------|-----------|------------|--------------|\n| AI strategies | `pieces/registry/ai/ai_strategies.txt` | `switch_ai_mode.c` | `pick_ai_strategy.c` | `decide_chase.c`, `decide_weighted_flee.c`, `decide_wander.c` |\n| LLM providers | `model_list.txt` or `provider_list.txt` | `switch_model.c` | `send_message.c` | provider-specific send code |\n| Terrain types | `terrain_types.txt` | — | `glyph_walkable()` | `raymarch_walls_3d()` |\n\n### 6.3 AI Strategy Architecture Detail\n\nFile: `404.architecture-bible/AI-ARCHITECTURE.txt` lines 1–322\n\n**Registry** (`pieces/registry/ai/ai_strategies.txt`):\n```\nid|name|op_name|description\n0|preset_chase|decide_chase|Always step directly toward hero\n1|weighted_flee|decide_weighted_flee|Chase normally but flee if health drops\n2|wander_with_detection|decide_wander|Random walk until hero detected, then chase\n```\n\n**Switch op** (`ops/switch_ai_mode.c`):\n- Usage: `switch_ai_mode.+x <monster_state_path> <strategy_id>`\n- Looks up strategy_id in registry, updates `ai_mode` field in state.txt\n\n**Dispatcher** (`ops/pick_ai_strategy.c`):\n- Reads `ai_mode` from monster `state.txt`\n- Looks up `op_name` in registry\n- Shells to `./ops/+x/<op_name>.+x` with args\n- Validates result, writes `action.txt`\n\n**Strategy ops** output format: `\"target_x target_y action_type reason\"`\n\nAdding a new strategy:\n1. Write `ops/decide_myname.c`\n2. Compile: `gcc -o ops/+x/decide_myname.+x ops/decide_myname.c -lm`\n3. Add row to `ai_strategies.txt`\n\n---\n\n## 7. Reference Implementations\n\nFile: `#.DOX/CHTPM_ARCHITECTURE_GUIDE.txt` lines 339–346\n\n| Project | What it Demonstrates |\n|---------|---------------------|\n| `014.wsr-pal` | Financial trading game (production reference) |\n| `101.mutaclsym` | Complex mutation game with monster AI |\n| `101.lpns` | Word game (ledger-player-npc-simple) |\n| `101.llpns+map+4` | LPNS with map variant |\n| `202.snes-civ` | SNES Civ emulator |\n| `203.gb-pokemon` | Game Boy Pokemon emulator |\n\n---\n\n## 8. File Naming Conventions\n\n| Suffix / Symbol | Meaning |\n|----------------|---------|\n| `.+x` | Executable op binary (e.g., `decide_chase.+x`) |\n| `.+1` / `+2` | Version modifier in directory names |\n| `#` prefix | System/utility directories (`#`.DOX, `#.notes, `#`.ref) |\n| `@` prefix | App directories (`@.apps, `@.app-store) |\n| `+.TSOTS-ALPHA-OMEGA` | The story / narrative content |\n| `404.architecture-bible` | Architecture reference docs |\n| `00.00` / `+01.00` / `+18.01` | Version numbers in directory names |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Key Directories\n\n| Directory | Contents |\n|-----------|----------|\n| `#.DOX/` | Architecture guides, how-to docs |\n| `404.architecture-bible/` | Deep architecture reference (AI, systems) |\n| `%.harnesses/` | Test harnesses and automation |\n| `@.apps/` | Application widgets and strategy docs |\n| `pieces/` | Game state files (within each game module) |\n| `data/` | Ledger files, persistent state |\n| `ops/` | Self-contained C op programs |\n| `system/` | System processes (renderer, keyboard_input, chtpm_parser) |\n| `pal/` | .pal event loop files |\n| `exchange/` | Shared data between apps (drag-drop, pet import) |\n\n---\n\n## 10. Testing Conventions\n\nFile: `404.architecture-bible/AI-ARCHITECTURE.txt` lines 255–289\n\n**File-injection testing** is the preferred approach:\n- Write to config/state files\n- Call ops directly\n- Verify output files changed\n\nExample test flow for AI switching:\n```bash\n# 1. Check initial state\ngrep ai_mode pieces/.../zombie_02/state.txt\n# 2. Run switch\n./ops/+x/switch_ai_mode.+x pieces/.../zombie_02/state.txt 1\n# 3. Verify change\ngrep ai_mode pieces/.../zombie_02/state.txt\n```\n\n**HONEST LIMIT**: file-injection tests verify DATA correctness but not RENDERING\n(terminal display, X11 windows). For visual verification, run a real game session.\n\nSee also: `#.DOX/drag-drop-how2.md` for X11 drag-drop testing with xdotool.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Environment Variables\n\nExported by `button.sh`:\n- `PRISC_PROJECT_ROOT` — absolute path to project root\n- `PRISC_PROJECT_ID` — project name identifier\n\n---\n\n*This is an anchored summary. Update when you change core patterns.*\n",
